# Improvement Backlog

Shared work queue for the scheduled improvement agents. Pick ONE item per run, check it off, and log it. Add new findings as you audit. Keep this file honest — it is the coordination point between runs.

**Mission: investor leads and conversions.** See CLAUDE.md. Every run should make the site better at (in order): capturing/converting leads, showing correct investor data and calculations, sending great weekly emails/alerts, looking clean and trustworthy, and delivering real value to investors.

## How to pick

Priority order: (1) anything broken or misleading — especially wrong numbers, (2) lead capture & conversion friction, (3) email/alert quality, (4) data & investor value, (5) design polish and SEO. Skip items marked in-progress by a run from the last 3 hours.

Also added the same format check to the /pre-construction VIP form and the /sell valuation form (both already guarded res.ok, so not leaks — but a typo now gives an accurate "enter a valid email" message instead of a generic error, lifting completion). - [x] **Phone screening caught up with the phone requirement (2026-08-03).** Hamza made a phone number MANDATORY today on the signup gate, on `/signup`, and after Google Sign-In. Two defects surfaced immediately behind that change, both in `/api/lead`: (1) the junk-number guard was gated on `source === 'registration'`, so the two paths that now FORCE a number out of a reluctant visitor — the gate (`Sign Up` / `View Limit`) and Google (`google-signin`) — were exactly the two nothing checked; `5555555555` stored clean. (2) the WhatsApp button in every lead notification built its href with `/\\D/g`, which in a template literal is an escaped backslash and matches a literal `\D`, NOT non-digits — proved by execution: the link read `https://wa.me/(647) 361-1234`, parens and a space in a URL, so that button has never worked for a formatted number (i.e. every number the site collects, since `formatPhone` formats them all). Fixed both, and screening now runs on EVERY source. Deliberately DROPS a bad number and stores the lead without it rather than returning 400: three forms send an optional phone (quiz, pre-construction VIP, sell valuation) and a 400 there would have thrown away a complete lead over a fat-fingered bonus field — the email IS the lead. The three mandatory surfaces reject fakes CLIENT-side first, so a visitor there is told to fix it instead of silently losing the field. Shared `lib/phone.js` now owns `isValidPhone` / `isFakePhone` / `phoneDigits` / `formatPhone` (40 tests, incl. that `647-361-5551` is NOT a fake — 555 must match only the area code or exchange, never the line number, or a real lead gets blocked on a mandatory field). VERIFIED against the real route on a production build: junk `647` (quiz) → 200 lead kept + `Lead phone rejected` logged; `(555) 361-1234` (precon) → 200 kept, number dropped; `1111111111` (seller-valuation) → 200 kept, dropped; `5555555555` (google-signin) → 200 kept, dropped; real `(647) 361-1234` on both `registration` and `Sign Up` → 200 with the number intact; no-phone email-only skip → 200; invalid email → still 400. Build green with no env vars

- [x] **Followed parameterized internal links had crept back — swept and closed with a shared rule (2026-08-03).** The 2026-07-30 Seobility audit scored internal links at 25% and its fix had two halves: repoint a link at the STATIC equivalent where one exists (`/gta?city=` → `/gta/[city]`, homepage `?hood=` → `/neighbourhoods/[hood]`), and mark genuine filters `rel="nofollow"`. The second half was applied BY HAND, so it drifted. Swept all 83 sitemap URLs in BUILT HTML and found **7 followed links to filtered views**: `/cash-flow-positive-properties-ontario` → `/listings?cf=1&sort=cashflow` ×3, `/market-pulse` → `/listings?sort=dom` ×2 (one in the client component, one in the server layout copy), `/hurontario-lrt-real-estate` → `/listings?lrt=1`, and `/pre-construction/projects` → `/pre-construction?project=…` ×3. The 24 hood links from `/neighbourhoods` were correctly nofollowed, confirming the original fix held where it was applied. New `lib/seo/internal-link.js` states the rule once (`relForInternalHref`) and `StickyMobileCTA` now DERIVES rel from its href rather than trusting callers — two of its callers were among the offenders, so a future caller with a query string cannot reintroduce this. 18 tests, including that real pages, fragments (`/faq#deal-score`), `tel:`/`mailto:` and protocol-relative `//host/?x` are all left FOLLOWED — nofollowing a real page would be worse than the bug. VERIFIED in built HTML on a running production server: 80 parameterized internal anchors sitewide, **0 still followed** (was 7), and the four affected pages return 200 and render with no overflow at 375px with the rel attribute live in the DOM. Also re-confirmed no regression in the rest of the July 30 set: X-Powered-By absent, apple-touch-icon present, and across all 83 pages 83/83 carry a twitter card, an og:image, a canonical and exactly one h1

- [ ] **Ambiguous + overlong internal anchor text (found 2026-08-03, NOT fixed — needs a design call).** Two link-quality classes the same sweep surfaced, both real Seobility criteria, neither fixable without touching visual design: (1) the anchor text "investment guide" points at 24 DIFFERENT `/neighbourhoods/[hood]` pages from the index, and "view listings" at 24 different filtered URLs — identical text, different destinations, which tells a crawler nothing about any of them; the fix is "Applewood investment guide" etc., which lengthens the button label and may not fit the card at 375px. (2) 10+ anchors exceed 100 characters because a whole card is wrapped in one `<a>` — the homepage neighbourhood cards run 160–184 chars ("🔥 hot Clarkson Mississauga 5.1 % Rent Yield $1.00M Avg Price +8.2% YoY 38d Avg DOM…") and the related-guide cards on `/rent-vs-buy-mississauga` and `/hurontario-lrt-real-estate` run 103–132. Fixing properly means wrapping only the heading in the anchor and making the rest of the card non-link, which changes the tap target on mobile — a design decision, not an SEO one. Left for a design run or Hamza

- [x] **Retention engine restored: newsletter self-healing + alerts now serve the whole lead database (2026-08-03, Hamza: "fix all this").** Diagnosis from production logs + Gmail, not guesses: (1) the Monday 14:00 UTC newsletter cron did NOT fire on Jul 20 or Jul 27 — zero invocations of /api/newsletter/weekly in the route-grouped logs for those windows, last delivered issue Jul 13, cause invisible from the repo (config unchanged since April; the daily crons fire fine). (2) The daily alert reached exactly ONE person (Hamza's own test account) because it read only `saved_searches` — 483 leads had no alert audience at all. (3) NO campaign has reached the contact list since the Jul 23 announcement: `in:anywhere` Gmail search (inbox+trash+spam) shows zero campaign emails in 4 days, and Hamza is himself in the audience — 8 [APPROVE] drafts went out, zero mass-sends completed. Fixes shipped: **(a)** a second, DAILY cron (15:30 UTC) on /api/newsletter/weekly with a `newsletter-draft-<wk>` dedupe row in broadcast_sends — first successful draft each week records the key, later invocations skip, so a missed Monday self-heals within a day and a working Monday makes the retry a no-op; probe-then-record (not lock-first) because this path emails one person and burning the week's key on a failed draft would recreate the missed-week bug. **(b)** /api/alerts/send now has a DEFAULT AUDIENCE: every lead (via getBroadcastRecipients, so unsubscribes are honoured) minus saved-search owners, governed by lib/alerts/default-audience.js — deal score >= 8 only, max 6 listings/email, one email per recipient per 3 days (the dedupe rows double as the send diary), 120 sends/run (rest roll over), NOTHING on a day with no qualifying fresh listing, and FAIL-CLOSED when alert_sent_listings is unreachable (for bare leads an unguarded daily blast is the disaster case; saved-search owners keep their fail-open since their own filters bound what they get). Honest copy: the default edition never claims a saved search exists — it says what it is (8+ scored new listings, "never more often than every 3 days") and unsubscribes the EMAIL via the signed token. 23 unit tests on the rules. **NOT verified end-to-end from the sandbox** (no Supabase/Resend/CRON_SECRET) — the 13:00/15:30 UTC production runs are the verification, and a scheduled check reviews both today

- [x] **"The email is lying" — /listings showed 0 multi-unit while the email said 11 (2026-08-03, Hamza reported).** He filtered Duplex/Multi, saw "No properties match your filters right now" over "Showing 0 of 2,435", and reasonably concluded the multi-unit email had fabricated its numbers. **The email was right.** Production `/api/listings?multiUnit=1` returns `total: 11` — 6 duplexes (3047 Grenville, 65 Mineola, 23 Broadview, 1106 Highgate, 1199&1201 Lakeshore, 1168 Stavebank), 3 triplexes (69 High, 24 Oakwood, 378 Lakeshore), 2 multiplexes (881 Lakeshore, 321 Lakeshore). Three real defects behind the contradiction: **(1) the empty state lied during load.** `isLoading` goes false as soon as SSR's 30 rows exist, while ~24 more pages stream in behind it; `ListingGrid`/`ListingTable` consulted only `isLoading`, never the `analysisComplete` signal the Deal Screener already uses, so a filter matching nothing in the partial pool rendered a confident dead-end. Multi-unit is the worst-hit filter on the site: the feed is `$orderby=ModificationTimestamp desc` and all 11 were last touched 12–84 days ago, so they arrive LAST — filtering in the first seconds reliably showed zero. Both components now show "Still scanning the market… N of M analyzed" until the set is settled (the screenshot's grey screener skeletons confirm the page was mid-load). **(2) the Duplex/Multi chip silently dropped Fourplex** — the hand-written test was `duplex || multi || triplex`, and `'fourplex'` contains none of those; it now reuses `MULTI_UNIT_TYPES` from the email's own module, so site and email cannot disagree about what multi-unit means. **(3) the email's CTA said "search duplex or triplex"** — search does match `type`, but it sent people to an unfiltered page to do work by hand, and "multiplex" doesn't contain "duplex" so two of the eleven were unfindable that way; it now links straight to `/listings?type=Duplex%2FMulti` (round-trips through the existing param parser, verified 200) and warns that these load last. 10 new tests assert the real 11 production rows all match, Fourplex matches, and every type the email can count is matchable by the filter. **NOT reproducible in the sandbox** — no AMPRE token here, so the streaming race itself was diagnosed from code + the live API, not replayed locally

- [x] **Audited ALL 12 strategy chips, 6 property types and 12 sorts against 99 real production listings (2026-08-03, Hamza: "check if any other filters have the same problem"). Found 2 more of the same class.** Method: pulled page 1 of the live feed, ran the real `processListings` + `applyFilters`, counted every filter. **(1) "Detached" returned Semi-Detached** — `'semi-detached'.includes('detached')` is true, so the substring test put every semi in the Detached bucket: 49 returned, of which **11 (22%) were semis**. Detached and Semi are now exact on the mapped type (a closed set from `mapType`, whose fallback IS 'Detached', so nothing is lost); Detached now returns 38, matching the raw type count exactly, and 38+11+21+29 = 99 reconciles. **(2) "LEGAL SUITE" recognised one phrasing out of seven** — the chip was a private `/legal basement/i` regex while the site's own `LEGAL_SUITE_KEYWORDS` matches "legal suite", "legal apartment", "registered suite", "registered basement", "legal secondary suite", "legal bsmt" too, so a listing the CARD badges legal was invisible to the filter meant to find it. Now reads the same `basementTier === 'legal'` the card reads. Its tooltip also claimed "or has potential", a different tier entirely (22 of the 99 rows) that this filter has never included — corrected to state the real rule, same discipline as the earlier HIGH CAP and BRRR tooltip fixes. **Checked and found HONEST, no change made:** Town/Condo overlap is real and intentional (20 rows are condo townhouses, genuinely both); `cf` returning 0 on this sample is correct (production screener reports `cfPlusCount: 6` of 2,422 — 0 expected in 99 rows); all 12 sorts preserve row count with no NaN ordering; the other 10 chips return plausible non-zero counts. Recording the clean results so a future run doesn't redo the sweep

- [x] **Campaign sends were silently losing most of the audience (2026-08-03, Hamza: "why only 140 sent it have 484 in my database").** The admin panel showed 138 delivered of 140 sent against a 484-contact list. Root cause is three defects that compounded, all four broadcast routes identical: **(1) no pacing** — `for (i += 10) { await Promise.allSettled(batch) }` fires TEN concurrent Resend calls with zero delay between batches, far above Resend's per-second limit, so most of a run is refused with 429; **(2) failures were invisible** — `sendEmail` ended `return res.ok`, discarding status and body, so a 429 storm and a real outage looked identical: a number that was merely lower than expected, with nothing in the logs and a "🎉 Sent to N" page that read like success; **(3) no time budget** — and `/api/broadcast/announcement`, the ONE campaign that actually reached inboxes (23 Jul), capped at `maxDuration = 60`, which cannot physically fit ~480 sequential sends: the function was killed mid-flight leaving no record of how far it got. 140 in 60s ≈ 2.3/s is a close quantitative fit for a rate-limited run hitting the wall, though runtime-log retention (1 day) has long since expired so that specific run cannot be re-read. New `lib/emails/send-bulk.js` — paced, 429-aware (honours `Retry-After`, halves the rate for the remainder of the run, retries only 429s so a 422 bad address doesn't burn the budget), records every failure reason with a sample body, and stops at 80% of `maxDuration` returning the EXACT untouched remainder so nothing is double-sent and the rest can be re-approved. Announcement raised 60s → 300s. Starting rate 8/s and env-overridable via `RESEND_RATE_PER_SEC` — deliberately not hardcoded to Resend's documented default because their docs 403 automated fetches and the ceiling varies by plan; the 429 backoff converges on whatever the real limit is. The "Sent" page now names the failures and says to re-approve for the remainder. 28 tests with injected clock (pacing, Retry-After parsing incl. HTTP-date form, retry-then-succeed, permanent-429 giving up, non-429 not retried, thrown errors recorded, budget remainder exact with no double-send). **NOT verified against Resend** — no API key or reachable Resend from the sandbox; the logic is tested against a fake transport only. **Still ambiguous and worth one check by Hamza:** the panel's "sent" is a count of `email.sent` WEBHOOK events, not of dispatch attempts, so a partial count could also mean events were missed; Resend's own dashboard shows the true per-campaign send count and settles it

- [x] **Condo townhouses split into their own type; ONE hardcoded property-type scheme site-wide (2026-08-03, Hamza: "condo townhomes have maintenance fees like condos… fix this once and hard code it").** He is right that lumping them with freehold townhouses misrepresents both — the maintenance fee is the single biggest carrying-cost difference between them. Root cause of this whole class of bug was **four verbatim copies of `mapType`** (lib/property-types.js plus `/api/listings`, `/api/listings-gta`, `/api/listing-single`), so one rule could drift four ways. Now: one `mapType`, imported by all three routes, over a closed `PROPERTY_TYPE` set — Detached · Semi-Detached · Townhouse (FREEHOLD) · Condo Townhouse · Condo (apt/co-op) · Duplex · Triplex · Fourplex · Multiplex. Split read straight off the board's `PropertySubType` spelling, not inferred from whether a fee is present (verified on live rows: 20/20 "Condo Townhouse" carry a fee, 1/1 "Att/Row/Townhouse" carries none). Filters now match by EQUALITY against that closed set via `PROPERTY_TYPE_MATCH` — never a substring — and the new "Condo Town" chip makes the buckets **disjoint and exhaustive**, asserted as a property in the tests. `MULTI_UNIT` is exported from the same module and re-exported by the multi-unit email, so site/email/feed cannot disagree. **THE TRAP, caught before shipping:** `estimateRent`'s tier-3 fallback tested `/condo|apartment/` BEFORE `/town/`, so renaming to "Condo Townhouse" would have silently moved those listings from the town price-to-rent ratio (0.0035) to the apartment ratio (0.0038) — changing estimated rent and therefore cash flow, cap rate and Deal Score. Town is now tested first, preserving every type's existing ratio exactly. **PROVEN no financial number moved:** re-mapped all 99 live production listings through the new mapper and diffed 8 financial fields (estimatedRent, cashFlow, capRate, cashOnCash, hamzaScore, monthlyExpenses, estimatedValue, noi) against the old output — **0 values moved**, while the type spread split correctly 21 Townhouse → 20 Condo Townhouse + 1 Townhouse. Also confirmed the condo-fee estimator was never affected: it reads `subType`, which already said "Condo Townhouse". 38 filter tests + all 7 suites (208 checks) green; 7 chips render at 375px with no overflow

- [x] **Multi-unit filter split by DOWN PAYMENT: Duplex vs Triplex+ (2026-08-03, Hamza asked whether duplex/triplex/multiplex were fixed too).** The types were already separate canonical values sharing one hardcoded definition (#103) — but they still shared ONE chip, unlike Condo Town. Split on the distinction that changes an investor's money at closing rather than on names: under CMHC's insured-mortgage rules an owner-occupied 2-unit can go 5% down on the first $500K, an owner-occupied 3-4 unit needs 10%, and 5+ units is outside residential insurance entirely (commercial financing). So **Duplex** and **Triplex+** are now separate chips; Fourplex and Multiplex ride with Triplex deliberately, because Mississauga currently has ZERO active fourplexes and two multiplexes and separate chips for those would sit permanently empty — which is its own kind of lie, and the exact "the site says none exist" problem fixed earlier the same day. **`Duplex/Multi` kept as a chip-less URL ALIAS selecting all four**: the multi-unit letter already sent to the contact list links to `?type=Duplex%2FMulti` and that email describes all 11 listings, so it must land on all 11 — a shipped email is a permanent URL contract, and dropping the key would have silently fallen those links through to an unfiltered page. The URL parser therefore validates against `PROPERTY_TYPE_MATCH` (which holds the alias) rather than the chip row. Tests updated to assert disjointness/exhaustiveness over the CHIP row specifically, that the alias is NOT a chip, and that every chip has a match rule — 42 checks. Verified live at 375px: 8 chips no overflow, `?type=Duplex`, `?type=Triplex%2B` and the legacy `?type=Duplex%2FMulti` all resolve, no JS errors

- [x] **GSC refresh 2026-08-03 (Hamza dropped a new 3-month export): fixed SERP truncation on every top-performing page.** Traffic is growing fast — clicks 12 (May) → 22 (Jun) → **135 (Jul)**, impressions 1,658 → 2,557 → **7,758** — but CTR is the bottleneck, not ranking. Measured the live `<title>` and `<meta description>` of every high-impression page against Google's render limits (~60 chars title, ~155 description): **every single one breached**. `condo-vs-townhouse` (1,915 impressions, 0.47% CTR, pos 8.4) had a 76-char title cut at "…Which Is Better for Ca" — the entire hook, cash flow and 2026, never reached the searcher; `is-erin-mills` 81/237; `families-school-quality` 71/170; `churchill-meadows` 61 (and 0 clicks from 144 impressions at position 4.9). **ROOT CAUSE, now fixed:** the auto-blog generator's own schema ASKED for "50-70 characters" titles and "Max 180" excerpts — both over the limit — so every post it writes is born truncating. Spec is now a hard MAX 60 / MAX 155 with explicit front-loading instruction and a no-figures rule. **For posts already published**, titles/excerpts live in Supabase and cannot be edited from the repo, so new `lib/blog/seo-overrides.js` maps slug → {title, description} and `generateMetadata` prefers it (on-page `<h1>` keeps the stored title; only what Google renders changes). 29 tests enforce ≤60/≤155 on every entry, reject figures in descriptions (a cap rate in a description goes stale and Google keeps serving it for months), and assert each slug matches a real measured page — a typo would silently do nothing. **NOT verified end-to-end**: the blog page needs Supabase to resolve a post and the sandbox has no credentials, so the override is unit-verified only; check a rewritten title in the SERP after the next crawl. **Recorded, NOT acted on:** `/rental-property-insurance-mississauga` sits at position **57.5** on 237 impressions despite the 2026-07-26 canonical override meant to consolidate it — the consolidation did not lift it and needs a fresh look; `/pre-construction/projects` at pos 31.9; homepage title (58) and description (157) are effectively within limits and were deliberately left alone rather than manufacturing churn

- [x] **/rental-property-insurance-mississauga "position 57.5" diagnosed — the number is mostly an ARTIFACT, and the real gap is now filled (2026-08-03, Hamza: "fix the rental property insurance page ranking").** Pulled every insurance query from the GSC export before touching anything, and the average position is misleading: the page collects ~90 GENERIC national queries — "landlord insurance" (57.8), "home insurance for rental property" (72), "insurance on rental property" (50), "homeowners insurance landlord" (63.7) — each with 1-4 impressions at position 45-85, competing with national insurers (Intact, Square One, RATESDOTCA) it has no authority to beat. Those drag the mean down. Its LOCAL and question-shaped queries are far healthier: "rented dwelling insurance mississauga" pos **11**, "rental property insurance mississauga" pos **14.7**, "renters insurance mississauga" 29, "property insurance mississauga" 39 — and it already ranks **position 1** for two question-shaped queries ("how to insure a rental property", "what should i ask an insurance broker before buying rental..."). That is the evidence for what actually works on this page. Added 4 FAQ entries (schema + visible copy, 5 → 9), each phrased the way the searcher types it and each targeting a cluster the page already earns impressions for but never answered: **"Does home insurance cover a rental property?"** (~15 variants, the largest cluster, previously only implied); **"Can you write off home insurance on a rental property?"** (tax cluster — the existing tax answer says "landlord insurance" while searchers say "home insurance"); **"What insurance does a landlord need for a condo in Mississauga?"** (entirely uncovered, and the most locally differentiated gap given Mississauga's condo-heavy stock — covers the master-policy split, improvements and betterments, and the corporation's deductible); **"What should I ask an insurance broker before buying a rental?"** (already pos 1 — expanded to hold and widen it). No invented figures anywhere; every answer keeps the "confirm with a licensed broker / accountant" hedge. **Corrected my own mid-task error:** I first reported this page as the only landing page lacking FAQ schema — wrong, it emits it through the `FAQJsonLd` component and my grep for the literal string missed it. Verified in built HTML: exactly ONE real FAQPage script (the apparent second is the escaped RSC Flight payload — the documented measurement trap), 9 Questions, single h1, all four new questions present in VISIBLE copy so there is no invisible-markup violation, 375px clean, no JS errors. **NOT claimed:** this cannot lift the generic national terms, and no on-page change will — that gap is domain authority, i.e. the backlinks item

- [x] **Vercel "Edge Requests spike 62x" alert (2026-08-04 01:25 UTC) — diagnosed, and cut the site's self-inflicted baseline by ~35%.** Alert: baseline 111 requests/5min over 7 days, 6.8k in the spike window. **The spike itself was NOT function traffic** — runtime logs across the whole 01:00-02:00 hour show only ~500 serverless invocations (top paths: /api/listings-gta 155, /api/listings 101, /api/listing-single 71), nowhere near 6.8k. Edge Requests count every static asset and every CACHED response, which never invoke a function and therefore never appear in runtime logs, so a 5-minute burst of 6.8k with normal function volume is the signature of a crawler or scraper sweeping the 5,277-URL sitemap and pulling each page's full asset set — not a code defect and not real visitors. **Needs Hamza:** the edge-level breakdown by path/IP/user-agent is only in the Vercel dashboard (Observability → Edge Requests, and Firewall for rate-limiting or bot challenge); nothing in this repo can see or block it. **What the investigation DID surface and fix:** the site was calling itself ~14,700 times a day on crons — roughly **46% of its entire ~32,000/day edge-request baseline** — to refresh numbers almost nobody was watching change. `/api/gta-screener` alone ran 48x/day and each run walks the 24k-row GTA feed in ~233 paged sub-requests = ~11,200 requests/day, for per-city market AGGREGATES that barely move hour to hour; `/api/warm/photos` ran 48x/day pre-warming the top-24 listings' photos at 2 widths (~49 requests/run) against real human traffic of a few dozen sessions a day. Cut to `13 */3 * * *` (8/day) and `7 */2 * * *` (12/day): internal requests 14,736 -> 3,652/day, **saving 11,084/day ≈ 35% of the whole baseline**. Frequency only — no logic, no calculation and no published figure touched, so nothing can move as a result. Left `/api/market-stats` at */30 deliberately: at ~1,200/day it is the cheapest of the three and it backs the first-paint summary on /listings. **Honest framing: this lowers the BASELINE and the bill; it does not explain or prevent the crawler burst the alert actually fired on.**

The capture paths exist; this sprint squeezes conversion rate out of them. Priority order below = do the topmost unchecked one per run. Design/improvement agents: keep changes honest (no fabricated counts/urgency), verify at 375px, and preserve every existing capture path. Measure-first items feed the rest.
