## 2 — Data & calculations (must be PERFECT)

- [x] **EIGHT OF THE 28 `/gta/[city]` PAGES SERVED ZERO LISTINGS — the board does not use those city names. Fixed 2026-08-01 (improvement agent), on Hamza's report "majority of the areas show 0 listings".** `/gta/etobicoke` rendered "Showing 0 investment properties / No deals match your current filters" on an UNFILTERED page, and `/api/listings-gta?city=Etobicoke` returned `{"listings":[],"total":0}` with HTTP 200 and healthy field probing. The API was fine; `City eq 'Etobicoke'` matches nothing, because TREB labels those rows `Toronto W08`, not `Etobicoke`. **Measured, all 28 CITY_COPY pages, live production, 2026-08-01 04:00 UTC (`total` from the feed's own `$count`):**

  | 0 rows BEFORE | | Already working (controls) | | | |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | Etobicoke **0** | Stoney Creek **0** | Toronto 9,695 | Vaughan 1,306 | Whitby 420 | Halton Hills 275 |
  | North York **0** | Dundas **0** | Brampton 2,047 | Richmond Hill 1,020 | Oshawa 667 | Georgetown 275 |
  | Scarborough **0** | Ancaster **0** | Hamilton 2,169 | Markham 1,184 | Clarington 437 | King 276 |
  | East York **0** | | Oakville 1,118 | Aurora 317 | Pickering 437 | Caledon 525 |
  | York **0** | | Burlington 773 | Newmarket 340 | Ajax 297 | Milton 539 |

  **What the board actually serves**, measured the same day (not assumed): Toronto rows carry a TREB sub-area code in `City` (`City eq 'Toronto C01'` alone = 1,347) and a COMMUNITY in `CityRegion` — never the district name. `CityRegion eq 'Etobicoke'`, `'North York'` and `'Scarborough'` each matched **0 rows in the entire feed**, against positive controls `CityRegion eq 'Mimico'` (9,146) and `'Islington-City Centre West'` (8,198), so a negative here is a measurement and not a broken probe. The districts cannot be isolated by name anywhere. They CAN be isolated by postal code: every one of the 9,695 active Toronto rows has one (`PostalCode eq null` = 0), and Canada Post assigns each Toronto FSA its pre-amalgamation municipality as the place name. Measured with those FSA sets: **Etobicoke 1,431 · North York 2,613 · Scarborough 1,697 · East York 321 · York 519** — mutually exclusive by construction, 6,581 of the 6,583 rows in their union (the 2-row gap is M8/M9 codes that are not real FSAs, and they land on no district page rather than a wrong one). Hamilton needs no geography: its rows carry the community in `CityRegion` — **Stoney Creek 231 · Ancaster 137 · Dundas 58** — with `startswith`, not `eq`, because Stoney Creek splits across `Stoney Creek` (132) and `Stoney Creek Mountain` (94). Postal codes were tested for Hamilton too and **rejected**: L9H holds 174 rows the board does not call Dundas against the 58 it does, and L9G/L9K holds 77 that are not Ancaster — Toronto's FSAs are former municipalities, Hamilton's are rural delivery areas.

  **NO NUMBER MOVED.** The rows keep their PARENT city (`Toronto`/`Hamilton`) — the legally correct municipality, and the one whose tax rate (0.63% / 1.26%) and rent tier (1.15 / 0.85) already applied to them. Relabelling them would have repeated the b6a778d bug exactly: none of the eight names is in `CITY_RENT_TIER`, so every listing would have dropped to the 0.90 unknown-city tier. Proven, not asserted: `processListings` run over 352 listings (22 cities × 4 subtypes × 4 bed counts) against the pre-change module is **byte-identical on 352/352** apart from one new `community` passthrough. One `CITY_SUBAREAS` in `lib/constants.js` is the single source (it is deliberately NOT a `CITY_ALIAS_GROUPS` entry — a district is a place inside a city, not a second name for one), `listingInSubarea` applies the identical predicate in memory, so saved-search alerts saved on those eight pages select rows instead of silently going dead, and the GTA screener can now publish their aggregates too (parent Toronto/Hamilton groups are NOT carved up — the district rows stay in them as well). Also fixed on the way past: `?city=` was interpolated raw into the OData `$filter`, so a crafted value rewrote the query and could read rows outside the site's own filters — every value now goes through an OData escape. 62 fixture assertions against the real route/constants/alerts/screener modules (only `next/server` and `fetchWithFieldTiers` stubbed), including all 20 other cities and the hub feed byte-identical. Build green with no env vars. **NOT verifiable until deploy:** that the eight pages RENDER listings — the filter strings the code now builds were each run against production by hand (counts above), but the pages themselves must be re-checked after release.

- [x] **THE DEAL SCREENER PUBLISHED FALSE HEADLINE NUMBERS FOR THE FIRST ~10-20 SECONDS OF EVERY VISIT — fixed 2026-07-31 (improvement agent), on Hamza's report "listings take too long to load, investors barely have any attention span".** Two real mobile screenshots, same page load, seconds apart: at *"30 of 2,493 analyzed"* the dashboard read **CF+ DEALS 0 · BEST CAP 4.7% · BEST CASH FLOW -$278/mo · PRICE DROPS 3 · AVG SCORE 3.2**; at *"524 of 2,493 analyzed"* the same six tiles read **1 · 5.6% · +$41/mo · 107 · 3.5**. Every one of those first figures is a max or a count over whichever ~1% of the market had streamed in, rendered in the same confident dashboard type as a settled value — so an investor landing mid-load was told, on the page that exists to sell them, that this market has **zero cash-flowing deals and a best property that loses $278/month**. Both false; both discouraging; and they only self-corrected if the visitor stayed. Per CLAUDE.md a wrong number is the worst bug on this site. THREE fixes: (1) new `lib/listings/screener-metrics.js` holds ONE aggregate implementation plus `resolveScreenerMetrics`, which withholds every set-dependent figure (cfPlusCount, bestCap, bestCf, priceDropCount, avgScore, suites, avgCf, avgCoc) until the set is settled — a withheld value renders a fixed-height skeleton + "analyzing…", never a number, and the "N of M analyzed" progress chip stays visible. Sample-honest averages (price, rent, DOM) keep updating. (2) The completion signal is now EXPLICIT (`analysisComplete` from the container). The old `listings.length < marketTotal` test could never distinguish "still loading" from "filtered to 40 results" — it was false forever the moment any filter was applied. (3) `/api/market-stats` already walks the WHOLE active feed and caches for a day, so it now also computes the same screener aggregate server-side (withheld below a 95% fill) and `/listings` — which already fetches that endpoint during SSR — hands it to the dashboard as `initialSummary`. Result: on the unfiltered default view the six tiles show the CORRECT whole-market figures in the first paint instead of ~25 client round trips later, at the cost of zero extra requests. FIXTURE-VERIFIED (20 assertions, real component through react-dom/server): the old component on a 30-of-2,493 fixture reproduces the screenshot exactly (0 / 4.7% / -$278/mo / 3 / 3.2); the new one renders skeletons for all five; with the summary it renders the true market values; and on a COMPLETE set the new markup is **byte-identical to the pre-change component** (8,299 chars, char-for-char) with an aggregate identical to the old inline formula. 375px screenshots captured for all three states. GTA pages pass no summary, so they get skeletons until their load finishes — honest, but see the open item below.
- [x] **GTA hub/city pages had no whole-market summary, so their Deal Screener sat on skeletons for the whole background load — BUILT 2026-07-31 (improvement agent), on Hamza's approval of the cost.** The objection to the obvious fix was "27 cities × a feed walk each". This does ONE walk: `/api/gta-screener` (new) pages the GTA feed once, groups the rows by city, and runs the SAME `computeScreenerMetrics` (`lib/listings/screener-metrics.js` — one implementation, never a second copy) per group plus once over everything, yielding every covered city's aggregate and a GTA-wide aggregate from a single walk and a single underwriting pass. `/gta` gets the GTA-wide figure, `/gta/[city]` gets its own, both as `initialSummary`, handed over only when no filter is active — identical to the `/listings` behaviour that shipped in e65bb13. **Two things make the walk affordable, neither of them a guess about upstream speed:** (1) new `nomedia=1` on `/api/listings-gta` drops the Media `$expand` — ~5 CDN size-variants per photo, ~18k media rows on a 100-row page, and an aggregate reads none of them; it selects the SAME fields (fixture-verified byte-identical `$select`) using the no-media tiers the cascade already falls through to, so only `photos`/`images` change and no published figure moves. (2) the walk's pages are fetched with a 2h `revalidate` and it carries a 45s wall-clock budget inside a 60s function, so a run that runs out of time leaves its prefix in the Data Cache and the next cron run starts further along — **the walk converges across runs instead of having to fit in one invocation**, and once complete the 24h response cache makes steady-state cost ~zero. Its own endpoint + cron (`13,43 * * * *`), never a page render: `/api/listings-gta` already `TimeoutError`s during `/gta/[city]` ISR regeneration and a 245-page walk hung off a page would have made that far worse. The pages read the CACHED body with a 4s abort, in parallel with their feed fetch, and every failure path returns null → skeletons → today's behaviour. **WITHHELD, not estimated:** nothing is published unless the walk covered every page with zero drops AND collected ≥95% of the feed's own browsable total (the same bar `/api/market-stats` uses). The feed is ordered by `ModificationTimestamp` — city-agnostic — so a truncated walk is a recency-biased sample of *every* city at once; there is no honest per-city number to salvage from it, and conversely a complete walk is complete for every city in it by construction. Cities the walk cannot honestly cover get null: the 10 CITY_COPY pages that aren't in the feed route's city list at all (Etobicoke, North York, Scarborough, East York, York, Stoney Creek, Dundas, Ancaster, Clarington, King) and the Georgetown/Halton Hills pair, whose page query and feed label don't provably describe the same rows (see below). FIXTURE-VERIFIED, 25 assertions through the real modules and the real components: per-city aggregates byte-identical to running the aggregate over each city's rows separately; city totals sum to the GTA total; photos-vs-no-photos aggregates identical; a dropped page, a 10%-fill walk and a page-capped walk each publish `null` for the GTA *and* every city; the real `DealScreener` on a 30-row city slice renders skeletons without a summary and the true whole-city figures with one (fixture: slice would have said CF+ 4 / best cap 6.5% / 10 drops, the city truly is CF+ 9 / 7.1% / 40); complete-state markup unchanged whether or not a summary is supplied; a filtered view still withholds; and the real `/gta` + `/gta/[city]` page functions hand the right aggregate (GTA-wide vs that city's vs null for Etobicoke) to `ListingsContainer` even with the listings feed returning 503. 375px checked on `/gta` and `/gta/hamilton` (no horizontal overflow; no new UI — it is the already-shipped `market` tile state). **NOT MEASURED, and it is the crux: this sandbox cannot reach the AMPRE feed or production, so the real walk duration, the real page count and the real per-request cost are unknown.** The design fails safe — a walk that cannot finish publishes nothing and shows skeletons, i.e. exactly today's behaviour — but whether it converges at all has to be read off production. See the two follow-ups below.
- [ ] **Read `/api/gta-screener` on production once it has run a few times and confirm the walk converges** (opened 2026-07-31). The response carries its own diagnostics — `complete`, `fillRatio`, `rows`, `feedTotal`, `pagesWalked`, `pagesTotal`, `pagesDropped`, `walkedAll` — so one `curl` answers it. If `walkedAll` stays false across several cron runs, the levers in order of preference are: raise `maxDuration` on that route (60 → 300 is a one-line change and this account clearly runs more than the Hobby cron limit), lengthen the walk's `revalidate` so more of the prefix survives between runs, or allow `$top` above 100 **only** when `nomedia=1` (the 100 cap exists because AMPRE rejects the Media expand at 200 — without media that constraint may not apply, which would cut ~245 requests to ~25, but it is unverified and must be measured, not assumed). Do not lower the 95% fill bar to make it "work".
- [x] **WRONG NUMBER — Georgetown / Halton Hills listings were rented, and therefore scored, one tier too low. Fixed 2026-07-31 (improvement agent), on Hamza's report.** One line in `/api/listings-gta` relabelled the board's `Halton Hills` as `Georgetown` in the response mapper, and it ran BEFORE `estimateRent(price, beds, city, type)`. `CITY_RENT_TIER` had `'Halton Hills': 0.95` and no `Georgetown` key, so every one of those listings silently took the `|| 0.90` unknown-city default — the base-rent term understated by 5.3%. **Worked example (fixture-verified through the real route + the real `processListings`): a 3-bed detached at $999,000 in Halton Hills — rent $3,000 → $3,100/mo, cash flow −$3,514 → −$3,419/mo, cap rate 1.30% → 1.42%, cash-on-cash −19.23% → −18.71%, Deal Score 1.8 → 1.8.** The correction makes those listings look BETTER, which is the direction the model always intended; property tax does not move at all (`getTaxRate` already carried both names at 0.85%, confirmed). Same line also meant `/gta/halton-hills` displayed every listing as "Georgetown" (collapsing Acton and Glen Williams under it) while `/gta/georgetown` asked the board for `City eq 'Georgetown'` — a name TREB may not use, i.e. a possibly-empty page. **THE LABEL TREB ACTUALLY SERVES IS STILL UNKNOWN — this sandbox cannot reach query.ampre.ca — so the fix is deliberately correct under either:** the relabel is gone (rows keep the board's own city), the request asks for BOTH names whenever either is requested (`(City eq 'Halton Hills' or City eq 'Georgetown')`, Toronto's `startswith` branch untouched), both names are in the hub's `GTA_CITIES`, and `'Georgetown': 0.95` is now a key in BOTH rent-tier tables (feed route and `lib/constants.js`) so neither label can fall to 0.90 again. One `CITY_ALIAS_GROUPS` in `lib/constants.js` is the single source: `cityAliases()`/`canonicalCity()`. Saved-search alerts follow it too (`poolForSearch` matched `l.city === filters.city` exactly, so a search saved on /gta/georgetown would have started matching nothing). 36 fixture assertions against the REAL modules, including a before/after over a 20-city × 4-subtype fixture in which every row outside this town is byte-identical. Build green.
  - The GTA screener's exclusion of this pair was LIFTED as part of it, and only because it is now provable: both pages issue the identical request, so both load the same union of rows, so the aggregate over that union is the honest figure for each — alias rows are merged into one group and published under both keys. Verified equal to `computeScreenerMetrics` over that union, with city totals still summing to the GTA total.

- [x] **THE CARD AND THE DETAIL PAGE DISAGREED ON CASH FLOW BY ~$684/mo — one maintenance rule now, everywhere. Fixed 2026-07-30 (improvement agent).** The maintenance reserve was computed FOUR different ways for the same property. `calculateCashFlow` (detail page Mortgage tab, the neighbourhood worked example, the printable cash-flow report, break-even rent) used *the greater of 8% of rent or 1% of property value per year* — the rule added deliberately in Apr 2026 (dab3a2e) and PUBLISHED on /score-methodology ("Greater of 8% of rent or 1% of property value per year") under the heading "Every cash flow, cap rate, and score on this site is computed from the same set of assumptions… Nothing is hidden." But `processListings` — which computes **every listing card, every deal score, every cap rate, both emails and the `cf=1` cash-flow filter** — never got the floor and reserved 8% of rent only; `calculateNOI` (every cap rate on the site, cards AND detail) never got it either; and /faq documented the un-floored rule, contradicting /score-methodology. Measured on a realistic $1.2M Erin Mills detached (rent $3,700): the card reserved $296/mo, the detail page $1,000/mo — same property, **cash flow $684/mo apart**, and the detail page's OWN two tabs disagreed with each other (Mortgage tab $1,000/mo vs the NOI breakdown's $3,552/yr), both rows labelled "Maintenance (8%)" above a number that was not 8% of anything. Dragging the maintenance % slider also did nothing whenever the value floor bound — it looked broken. FIXED by making the published rule the single source of truth: new exported `maintenanceReserve({price, rent, maintenancePct, monthlyCondoFee})` in the engine returns `{monthly, annual, basis}`; `calculateCashFlow`, `calculateNOI` (new optional `price`) and `processListings` all call it. Direction of the fix was deliberate — we unified UP onto the DOCUMENTED, more conservative rule rather than quietly deleting the floor, because dropping it would have made every freehold look ~$684/mo better than the model the site publishes. Condo behaviour is untouched (a real condo fee still replaces the reserve entirely — no double-count) and `calculateNOI` without a `price` is byte-identical to before, so no caller is silently penalised. The `1` is now `DEFAULT_ASSUMPTIONS.maintenanceValueFloorPercent` instead of a magic number, and /score-methodology reads it from there (that table's own comment says "never retyped" — it was retyped). Row labels now name whichever term won ("Maintenance (8% of rent)" / "Maintenance (1% of value/yr)") on the Mortgage tab, the NOI breakdown and the printed report; /faq corrected to match /score-methodology. VERIFIED with a 21-assertion harness against the real modules (jiti): value floor / rent term / condo paths each hit, malformed input → 0 not NaN, **card cashFlow === detail cashFlow**, card capRate === detail capRate, NOI annual maintenance === 12× the cash-flow figure, breakdown parts sum to the total, legacy no-price NOI unchanged; plus a 720-listing synthetic sweep (6 types × 6 hoods × 5 prices × 4 bed counts) with **0 non-finite money/score fields**. Both corrected disclosures confirmed in the BUILT static HTML and both new labels confirmed in the shipped `/listings/[id]` client chunk. IMPACT, stated plainly: freehold cash flow, cap rate and deal score all drop (a $1.2M/$3,700 freehold: cap rate 2.13% → 1.45%); condos and any listing where 8% of rent already exceeded the floor are unchanged. **Note for Hamza:** this makes freehold numbers more conservative on purpose — it is the model the site already promises. If you'd rather publish the lighter reserve, change `maintenanceValueFloorPercent` and the two disclosure strings follow automatically. NOT touched (deliberate, and left as a finding below): the standalone /mortgage-calculator.
- [ ] **/mortgage-calculator does not apply the maintenance value floor** (found 2026-07-30 while fixing the above). Its local math reserves the slider % of rent only, so a listing modelled there can differ from the same listing's card. Left alone on purpose: it is a "run your own scenario" tool whose whole value is that its sliders say exactly what its math does, and a hidden floor would break that. Two honest options for a future run — (a) add the floor plus a visible "greater of" note under the Maintenance slider, or (b) leave it and soften the claim on /cash-flow-positive-properties-ontario that the card "matches what you would model yourself" in the calculator. (b) is smaller; (a) is more consistent. Needs a product call.

- [x] **Full-feed consumers were computing on ~70% fills — batched the fan-out + restored the email pool. Fixed 2026-07-27 (improvement agent).** Live recompute of market-stats showed activeCount 1,765 of ~2,555 even after the fill-ratio guard: fetchFeedPages fired all 25 extra pages IN PARALLEL, i.e. 25 concurrent heavy AMPRE queries — the upstream throttles the burst and several pages blow their timeout on every compute, so the fill never completes and the homepage hero (which reads activeCount) shows an undercount. Extra pages now fetch in batches of 6 with failures logged (small consumers like the SSR pages: 2 are one batch, unaffected). ALSO: `fetchAllListings` (daily alert + weekly newsletter pool) still had maxPages 15 sized for 200-row pages — since the feed clamps to 100, the retention channels were quietly matching against ~1,500 of ~2,555 listings (~59%), the exact 8%-sample bug this helper was built to kill, reintroduced by the clamp; now maxPages 30/limit 100. AND `neighbourhood-stats` (per-hood avg price/DOM/yield on all 24 guide pages + homepage cards) was the last route on the poisoned limit=200 Data Cache keys — moved onto fetchFeedPages. `seo/ping` checked and left: revalidate 0, page-1-by-design for fresh listings.

- [x] **/gta STILL empty after #62 — root cause finally measured, not guessed. Fixed 2026-07-27.** Runtime logs showed /api/listings-gta answering 200 on every call — the failure was page-side: the SSR fetch ABORTED at its timeout while the API kept running and logged a 200 nobody consumed (the GTA cache-seeding render is genuinely slower than 8s: 24.5k-row filter + $count + media expand + cold-instance probe). Compounding it, fetchFeedPages' FIRST fetch was unwrapped, so the abort THREW and the page catch swallowed it with nothing in the logs. Fixed: (1) seed budget 25s — with the fetch cached at revalidate 300 + stale-while-revalidate, exactly ONE render per 5 minutes pays it, everyone else gets cached rows instantly; (2) `export const maxDuration = 60` on the page so the platform doesn't kill the function under the fetch; (3) the first fetch now catches and console.errors with the path and error name, so the NEXT diagnosis reads a log line instead of correlating timestamps. Reviewer note for the record: the "Toronto returns 0 / no multi-city param / mlsId null" diagnosis probed /api/listings (the Mississauga route) — /gta calls /api/listings-gta, which was verified healthy live (24,555 total, Toronto districts via startswith, real dom 4/1/21/55, mlsId populated).

- [x] **/gta SSR shipped ZERO embedded listings + 13MB /listings payload cut. Fixed 2026-07-27 (Hamza's reviewer confirmed /listings fully fixed, flagged /gta + page weight).** /gta DID get the shared feed fetch in #61 — the killer was a pre-existing per-page quirk: the hub's SSR timeout was 4s (`city ? 8000 : 4000`) while the GTA hub runs the slowest query on the site (17-city OR filter + every Toronto sub-area + media expand + the field probe on cold API instances), so the fetch timed out on essentially every render and the page shipped a 278KB empty shell (verified live) while /listings shipped 99 records. Now 8s uniform. PAGE WEIGHT: /listings SSR was 13MB — ~200 full listing objects in the RSC payload (up to ~40 photo URLs + full MLS remarks each) while the server markup renders 30 cards using photos[0] and no remarks. New `slimForSSR()` in the shared helper (30 rows, first photo only, remarks dropped — every computed number kept) applied to BOTH pages; safe because the container background-refetches the full feed on mount and replaces initialListings. Also both pages fetch 1 feed page instead of 2 (the grid renders 30, the ItemList uses 20 — page 2 was pure payload). Unit-verified slim: 100 rows -> 30, payload ~7% of original, empty/no-photo rows safe.

- [x] **SSR pages served PRE-FIX feed data for hours after every fix — one shared feed fetch now. Fixed 2026-07-27 (Hamza: "The API is fixed. The page is not").** Verified live before touching anything: /api/listings returned dom 1-81 (38 distinct) while the /listings SSR payload embedded dom:0 on all 199 records with the pre-#60 hood distribution. Mechanism was NOT a separate query — all three pages call our own API — it was Next's DATA CACHE, which persists ACROSS DEPLOYMENTS on Vercel: /listings and /gta cached their fetch 600s, the homepage 3600s, and the smoking gun is that the SSR payload was a 199-row limit=200 response, a shape the API stopped producing THREE deploys earlier (the feed clamps limit to 100 since #56). Same class as the killed 24h CDN cache, one layer deeper. Fixed with `lib/listings/fetch-feed.js` — ONE fetch path used by /listings SSR, /gta SSR and the homepage top-deals (Hamza asked for exactly this so it cannot drift again): limit=100 keys (which also permanently orphans every poisoned limit=200 cache entry), revalidate 300, 8s timeout per request, per-page error tolerance. Also: `mlsId` was null on every active record (ListingId is VOW-suppressed) — now falls back to ListingKey, which IS the MLS number, in all three API routes.
- **MOTIVATED-SELLER RADAR IS NOW REAL (Hamza's read of the live data, recorded so it ships):** in a 99-listing live sample, 21 listings have price cuts, max DOM 81, and 4 have BOTH 60+ DOM and a cut — scaled to ~2,608 active listings that is roughly ~100 genuinely motivated Mississauga sellers identifiable from real data nobody else surfaces. The MOTIVATED chip (dom>=45) and BRRR filters have been live-inert until today because DOM was suppressed; they now fire on real numbers. Product/pitch angle is Hamza's to run with ("~100 Mississauga sellers sitting 60+ days who already cut their price").

- [x] **FSA guess was overriding the MLS community — 47/100 listings under the wrong hood. Fixed 2026-07-27 (improvement agent).** `process-listings` resolved hood as `FSA_TO_HOOD[postal] || CityRegion || …` — correct back when neighbourhood was always "Mississauga", but after the CityRegion unlock the one-FSA-one-hood approximation was actively OVERWRITING ground truth. Measured on the real production 100-listing payload: 47 listings changed hood once the board community wins (MLS-says-Mineola was scored as Lakeview, Cooksville as City Centre, East Credit as Mississauga Valleys), and 44 rents moved onto the correct HOOD_RENTS row — same table, right key, no new math. FSA stays as the FALLBACK, which is exactly right for non-canonical community names (e.g. "Creditview") that need routing to a nearby hood instead of the city default. Also killed the SECOND copy of the uniform-floor bug: process-listings' domFloor fallback still folded `daysSinceUpdate` (the sync stamp the feeds sort by) into the floor — harmless while real dom dominates, but the exact "3+ on every card" mechanism the moment dates go missing. Verified: 0 non-finite money fields, dom distinct count unchanged (35), floor never below dom, before/after diff reviewed listing-by-listing on real data.

- [x] **DETAIL PAGE DISAGREED WITH THE CARD on dom/CoC/cash flow — fixed 2026-07-27 (Hamza: "dom, cac and cashflow is not working properly").** `/api/listing-single` — the route behind every listing detail page — kept its own hardcoded `$select` and never got the probe, so while the CARDS showed real dom/community/fees, the DETAIL page for the same listing still showed dom "—", neighbourhood "Mississauga", sqft 0, and computed CoC/cash flow on an ESTIMATED condo fee instead of the real one — two different cash flows for one property, in front of the same visitor. Worse, the route was CDN-cached at `s-maxage=86400` (the exact 24h-cache bug already killed on the feeds but missed here), so even fixes replayed stale for a day. All four fixed: probe-driven $select (byte-identical field surface to the feeds), `computeDaysOnMarket` (real listing dates), CityRegion, LivingAreaRange, real AssociationFee with frequency normalization, cache to 900. Also fixed the listed-today edge: `diff > 0` made a listing entered TODAY collide with the 0-means-unknown sentinel — brand-new listings (the ones the New badge exists for) rendered "—" and could never earn the badge; same-day now clamps to 1 ("day one on market"). VERIFIED the whole pipeline on the REAL production 100-listing payload run through `processListings` locally: 0 non-finite money fields, dom 35 distinct/0 unknown, condo fees real on 50/50 (0 estimated), capRate 0.42–4.92%, cashFlow 99 distinct values. The card/detail mismatch is structural-fixed, not patched: both routes now read the SAME probed field list, so they cannot drift again.

- [x] **REAL DAYS-ON-MARKET IS LIVE — verified against production 2026-07-27 after the probe deploy (#57/#58).** `supportedFields` from the live API: `OriginalEntryTimestamp, ListingContractDate, CumulativeDaysOnMarket, CityRegion, LivingAreaRange, BuildingAreaTotal, AssociationFee, AssociationFeeFrequency`. **The "toxic trio" theory was WRONG — only `OnMarketDate` is unsupported.** One bad name in a joint $select poisoned the whole group and two perfectly good date fields were written off with it; the per-field probe settled it in one deploy. Measured on a live 100-listing page: **DOM has 35 distinct values (2–63), 0 unknowns** (old state: one identical value across every card); **neighbourhood has 21 distinct real communities, zero rows saying "Mississauga"** (old state: 100% "Mississauga"); **sqft non-zero on 100/100** (old: 0/100, from LivingAreaRange bands, flagged approximate); **all 36 condos on the page carry REAL AssociationFee values ($452–$1,341)** — the "(est.)" label now only fires where a fee is genuinely absent. `fieldTier: probed+media`, `firstSeenTracker: on` (Hamza ran the migration). Still token-suppressed on active listings (VOW would unlock): `ListingId` (mlsId), `YearBuilt`, `Latitude`/`Longitude` — but DOM, the one that mattered most, did NOT need the VOW token after all. **Agents: the probe is the source of truth for field availability now. Never hand-edit a $select tier again; read `supportedFields` from production instead.**

- [x] **"3+ days on market" on EVERY listing — fixed 2026-07-27 (Hamza: "wtf is 3+ days on market every listing shows that").** He is right and this one is on us. `computeDomFloor(dom, daysSinceUpdate, daysSinceFirstSeen)` took `daysSinceUpdate`, which derives from `ModificationTimestamp` — and BOTH feeds query `$orderby=ModificationTimestamp desc`, so every listing on a page shares essentially the same stamp. Page 1 was synced ~3 days ago, so all ~100 cards read "3+". **This is the original sync-stamp bug this module was written to kill, wearing a plus sign.** It passed review because it is a genuinely true lower bound; what got missed is that a true number carrying ZERO per-listing information is worse than no number — it looks like data and isn't. `daysSinceUpdate` no longer feeds the floor. Only per-listing signals count now (feed `DaysOnMarket`, first-seen tracker); everything else renders an em dash. Verified: with the tracker off and no feed DOM, the floor is 0/— on every row instead of a uniform 3; with the tracker on it produces genuinely varied per-listing values (12/31/2/63). **Lesson for future runs: before shipping a derived metric, check that it VARIES across a page. Uniformity is the tell.**
- [x] **Replaced hardcoded $select guessing with an empirical per-field probe 2026-07-27.** The "we can't have days-on-market" conclusion rested on ONE differential test that named `{OnMarketDate, ListingContractDate, OriginalEntryTimestamp}` TOGETHER and saw a 4xx. Since AMPRE fails the whole query on a single bad name, that evidence only proves AT LEAST ONE is bad — it never justified writing off all three, and DOM is the field the site's entire freshness story rests on. `probeSupportedFields()` now tests each optional field ON ITS OWN with a cheap `$top=1` query (~10 parallel requests, memoised 1h per instance), and the real `$select` is built from whatever answers 200. Also probes `CumulativeDaysOnMarket`, a standard RESO field nobody had tried. Self-healing: the hour a VOW-scoped token lands, newly-permitted fields start flowing with no code change and no deploy. The accepted set is returned as **`supportedFields`** on both feeds, so what the token can actually see is a fact readable from production in one request instead of inferred from symptoms.

- [x] **NEIGHBOURHOOD WAS HARDCODED TO THE CITY NAME — fixed 2026-07-27 (improvement agent), found by Hamza reading the live API.** Both feed routes mapped `neighbourhood: city`, and `CityRegion` (the RESO field carrying the actual community — Cooksville, Malton, Port Credit) was never in the `$select`. So every one of ~2,600 Mississauga listings reported its neighbourhood as the literal string "Mississauga". Impact was not cosmetic: `process-listings.js:50` resolves a listing's `hood` as `FSA_TO_HOOD[fsa] || (HOOD_DATA[neighbourhood] ...) || ...`, and **there is no `HOOD_RENTS['Mississauga']` row** — so any listing whose postal-code FSA missed the 22-entry `FSA_TO_HOOD` table fell straight past its real community to the engine's generic default rent. The real per-hood spread is large (3-bed: Malton $2,500 → Lorne Park $3,700, ~48%), and rent drives cash flow, cap rate and the deal score. Fixed by adding `CityRegion` to a new `$select` tier and mapping `neighbourhood: l.CityRegion || city` on both feeds. VERIFIED NO TAX REGRESSION before shipping — the concern was that a GTA listing's hood would change from "Brampton" to a community like "Bramalea" and lose Brampton's 1.13% rate; replayed the exact `hood` resolution chain and confirmed GTA falls through to `l.city` unchanged (Brampton 1.13%, Toronto 0.63% both identical before/after) while Mississauga communities correctly resolve to the Mississauga rate.
- [x] **sqft parsed from `LivingAreaRange` 2026-07-27** — `LivingArea`/`BuildingAreaTotal` are confirmed-unsupported by this feed, which is why `$/sqft` has been blank site-wide. `LivingAreaRange` is a different field: TREB serves square footage as a banded STRING ("700-799"). Added `parseLivingAreaRange()` (returns the band midpoint, 0 when unparseable) and a `sqftApproximate` flag so surfaces can label it honestly. Unit-verified 13 cases; caught a real bug in my own first version — a `> 0` filter silently dropped the zero lower bound of TREB's smallest band "0-499", turning it into 499, i.e. reporting the TOP of the band as the unit's size on exactly the smallest units where that ~2x overstatement hurts $/sqft most. DONE 2026-07-28 (improvement agent): compare page's Sq Ft and $/SqFt cells now render `~` prefix when `sqftApproximate` is true — format functions now accept `(v, l)` and the render loop passes the full listing as the second arg. One-line change per metric + one call-site change; backward-compatible (existing format fns ignore the extra arg).
- [~] **DOM: the diagnosis "the date fields were never requested" is half right, and the other half matters.** `DaysOnMarket` and `ListingId` ARE in `SELECT_CORE` and always have been — they return null on ACTIVE rows while returning real values on Closed/Leased ones, which is the IDX-vs-VOW suppression signature, not a `$select` omission. `computeDaysOnMarket()` already reads `OnMarketDate`/`ListingContractDate`/`OriginalEntryTimestamp` and has since 2026-07-27, so the mapping was never the gap either. What IS true: those three date fields are genuinely absent from the `$select` — deliberately, because differential testing proved naming any of them 4xxs the ENTIRE query, which is what previously collapsed the cascade to the minimal tier and cost the site its photos, condo fees and sqft. Re-adding them to the primary tier would reintroduce exactly that outage. Shipped instead as a **top probe tier**: it asks for all three, and on rejection the cascade falls through to precisely the tier it lands on today, costing one extra request. If the VOW token ever lands, real DOM starts flowing with no code change and no deploy. **Still Needs Hamza: `AMPRE_VOW_TOKEN` remains the only path to exact per-listing DOM.**
- [x] **`mlsId` null is not a bug in our code** — `ListingId` has always been in `SELECT_CORE`. Null on active rows, same VOW suppression as `DaysOnMarket`. No code change possible or warranted; recorded so a future run doesn't re-chase it.

- [x] Homepage "Top neighbourhoods" data was STATIC (Hamza flagged 2026-07-22): the Hot/Warm/Cool badge, avg price, YoY, DOM and yield for all 24 hoods were hardcoded in HOOD_DATA (lib/constants.js), last touched April 2026, presented as if current. Per Hamza's call ("live stats + label the rest"): avg price, avg DOM and rent yield now compute LIVE per-neighbourhood from the active listing feed (reusing the listings fetchTopDeals already does — zero extra fetch; ≥4-listing sample threshold, else curated fallback), the top-4 now rank by live yield, and a green "Live from active listings" pill marks live cards. Trend + YoY stay curated but are now LABELED as "Hamza's expert outlook (last reviewed {HOOD_OUTLOOK_AS_OF})" with a YoY* footnote. Graceful fallback to curated when the feed is down. Unit-tested the aggregation; build green; homepage 200, single h1.
- FOLLOW-UP DONE 2026-07-22: rolled the same live merge onto the /neighbourhoods index + detail pages. Extracted computeHoodStats to lib/listings/hood-stats.js (shared) + added a GET /api/neighbourhood-stats endpoint (revalidate 600) so both a client page (index) and a server page (detail) can consume it. Index fetches it client-side + merges; detail is now async ISR (revalidate 600) fetching server-side, and its worked cash-flow example + FAQ + FAQ-schema now use the live avg price/DOM/yield too. Trend/YoY/inventory/scores stay curated + labeled (* footnote, "last reviewed April 2026"), Live pills on live cards, graceful curated fallback when the feed is down. Build green (87 pages); endpoint returns valid JSON; both pages 200, single h1, FAQPage schema intact.

- [x] Mortgage calculator math audited 2026-07-20 — ALL VERIFIED against references: payment formula exact ($500K @ 5%/25yr = $2,908.02, semi-annual compounding), Ontario LTT brackets correct (marginal 0.5→2.5%), CMHC tiers correct (4.00/3.10/2.80% + 8% ON PST), stress test correct (max(rate+2%, 5.25%)), min down payment correct (5%/10%/20% tiers). FIXED: added warning when <20% down is paired with >25yr amortization (insured mortgages capped at 25yr except FTB/new builds). Plain-language explanations = the FAQ section added earlier today
- [x] Listing-card derived numbers hardened 2026-07-21: capRate/cashOnCash rendered via .toFixed unguarded — a single malformed listing (undefined/NaN field) would THROW and blank the entire listings grid. Added pct1()/money() defensive formatters (valid number → formatted; NaN/undefined → '—', never a crash or NaN); also guarded unit-rent/basement/condo-fee renders. Verified formatters + build. Engine formulas already guard divide-by-zero (capRate/CoC return 0 on price<=0)
- [x] HOMEPAGE deal cards hardened 2026-07-22: HomeDealCard (the homepage "top deals" grid — the single most important page) rendered deal.capRate.toFixed(1) UNGUARDED, so one malformed deal would THROW and blank the whole deal section; hamzaScore badge + cashFlow could also print "undefined"/"$NaN". Added the same pct1() guard + finite checks (score/cap/cash-flow → '—' fallback, never throw/NaN); also guarded the ListingCard score badge (last unguarded number on that card). Unit-tested malformed deal (no throw, clean '—'); homepage + /listings render 200; build green
- [x] ALL remaining listing-view number renders hardened 2026-07-22: swept the rest of the public listing surfaces that still had the same unguarded-.toFixed throw risk — the Top-Picks mini card (listings-container.js), the table view (listing-table.js), and the map popups (listing-map.js) all called capRate/cashOnCash.toFixed(1) raw. Made it a DRY fix: added a guarded pct1() to lib/utils/format.js and hardened fmtK/fmtNum/fmtCurrency to return '—' instead of "$NaN" on non-finite input (used site-wide), then routed those views + the deal-screener aggregate tiles through the guards. Unit-tested (undefined/NaN/null → '—', valid unchanged); /listings + homepage 200; build green. Defensive-number rendering is now consistent across every listing card/table/map/screener
  - FOUND + FIXED 2026-07-27 (improvement agent): `/compare` was missed by this sweep — two raw `listing.capRate.toFixed(1)` calls (the "Best Investment" winner banner and the "Best Cap Rate" summary tile) would THROW and blank the whole comparison for any of the up to 4 listings a visitor picked, if one had a malformed/undefined capRate. The page's own METRICS table already used the safe `(v || 0).toFixed(1)` pattern — only these two standalone JSX spots outside that table were unguarded. Routed both through the same `pct1()` used everywhere else. Verified directly: `{ capRate: undefined }.capRate.toFixed(1)` throws `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'toFixed')`; `pct1(undefined)` returns `'—'` — same guard, zero behavior change for any real (finite) capRate. Build green, one file, 3 lines.
- [x] Sold comps audited 2026-07-21: type/beds±1/baths±1/FSA filters correct, BUT had NO recency window — 2-year-old sales could skew "current market" averages. Fixed: 180-day ModificationTimestamp window on targeted queries (broad fallback stays unwindowed but is now labeled); freshness caption under stats ("Based on N closed sales in the last 180 days..."), honest "(older sales included)" note when the fallback fires. Rental-comps recency VERIFIED 2026-07-21 — already correct: 12-month window + 18-month wider fallback + active-rental tier, each with an honest freshness label. No change needed
- [x] Market-pulse stats 2026-07-21: verified aggregation math (sale-to-list = (100+negotiationGap)/100, avg DOM/price means — all correct). Real gap was PROVENANCE: page blends continuously-updated live MLS stats with a FIXED TRREB monthly snapshot (Feb 2026) and presented both as "current" — 5-month-old sale-to-list/inventory/YoY read as today's numbers. Added `tRREBAsOf` ISO date to /api/market-stats and a data-provenance strip on the page (live-vs-monthly split + "as of {month}"), plus an as-of caption on the Avg-Prices-by-Type chart (which silently falls back to TRREB sold prices). NEEDS HAMZA: refresh the hardcoded TRREB Feb 2026 block in /api/market-stats when newer Market Watch data is published
- [x] Estimated-value display verified 2026-07-21: already shows range + confidence badge + comp count (not false precision — good). Hardened the render: range only shows when both bounds are finite, confidence badge/comp-count guarded against undefined (were unguarded — confidence.charAt would throw, could show 'undefined comparable sales')
- [x] WRONG-NUMBER fix 2026-07-21: listing-detail interactive calculator (Mortgage tab) OMITTED CONDO FEES entirely — calculateCashFlow had no condo-fee param, so a condo's "Potential Cash Flow", "Cash-on-Cash" and Monthly Breakdown ignored the $400-800/mo fee while the listing card (process-listings) correctly included it. A cash-flow-negative condo could read positive. Fixed calculateCashFlow to accept monthlyCondoFee and, like the listings engine, replace the rent-based maintenance reserve with the actual condo fee (no double-count); passed listing.condoFee at both call sites (cash flow + cash-on-cash); added a Condo Fee breakdown row (replaces the Maintenance row for condos). Hand-verified: $500k/$2500 condo w/ $600 fee → maintenance $417→$0, condo fee +$600, cash flow correctly −$183/mo vs before; parts sum == total. Still open: break-even rent (needs a solve; deferred)
- [x] WRONG-NUMBER fix (part 2) 2026-07-21: same condo-fee omission existed in the listing-detail Analysis tab's CAP RATE — calculateNOI was called without monthlyCondoFee, so a condo's NOI excluded the fee and its cap rate read ~1 pt too HIGH vs the card. Added the same "condo fee replaces maintenance reserve" logic inside calculateNOI (process-listings already zeroed maintenance for condos, so unaffected), passed listing.condoFee at the Analysis-tab call site, and added a Condo Fee row to the NOI Breakdown. Hand-verified: $500k/$2500 condo w/ $600 fee → cap rate 4.08%→3.12% (now matches the card). Condo-fee consistency now closed across all 3 metrics (cash flow, cash-on-cash, cap rate/NOI)
- [x] Break-even rent added 2026-07-21 (closes the "add investor metrics" item — cash-on-cash + monthly carrying breakdown already existed): new breakEvenRent() engine helper + a line on the listing-detail Mortgage tab showing the gross monthly rent at which the property covers every cost, and whether the estimated rent clears or falls short of it. Solved NUMERICALLY against the actual calculateCashFlow (binary search on the monotonic cash-flow-vs-rent curve) so it can NEVER diverge from the cash-flow figure shown beside it, and it inherits the correct freehold-maintenance vs condo-fee handling automatically. Hand-verified: freehold $700k → $4,672/mo (matches closed-form; cashFlow=0 at that rent, clean ±$1 crossing), condo $500k+$600 fee → $3,554/mo. Recomputes live with the sliders. Build green

- [x] Equity-build ("forced savings") shown on the listing calculator 2026-07-22: the CAP/PCF question from Hamza exposed that negative cash flow reads as "losing money" when most of it is principal paydown the investor KEEPS — a conversion-killing misread that nothing on the site corrected. Added a verified mortgageSplit() engine helper (first-month interest/principal split, same Canadian semi-annual compounding as calcMonthly) and a green line on the listing-detail Mortgage tab: "You build ~$X/mo in equity … of the $Y payment, $X pays down principal (year one) — money you keep." When cash flow is negative AND equity build exceeds the shortfall, it honestly adds "your net worth still grows." Recomputes live with the sliders. ADDS a line only — no existing number (cash flow, cap rate, score) changed. Hand-verified: $630k→$867/mo, $999k→$1,374/mo, $800k→$1,100/mo principal (exact match to the earlier CAP/PCF reconciliation); build green.
- [x] **WRONG NUMBER — Toronto municipal LTT missing from cash-on-cash — FIXED 2026-07-25 (improvement agent).** New `calculateTorontoMLTT()` (Toronto's published residential schedule: provincial-mirroring brackets to $3M, then the 2024 luxury tiers 3.5/4.5/5.5/6.5/7.5%), `chargesTorontoMLTT()` (matches Toronto AND the five amalgamated districts — Etobicoke/North York/Scarborough/East York/York — which our GTA feed reports under district names, the same trap the property-tax table had to fix), and `calculateLandTransferTax(price, city)` = provincial + municipal-when-Toronto. City is now threaded into `calculateCashOnCash()` and `getClosingCosts()` at all three call sites (process-listings for every card, plus both listing-detail tabs), and the detail page's closing-costs panel gained a real "Toronto Municipal LTT" row — replacing the footnote that used to apologize for the omission. HAND-VERIFIED against published figures: $1M provincial $16,475 (the standard reference) + Toronto MLTT $16,475 = $32,950 combined; $500K/$1.5M/$2M/$2.5M all match; luxury tiers rebuilt bracket-by-bracket ($4M = $96,475, $5M = $141,475); parts sum to totals; no city passed = byte-identical old behavior (Mississauga unaffected). IMPACT: a $1M Toronto purchase needs $235,950 at closing, not $219,475, and a positive-cash-flow Toronto deal that read 4.10% cash-on-cash now correctly reads 3.81%. Original finding text kept below for the record:
- ~~**Toronto municipal LTT is missing from cash-on-cash (found 2026-07-25 by the seo agent)**~~ `calculateOntarioLTT()` is the only land-transfer-tax function in the codebase (`lib/cash-flow-engine.js:192`), and both `calculateCashOnCash()` and `getClosingCosts()` call it with price alone — no city. Toronto (and its amalgamated districts Etobicoke / North York / Scarborough / East York / York) charges a **municipal** LTT on top of the provincial one, at roughly the same brackets, so the real closing cost there is about DOUBLE what we show. On a $1M Toronto purchase that is ~$16.5K of cash required that we omit, which INFLATES the cash-on-cash return on every Toronto-area GTA listing (smaller denominator) and flatters those deals against Mississauga ones. Fix needs: a city-aware LTT (municipal component for the 6 Toronto names, first-time-buyer rebates are irrelevant for investors), threaded through both call sites plus the listing-detail closing-costs breakdown. Hand-verify against a known example before shipping. NOTE: task list #8 was marked "Add Toronto municipal LTT to cash-on-cash for GTA listings" as completed — the code shows it never landed; treat that checkmark as wrong.
- NEEDS HAMZA (flagged 2026-07-22): the cash-flow engine uses a FLAT monthlyInsurance=$225 for every property regardless of price/type (a $1.5M detached and a $500K condo both get $225/mo). That's a deliberate simplification, not a clear bug, but it understates carrying cost on expensive homes and affects every listing's cash flow/cap rate/score site-wide — so it needs a product decision (keep flat, or scale by value/type?) before changing. Not touched.

## 3 — Weekly emails & alerts

- [x] Condo-fee provenance disclosed in both emails — DONE 2026-07-27 (improvement agent). The feed never supplies a real condo fee (confirmed 2026-07-27: fieldTier settles at `core+media`, never `fees+media`), so CAP/cash-flow for every condo/apt listing bakes in a bedroom/sqft ESTIMATE — the site labels this "Condo Fee (est.)" everywhere it's shown (card, listing detail, NOI breakdown), but both the daily alert email and the weekly newsletter rendered the same estimate-derived numbers with zero disclosure, the exact provenance gap the rent-assumption line was added to close for rent. Both templates already carry a `condoFeeEstimated` flag straight from `processListings()` (no field-stripping step drops it before templating) — extended the existing "Assumes $X/mo rent" disclosure line in both `rentAssumptionLine()` (alerts) and `rentAssumption()` (newsletter) to append "· condo fee is estimated" when the flag is set, reusing the established disclosure pattern instead of adding new UI. Verified with a real preview render (`npm run dev`, `/api/newsletter/weekly?preview=1`): the Condo Apt sample now renders "Assumes $2,700/mo rent · condo fee is estimated" while the two non-condo samples are unchanged. Build green, two files, ~10 lines, no calculation touched — only a disclosure string.
- [x] CRITICAL (QA 2026-07-21): daily /api/alerts/send cron was 500-ing every day — NO alert emails were going out. Root cause: it built the internal listings fetch from `new URL('/api/listings', request.url)`; on a cron invocation request.url is the *.vercel.app deployment host, which sits behind Vercel deployment protection and serves an HTML auth wall (200), so `.json()` crashed on "<!DOCTYPE ...". Confirmed via Vercel logs (13:04 UTC 500; no /api/listings function invocation). Fixed to fetch from the absolute public SITE_URL (same pattern listing metadata already uses) + added a content-type guard so a non-JSON body throws a clear error instead of a cryptic JSON.parse. Verify next 13:00 UTC run returns 200 + Resend acceptance

- [x] Weekly newsletter template audited 2026-07-20: table layout + inline styles + 600px max all correct, UTM params and unsubscribe link present, user fields escaped. Added ?preview=1 test-render mode (dev-open, auth-gated in prod). FIXED: template fabricated stats when data was missing ("2,500+" active listings, avgDOM 28, sale-to-list 97.2%) and rendered "N/A" tiles — stat blocks now show only real data and hide themselves otherwise. Dark-mode-safe pass still open
- [x] CRITICAL follow-up 2026-07-21: the SAME request.url self-fetch bug that broke the alerts cron also lived in /api/newsletter/weekly (fetchScoredListings built the listings URL from request.url). On the Monday 14:00 UTC cron that host is the protected *.vercel.app deploy → HTML auth wall → the fetch's try/catch swallowed the JSON.parse crash and returned [], so the weekly newsletter would have shipped with ZERO deals (its entire point) with no error surfaced. Fixed to the hardcoded public SITE_URL (matching the market-stats fetch right above it) + content-type guard + error logging so a failure is diagnosable, not silent. Preview verified: renders, 0 "N/A". NEEDS HAMZA: still ships only via the next daily release
- [x] RENT ASSUMPTION NOW IN BOTH EMAILS 2026-07-25 (improvement agent): the site's listing cards state "Est. rent $X/mo · every figure below assumes this rent" on every card — added after an investor's critique that a cash-flow number with an unstated rent assumption isn't credible — but BOTH emails shipped the figures bare. The daily alert row and the weekly newsletter's hero + "Also on the Market" rows showed CAP/cash-flow with no indication of what rent produced them, so the channel that reaches subscribers directly was the one place the assumption stayed hidden, and a skeptical investor had no way to sanity-check "+$450/mo". Both templates now render the same line, reading the SAME fields the card's RentAssumption component reads (estimatedRent / baseRent / basementIncome / basementTier / unitBreakdown) so email and site can never disagree, with the suite and multi-unit breakdowns that make large totals believable. Renders NOTHING when rent is unknown rather than inventing one. Unit-verified 7 shapes against the real source (plain, legal suite, potential suite, multi-unit, zero/undefined/NaN rent → blank) and confirmed in a live newsletter preview render: "Assumes $4,300/mo rent — $3,100 main + $1,200 legal suite". Also gave the preview fixtures rents so the preview actually exercises the line. Remaining newsletter item: leads with the best deals + one useful market stat; subject lines specific not clickbait; correct unsubscribe link in every send. SUBJECT LINE done 2026-07-21: the generic subject (most of the 477 imported subscribers have no saved search) was a flat "Mississauga Market Weekly — {date}" with no hook. Now leads with the top deal's REAL, honest hook via buildSubject(): "+$X/mo cash flow in {hood}" when it cash-flows, else "N new deals — top pick X.X% cap rate in {hood}", else clean fallbacks. All values guarded (never N/A/NaN/undefined — unit-tested across 6 cases); honest (won't claim cash flow a negative-CF deal doesn't have). Email already leads with deals + market numbers, and unsubscribe link is in every send (signed + List-Unsubscribe). Remaining: could A/B test subjects later
- [x] Deal alerts: verify matching logic against saved filters (no misses, no floods), cap per-email volume, dedupe listings already sent. AUDITED 2026-07-20: per-email dedupe + 15-listing cap + DOM≤3 freshness gate all correct, BUT the DOM≤3 window can resend the same listing up to 4 consecutive days — proper fix needs a sent-listings table (Supabase migration; product call on schema)
  - FIXED 2026-07-25 (improvement agent): new `supabase/migrations/create_alert_sent_listings.sql` (email+listing_id PK, sent_at index, RLS) + repeat-send guard in /api/alerts/send: one batched 7-day-lookback query per run builds an email→Set(listing_id) map; each search's matches drop already-sent ids BEFORE the DOM/cap slicing (first-alert path too, so a second saved search can't re-deliver what an earlier alert showed); rows recorded via upsert ONLY after Resend accepts (a rejected send stays eligible); 14-day best-effort cleanup keeps the table small. DEGRADES GRACEFULLY: if the migration hasn't run or any guard query fails, the sent-set stays empty and behavior is byte-identical to before — dedupe can never block a send. Unit-verified 5 cases (repeat excluded, empty-set = old behavior, first-alert exclusion, numeric/string id coercion, all-repeats → email skipped). NEEDS HAMZA (one-time): run the new migration in Supabase SQL editor to turn the guard on — until then the run logs a "repeat-send guard unavailable" note and sends as before.

## 4 — Design & trust

- [x] sqft chip on the listing detail quick-stats 2026-07-27 (design): real square footage has flowed since the LivingAreaRange unlock but was never DISPLAYED — it only fed JSON-LD and the estimated-value API. Added a chip beside type/beds/DOM: "~1,750 sqft", tilde-prefixed via `sqftApproximate` because TREB serves a banded range and the value is a band midpoint, not a measured area; hidden at sqft 0. FOUND WHILE VERIFYING: `processListings` rebuilds each listing and silently DROPPED `sqftApproximate` — un-flagging every approximate value for all consumers (pricePerSqFt included); the flag is now carried through the pipeline. Fixture-verified at 375px: chip renders "~1,750 sqft", no overflow; without the pipeline fix it rendered unflagged "1,750 sqft".

- [x] Audit every public page at 375px for overflow, tap targets, layout breaks — COMPLETE 2026-07-20: homepage, listings, mortgage-calculator, market-pulse, gta, blog, precon, auth all clean (no horizontal overflow). Issues found & fixed along the way: cookie banner size, market-pulse zero-stat, deal-screener zero-dashboard. (768px pass still open if wanted)
- [x] Trust signals near every capture form — verified/complete 2026-07-20: homepage EmailCapture ("no spam, unsubscribe anytime"), exit-intent popup ("free forever" + social proof), and /alerts form (added expectation-setting block: match-only emails, free, one-click unsubscribe, email never shared). Brand identity consistent
- [x] Error/empty/loading states — complete 2026-07-21: deal-screener skeleton/empty (Jul 20), market-pulse + news hide unavailable stats, compare/saved have proper empty states (verified), and the big one: /listings no longer shows eternal 'Analyzing...' skeletons when the feed fails — honest error card with Try Again retry + phone fallback; also fixed the '{0 && ...}' JSX bug rendering 'Showing 0 0investment properties'
- [x] Accessibility — FOOTER AA FIX 2026-07-25 (design): measured every capture-path page at a true 375px viewport and found the site-wide footer failing badly on EVERY page — the mandatory disclosures (PropTx VOW bona-fide notice, TRREB/CREA trademark attribution, investment + deal-score disclaimers, PIPEDA/CASL notice) rendered at 10px `text-white/30` = ~2.4:1, and the copyright plus the **Privacy Policy / Terms** links at `text-white/25` = 2.23:1. A legally-required disclosure nobody can read is barely a disclosure, and it sat under every page of a site whose whole pitch is trustworthiness. Also failing: every footer link using brand `text-accent` on navy (2.75:1) — including the tap-to-call PHONE NUMBER and email, i.e. conversion elements. Fixed: disclosures to `text-white/60` at 11px (6.1:1), copyright/legal links to `text-white/70`, and on-navy links to `#8AB6FF` (6.9:1) — already the site's established on-navy blue from the homepage hero/seller band, so nothing new was invented. Also raised the public capture forms' required-field asterisk from red-400 (2.77:1) to red-600, marked aria-hidden since 'required' is already conveyed to assistive tech by the input. Re-measured: ZERO AA failures on /signup, /alerts, /, /listings and /blog. (Admin pages keep red-400 — they are dark-themed, where it passes.) Earlier a11y work 2026-07-21: mega-menu keyboard nav (Jul 20), form labels verified (alerts/signup-gate have real labels; homepage EmailCapture + exit-intent inputs now have aria-labels), error messages announce via role=alert and successes via role=status on all capture forms, focus rings present on inputs/buttons (focus:ring-accent)
- [~] Performance: next/image everywhere, dynamic-import leaflet/markdown, LCP/CLS on homepage and listing detail — THIRD-PARTY CDN REMOVED 2026-07-25 (seo): the map component fetched Leaflet's stylesheet at runtime from `unpkg.com` even though `leaflet` is an installed npm dependency whose dist ships that exact file. So the map's appearance depended on a third-party CDN being reachable (an ad blocker, corporate proxy or unpkg blip left it unstyled), it cost a DNS+TLS handshake to an un-preconnected origin at the moment the user opened the map, it was an unconsented third-party request on a site otherwise careful about PIPEDA, and the URL hardcoded 1.9.4 while package.json resolves ^1.9.4 — a minor bump would have desynced CSS from JS. Now a plain `import 'leaflet/dist/leaflet.css'`; webpack emits it as its OWN 12K CSS chunk (26 leaflet rules) loaded only with the dynamically-imported map, so non-map visitors still pay nothing. Verified: zero `unpkg.com` references remain anywhere in .next/static or .next/server, and zero unpkg requests fire in a real browser session. COULD NOT visually confirm the styled map locally (no AMPRE_TOKEN -> the listings feed fails -> the map toggle never renders, so the chunk is never requested); the bundling evidence is conclusive but it deserves a production eyeball on the /listings map view. Earlier CWV win 2026-07-21: added `preconnect` + `dns-prefetch` to the MLS photo CDN (trreb-image.ampre.ca) in the root layout head, so the browser opens the TLS connection before it discovers the LCP `<img>` on listings/detail/homepage deal cards; dns-prefetch for GTM + Clarity too. Verified tags render site-wide. Still open: convert remaining raw <img> to next/image, audit CLS

### REFRESH 2026-07-26 (Hamza dropped a new 3-month GSC export, "do what needs to be done")

**Headline: traffic is genuinely accelerating, not stalling.** The exported Queries tab undercounts (GSC omits very-low-volume queries from that export for privacy — a known, documented behaviour, not a real drop), so the real read is the Chart tab's daily total: 133 clicks / 9,582 impressions over the 3-month window, up from 117/8.94K last time, and the WEEKLY breakdown shows it isn't flat growth — clicks jumped from single digits/week through May–June to 37 and 35 in the first two weeks of July, right as the redesign/SEO/conversion work shipped. Good sign, not a false one — verified against the Chart tab's own daily series, not inferred from the (misleadingly smaller) Queries tab total.

**All 6 dedicated pages built for the 2026-07-21/22 striking-distance list are still flat.** None of the 6 target queries has moved meaningfully (a couple even ticked slightly worse), and every one still shows 0 clicks. Too early to call this a failure — 4–5 days is not enough time for a new page to fully index and re-rank, and today's robots.txt fix (Googlebot was blocked from the site's own CSS/JS until this morning) removes a real handicap that was suppressing ALL of this work; expect this list to need another refresh in 2–3 weeks before judging it.

**The real finding this round: severe, evidenced keyword cannibalization from the auto-blog generator, including two direct collisions with the dedicated pages themselves.** Checked page-level GSC data for every one of the 6 built pages and found:
- `/rental-property-insurance-mississauga` (the dedicated page) sits at **position 55.48** (64 impressions) while `/blog/mississauga-rental-property-insurance-2026-complete-guide` — an auto-generated post on the identical topic — sits at **position 12.13** (54 impressions). The page built specifically to win this query is losing to the site's own blog post.
- `/mortgage-calculator` sits at **position 30.93** (82 impressions) while `/blog/investment-property-mortgages-in-mississauga-2026-complete-guide` sits at **position 12.42** (36 impressions) — same pattern.
- Three separate posts on "rent vs buy" (positions 30.79/24 impr, and two more), three on "cap rate / cash flow / ROI" (one pair literally the SAME title under two different slugs — `cap-rate-vs-cash-flow-vs-roi-mississauga-investment-guide-2026-mp850elg` and `-mq5050pt`), and four on "how to analyze rental cash flow" — none of these break out of position 5–15, each splitting the same search intent with its siblings.

**Root-caused and fixed the generator bug** (`app/api/auto-blog/route.js`, `pickTopic`'s dedup check): it split titles on whitespace only (a trailing comma/colon stuck to a keyword broke every match — "rate," and "mississauga:" never matched anything), required words over 4 characters (discarding exactly the short words that define this domain — "cap", "rent", "buy", "roi"), and did zero stemming ("renting"/"rent", "buying"/"buy", "rates"/"rate" were treated as unrelated words). Reproduced the exact failure against the real duplicate title pairs pulled from this export before touching any code, then fixed it with punctuation stripping, a stopword list tuned to what's generic on THIS site (not a length cutoff), and light stemming — verified the fix now blocks all three reproduced cases. Also added a small `RESERVED_PAGE_TOPICS` list (rental insurance, mortgage, rent-vs-buy) that pre-excludes any evergreen topic angle overlapping a page already hand-built for that query, closing the two direct page-vs-blog collisions above at the source, and surfaced the same reserved list in the LLM's own "don't repeat these" prompt block so the news-headline-driven path (which bypasses `pickTopic` entirely) also avoids them. Removed one template angle that was a word-for-word duplicate of another already in the pool ("understanding cash flow analysis for Mississauga rentals" vs Strategy's "how to analyze a rental property deal in under 5 minutes") — this ONE pair had already produced two live, distinct-enough-worded posts that the fixed fuzzy check would NOT have reliably caught after the fact, so removing it from the source was the only actually-reliable fix. Two other borderline-overlapping angle pairs were deliberately left as-is after confirming the fixed fuzzy check reliably blocks them from being independently re-published even under realistic LLM wording variation (stress-tested with plausible divergent titles, not just the original angle text).
- [~] 2026-07-26 — Fixed the auto-blog dedup bug causing the above (see paragraph above for detail). PREVENTS new duplicates; does NOT clean up the ones already live. That cleanup (301-redirect or merge the losing post into the winning page/post for each of the 4 evidenced clusters above) still needs Supabase write access this environment doesn't have — see item 13 in Code Health, now re-scoped with the exact URLs.
- [x] 2026-07-28 — Canonical consolidation: for the 3 evidenced blog-vs-dedicated-page collisions, `blog/[slug]/page.js` now redirects search authority to the dedicated page via `<link rel="canonical">` override. A `CANONICAL_OVERRIDES` array (slug substring → absolute canonical URL) covers the three measured cases: `rental-property-insurance-*` → `/rental-property-insurance-mississauga`, `investment-property-mortgage-*` → `/mortgage-calculator`, `rent-vs-buy-*` → `/rent-vs-buy-mississauga`. The override is applied in `generateMetadata` (the only place that matters for crawlers); the blog post itself stays accessible and renders a subtle "dedicated interactive guide" notice box pointing to the canonical page so users also find the better-converting destination. No hardcoded slugs needed — substring matching catches any slug variation the auto-blog generator produces. The non-canonical blog posts keep their Article JSON-LD (legitimate articles) while the `<head>` canonical tells Google which URL to rank. Build green, one file.
- [ ] REFRESH: Hamza can re-export GSC (Performance → Export) anytime and drop the xlsx in chat — reprioritize this list from the new numbers. Address-level queries already earn clicks (listing SEO works — leave it)

## 5 — Investor value & SEO

- [x] **/pre-construction/projects was a client-only shell — server-fetched, crawlers now see the projects. Fixed 2026-07-31 (improvement agent, first of the two audit-deferred restructures).** The page's meta promises "80+ pre-construction condos and townhomes" but the entire list arrived via a client-side `/api/precon` fetch after hydration, so crawlers saw only a loading skeleton and a hero chip reading "0+ Active Projects Across the GTA" — an indexable-content gap on a lead-gen vertical (every project card CTAs into the VIP list). Restructured on the listing-detail pattern: `page.js` is now a server component (revalidate 300, matching /api/precon's s-maxage) using the VERCEL-gated SITE_URL fetch with an 8s AbortSignal timeout; the old client page moved byte-for-byte (minus the fetch wiring) to `projects-client.js` and receives `initialProjects` + a `fromApi` flag. The client only re-fetches when the server render was handed the fallback (`fromApi=false`) — no redundant refetch of a 5-min-cached endpoint on every mount, but transient server-side failures still self-heal in the browser. The curated 3-project fallback moved to shared `lib/precon/fallback-projects.js` so server and client can't drift. BONUS honest-numbers fix: the hero chip only renders a count when `projects.length >= 10` (the real DB list); the fallback/pre-data render previously produced "0+"/"3+ Active Projects" — an understatement contradicting the page's own meta. Verified in the built static HTML (env-less build → fallback path): all 3 fallback project names + developers + the Mississauga h2 server-rendered, no skeleton (loading initializes false when initial props exist), numberless hero chip, `fromApi:false` in the RSC payload. On Vercel the build/ISR fetch hits the live domain and embeds the full real list. Build green, 3 files. REMAINING from the same audit: /market-pulse is still 100% client-rendered — same restructure applies (server-fetch /api/market-stats, pass as initial props); good candidate for a next run.
- [x] **/market-pulse server-rendered — DONE 2026-07-31 (improvement agent, second and last of the two audit-deferred restructures; commit 6daed49).** Closes the item directly above. The page's every figure (avg DOM, sale-to-list, months of inventory, the price-by-type chart, the Motivated Seller Radar, mortgage rates) arrived only after a browser fetch of `/api/market-stats`, and until it resolved the `loading` branch rendered a bare skeleton — which also meant **no InlineCTA and no StickyMobileCTA on the page at all** for up to ten seconds, i.e. a lead-capture gap, not just an SEO one. Split on the /recent-sales pattern: `page.js` is now a server component (`revalidate = 900`, VERCEL-gated SITE_URL origin, 8s `AbortSignal.timeout` so a cold 60-90s market-stats recompute can never hold a render) and the old client page moved to `market-pulse-client.js` taking `initialStats`. `loading` initializes to `!initialStats`, and the client fetch is kept but now runs **only** when `initialStats` came through null — so a failed/timed-out server fetch degrades to exactly today's behaviour, never worse. Not one figure, fallback or piece of copy changed. VERIFICATION, honestly: build green (121 pages), and a build whose fetch cache happened to be warm prerendered the FULL page (Avg DOM, months supply, Current Mortgage Rates, Average Prices by Property Type, the sticky CTA) into static HTML — proving the server→client wiring renders. A cold build with nothing listening on the origin prerenders the skeleton instead, but `/recent-sales` does exactly the same under those conditions, so that is the shipped pattern's normal env-less behaviour, not a defect. The runtime self-fetch could NOT be verified under `next start` in the sandbox (concurrent dev/prod servers corrupted `.next` mid-test); it is production-proven on /recent-sales and /pre-construction/projects. Worth eyeballing `/market-pulse` on production after the next release — the metric tiles should be in the HTML before JS runs.

- [x] Sitemaps unified onto the shared feed fetch 2026-07-27 (seo): both app/sitemap.js listing branches and the image sitemap still ran their own fetch loops with limit=200 cache keys — the split-brain that bit /listings SSR (#61) and market-stats (#65), and the reason this cycle's live sitemap regenerated with ZERO /listings/{id} URLs while the pages served fresh rows. All three now use fetchFeedPages (limit=100 keys, 8s bounds preserved, same budgets), so every consumer of the feed — pages, stats, sitemaps — reads the same Data Cache entries and can never disagree again. Dead timedFetch helpers removed. Build green with no env vars (fetch failures degrade to the same empty branches as before).

- [x] Community in listing-detail titles 2026-07-27 (seo): the CityRegion unlock means detail pages know their real community, but the metadata title on ~5,400 of the site's highest-traffic pages (the only pages already earning GSC clicks, on address queries) still read "{address}, Mississauga". Title/OG/description now render "{address}, Port Credit, Mississauga" style — targets street+neighbourhood queries and makes every title genuinely distinct. Guarded so the hood only renders when it adds information (fallback sets neighbourhood = city; a doubled "Mississauga, Mississauga" would read as a bug in the SERP). Verified with a real prod-build render against a temporary, fully-reverted fixture stub: community case correct in title/og:title/description, hood==city case does NOT double. Build green, one file — cdf97a1

- [x] Neighbourhood guides verified 2026-07-21: data-rich metadata with real numbers (price, YoY, yield, DOM), FAQ + breadcrumb schema, and full interlinking (filtered listings CTA, book-call, sibling guides, methodology). Listing detail pages link back to guides (Jul 20). Complete
- [x] Blog/auto-blog SEO complete 2026-07-21: metadata + Article/Breadcrumb schema + guaranteed images all in place; generation prompt now requires 2-3 internal links from a fixed allowed-path list (listings/calculator/market-pulse/recent-sales/alerts/neighbourhood guides — no invented URLs); markdown renderer keeps internal links same-tab (external still new-tab). - [x] Listing-detail OG/Twitter image fallback 2026-07-23 (seo): listing detail pages (the highest-traffic page type — they win address queries, and realtors share these links constantly in texts/social) build their og:image + twitter:image from the listing's first photo, but when that photo was absent OR the /api/photos fetch failed (that endpoint intermittently times out — 29 hits in the last runtime-error window) the openGraph/twitter blocks got NO images at all, so the listing shared with a blank preview. Same absolute-URL mechanism as the 22-page og:image fix (verified resolves to the branded card). Build green (89 pages); no calc/data change.
- [x] OG social card redesigned 2026-07-23 (design): the branded /opengraph-image (now load-bearing on 22+ pages after yesterday's og:image fix — it's the preview on every FB/LinkedIn/WhatsApp/Slack/iMessage share) hardcoded FOUR market stats presented as current — "2,000+ Active Listings", "96% Sale-to-List", "36 days Avg DOM", "5.2 mo Inventory" — baked into a static, un-refreshable edge image, so they silently rot into wrong numbers on the brand's most-shared asset (the exact "a wrong number is the worst bug" trap, and the same stale-provenance issue flagged on market-pulse). Also used an off-brand emerald ".ca" (the site wordmark is accent-blue #2563EB everywhere). Rebuilt on the brand "dusk" gradient (#16223D→navy #1B2A4A→#25355C, matching PageHero), site-matched accent-blue ".ca", a gold (#F59E0B) accent underline for polish, and replaced the decaying stat tiles with FOUR evergreen always-true capability chips (Live MLS Listings · Cash Flow & Cap Rate · Investor Deal Scores · Free Deal Alerts). Rendered the endpoint locally: 200, valid 1200×630 PNG, premium + on-brand; white-on-navy AA. No calc/data logic touched (presentational asset only).
- [x] og:image regression fixed site-wide 2026-07-23: EVERY page that exports a page-specific `openGraph` block (to set its own og:title/description/url) was silently DROPPING the branded og:image — Next.js replaces, not merges, the `openGraph` object across segments, so the root layout's `openGraph.images` was lost the moment a page defined its own openGraph. Confirmed in the prerendered HTML: 22 pages (market-pulse, book-call, faq, compare, quiz, all 5 investor guides, /guides, /sell, /alerts, mortgage-calculator, neighbourhoods index+detail, recent-sales, all 3 pre-construction pages, blog index) had `twitter:image` (root twitter isn't overridden) but NO `og:image` — so on Facebook/LinkedIn/WhatsApp/Slack/iMessage (which read og:image, not twitter:image) these money pages shared with a blank/broken preview, killing share CTR + trust. Added `images: ['/opengraph-image']` (the branded dynamic OG, resolved absolute via metadataBase) to each block. Pages that intentionally set their own image (about→headshot, blog/[slug]→cover, listings/[id]→listing photo) left untouched. Verified og:image now renders on all previously-broken static pages on a clean prod build (89 pages); book-call is dynamic (identical source fix). Zero calc/data change.
- [x] 28 GTA city-page descriptions de-templated 2026-07-27 (seo): 27 of the 28 `/gta?city=` pages' meta description, OG/Twitter description AND visible hero subtitle were literally "Active {city} listings — scored and analyzed" — the same five words with only the city name swapped, on 27 separate indexable pages. That is the exact near-duplicate-content pattern Google's own guidance flags as hurting indexing, and it wasted the one genuinely city-specific fact these pages already had on hand: the real municipal property tax rate `CityInvestorNotes` already states in prose lower on the same page (27 of 28 cities have one researched in `lib/constants.js`; only King doesn't). Rebuilt `sub` for all non-Toronto cities from `region` + `getTaxRate()`/`hasExplicitTaxRate()` — the SAME constants the cash-flow engine reads, so it can't drift or need hand-updating — giving each page a real, distinct sentence (e.g. "Active Brampton listings in Peel Region — cash flow, cap rate and deal score costed with Brampton's 1.13% property tax."); King correctly falls back to the generic sentence since it has no researched rate. Toronto's own hand-written aggregate copy (names its 5 sub-areas) was left untouched — already unique, regenerating it would be redundant. Verified programmatically (27/27 unique, longest 132 chars — safe for a meta description) and with a real prod-build render at 375px on 3 cities (Halton Hills, Toronto, King): zero horizontal overflow, correct canonical, description/OG-description/subtitle all match the new text. Build green, one file, ~20 lines. No calc/data logic touched — description strings only.
- [x] GTA city pages de-templated a SECOND time 2026-07-31 (seo) — the 2026-07-27 pass above fixed the words but not the shape. On 27 path-segment URLs the sitemap submits at priority 0.7, that is doorway-shaped. Added a `place` field per city — one QUALITATIVE, verifiable clause naming real communities, GO/subway lines, geography or institutions ("from Mimico and Humber Bay Shores out to Kipling station", "near Ontario Tech University and the GM Oshawa assembly plant", "in the Dundas Valley at the west end of Hamilton") — and rebuilt `sub` to LEAD with it, tax rate second. Deliberately contains no statistic, price or count: nothing on that page can source one, so inventing a "median price" or "X% of stock is condos" was off the table. The same `place` clause now also opens the visible "Investing in {city}" paragraph and the first FAQPage answer, so the body copy and JSON-LD diverge per city too. Also fixed the article agreement in that paragraph — "a Oshawa listing" → "an Oshawa listing" (5 vowel-initial city names).
