- [x] GSC indexing fixes 2026-07-28 (Hamza request — "analize this and fix it" re: Coverage Excel + Page Indexing screenshots): Parsed the GSC Coverage Excel (Coverage20260727.xlsx, 7 non-indexing reasons). Fixed two root causes: (1) THREE noindex pages (/profile, /saved, /recent) had `robots: { index: false }` but NO `alternates.canonical` in their layout.js — GSC reported these as "Duplicate without user-selected canonical" because Google crawls pages even when noindex'd and flags canonical absence separately from the noindex directive. Added self-referencing canonical to all three layouts. (2) /compare rendered thin content for Google — the page is `'use client'` and its only server-rendered output was a PageHero + loading skeleton; after JS executed, Google saw only "No properties to compare" (empty state) + a CTA link, which is Soft 404 territory. Fixed by adding a server-rendered 4-question FAQ section to compare/layout.js covering what metrics are compared, how Deal Score works, how rent is calculated, and how to add properties — gives Google ~300 words of relevant indexable content and makes the page rankable for "compare investment properties" queries without changing the user experience when listings are loaded. STILL OPEN: (a) "Duplicate, Google chose different canonical than user" (9 pages) — most likely the 28 GTA city pages (/gta?city=X) where Google prefers /gta over the query-param self-canonicals; the fix is migrating city pages to clean path URLs (/gta/toronto, /gta/brampton, etc.) — a larger refactor, noted below. (b) 2 remaining "Blocked by robots.txt" pages are almost certainly intentional API routes (/api/market-stats, /api/listings) — no action needed. Build green, 4 files.
- [x] GTA city pages: migrated from /gta?city=X query params to /gta/[city] path segments 2026-07-28. Now: new app/(public)/gta/[city]/page.js with generateStaticParams + generateMetadata + proper path-segment canonical; gta/page.js adds permanentRedirect() for old ?city= URLs (fixes old bookmarks/indexed URLs); header.js cityHref() and region-switcher both updated to /gta/[slug]; sitemap.js updated to /gta/[slug]; CityInvestorNotes sibling links updated; fetchGtaListings and CityInvestorNotes exported so [city]/page.js reuses them. ListingsContainer gets a city prop for path-segment pages (no ?city= in URL for these). dynamicParams=false on [city] page so unknown slugs 404 cleanly. Build green, 5 files + 1 new.

Structured data is not a crawl path; anchors are. The 19 unlinked pages included the five Toronto districts and the three Hamilton communities — the exact eight that served ZERO listings until 73ff2e3 hours earlier — whose only inbound links anywhere in server HTML were the sibling lists on their own parent-city page (the header mega-menu renders its city links only while `open`, so they are absent from server HTML; the footer carries six cities). Counted across the whole built site: `/gta/oakville` and `/gta/toronto` appear on 82 pages (footer six), every other city on 4-6 — so the districts were 3 clicks from home behind a hover menu. Added a server-rendered "Investment properties by GTA municipality" directory on the hub only, grouped by region and **derived from `CITY_COPY.region`** — the same object the ItemList and the sitemap iterate, so the visible list cannot drift from either; an unrecognised region is appended rather than dropped. Mississauga included in the Peel group pointing at `/listings` (it has no `/gta/[city]` page and a Peel list without it reads as an omission). Verified on a real prod build served by `next start`: 28 unique city anchors == 28 ItemList URLs, all 28 + `/listings` return 200, section renders clean at 375px with `documentElement.scrollWidth === 375`. - [x] Verify auth actually protects every privileged route — DONE 2026-07-26. Audited all 39 API routes; the admin/* set was already fail-closed, but THREE cron endpoints authorised anyone when their secret was unset (see the Run Log). Guards now live in `lib/api-auth.js` and fail closed. Probed every privileged route in both configurations.
- [x] Migrate the remaining hand-rolled guard in `app/api/admin/newsletter-import` and `app/api/broadcast/announcement` to `lib/api-auth.js` — DONE 2026-07-28 (improvement agent). Both broadcast routes and the newsletter-import route now use the centralized helpers. Added `isBroadcastAuthorized` + `requireBroadcast` to api-auth.js to preserve the `?key=` query-param auth path the broadcast routes expose for browser-triggered draft sends. All 3 local `isAuthorized` functions removed; 6 call sites updated. Build green.
- [x] **Full OData-injection audit of every feed route — DONE 2026-08-01 (improvement agent).** The `?city=` hole found incidentally in `/api/listings-gta` was not unique: `/api/listings` (`?city=`), `/api/rental-comps` (`?city=`, both the lease and active-rental queries) and `/api/photos` + `/api/photos-all` (`?id=`) all interpolated request input into a `$filter` with no escaping. The two photo routes were worse than the rest — their filters and entity-key paths were never passed through `encodeURIComponent` either, so an `&` in `?id=` could append arbitrary query parameters (`$top`, `$select`, `$filter`) to the upstream URL. Every escape now goes through one shared `lib/listings/odata.js` (`odataStr` for values inside an encoded `$filter`, `odataKey` for a value in the URL path or a hand-built query string), and the five routes that already had a correct ad-hoc `replace(/'/g, "''")` — sold-comps, price-history, estimated-value, listing-single, photos-batch — were migrated onto it so a second, subtly different escaper cannot drift back in. Numeric params (`parseInt`/`parseFloat` on page, limit, beds, baths, minPrice, maxPrice, price, sqft, lat, lng) are already safe and were deliberately left alone; so are the `typeMap` lookups in sold-comps / rental-comps / estimated-value (allow-listed clauses, never the caller's string) and the regex-validated `?fsa=`.

## Needs Hamza

(Items blocked on credentials, accounts, or product decisions — agents: add here, don't guess.)

- [ ] **BACKLINKS — the single biggest lever on the SEO score, and no agent can touch it (Seobility audit, 2026-07-30).** The site scores 77% overall with meta data at 99% and page structure at 100%, but **external factors at 3%**: the whole domain has **2 backlinks from 1 referring domain / 1 IP**. That is the metric dragging the score down hardest, and it is entirely off-page — nothing in this repo can move it. Agents: do NOT attempt link-building, do not add link-exchange sections, and do not buy/solicit links in code. What actually helps, for Hamza: Cityscape Real Estate's own site linking here; the local Mississauga Board of Trade and BIA directories; RECO/realtor profile links; Google Business Profile; guest posts on GTA investing sites; and being cited by local news. On-page SEO is close to exhausted — this is where the remaining upside is.
- [ ] **Homepage HTML document size ~844 kB (Seobility audit, 2026-07-30) — needs a product decision.** Flagged under both page quality and performance. Response time is excellent (0.08s) so this is not currently hurting speed, but it is a large payload on mobile. The bulk is the inlined RSC payload for the homepage listing data (the deal cards + hero deal), not CSS/JS bloat. Reducing it means cutting how many listings the homepage serializes server-side or moving some client-side — **that is listing-pipeline code, which is frozen**, and it trades off against the server-rendered content that currently makes the page indexable. Agents: do NOT unilaterally cut listing counts to chase this number. Needs Hamza to decide whether fewer inlined deals is an acceptable trade.
- [ ] **`public.alert_sent_listings` does not exist in Supabase — deal alerts are sending with NO repeat-send guard.** Confirmed in production runtime logs 2026-08-01 (first seen 2026-07-26, still firing on every `/api/alerts/send` run): `Alerts: repeat-send guard unavailable (run supabase/migrations/create_alert_sent_listings.sql?) — sending without dedupe: Could not find the table 'public.alert_sent_listings' in the schema cache`, plus a matching per-subscriber `could not record sent listings for <address>`. The code fails OPEN by design — alerts still go out — so the failure mode is a subscriber being emailed the same listing again on a later run, which is the one thing the newsletter must never do (mission #3: "never spam"). No agent can *fix* it — the migration just needs running against the Supabase project. **Made visible 2026-08-02:** it is now the first row of the admin's email-setup panel, flagged **LIVE ISSUE** in red with a one-tap **Copy SQL** button, and it cannot be dismissed off the dashboard while it is missing (pending setup steps can be). The SQL the panel serves is a re-runnable variant of the migration: the repo file ends with a bare `CREATE POLICY`, and Postgres has no `CREATE POLICY IF NOT EXISTS`, so pasting the original twice errors — the panel's copy does `DROP POLICY IF EXISTS` first. Hamza: open /admin, tap Copy SQL on the top row, run it in Supabase, then tap Re-check.

(2) The /alerts form NEVER CREATED A SAVED SEARCH AT ALL — it only logged a lead + wrote localStorage, so its promised alerts could never fire; it now POSTs /api/alerts/subscribe with canonical filters (maxPrice→priceRange, minBeds→beds, neighbourhood→neighbourhoods, cashflow/brrr→strategy chips) and keeps the labeled lead capture as fire-and-forget. UNIT-VERIFIED round-trip (button payload / form payload / legacy keys / junk input → sanitize → DEFAULT_FILTERS merge → applyFilters on synthetic listings): each case matches exactly the intended listing, junk is clamped, empty input stores {}. - [ ] **AMPRE_VOW_TOKEN — the highest-value thing only Hamza can do (found 2026-07-27).** The feed serves NO days-on-market for ACTIVE listings: `DaysOnMarket`, `ListingId`, `YearBuilt`, `Latitude` and `Longitude` all come back null. Verified through `/api/listing-single`, which reads `DaysOnMarket` directly with no fallback and still returned 0 for W13238264 — a listing that had already taken a 2% price cut, so it demonstrably wasn't listed that day. The same feed DOES return real `DaysOnMarket` on Closed/Leased records (that's why `/api/rental-comps` shows genuine values like 17/41/15). **That active-vs-closed asymmetry is the classic IDX-vs-VOW field-suppression signature.** `app/api/listings/route.js` reads `process.env.AMPRE_VOW_TOKEN || process.env.AMPRE_TOKEN` — if only the IDX token is set in Vercel, that alone would explain every one of those nulls. Hamza said on 2026-07-26 that "IDX and VOW has been approved", so the VOW token may simply never have been added to the Vercel env. **What setting it would unlock, with zero further code changes:** real days-on-market (which restores the DOM sort, the Motivated-Seller and BRRR filters, and the New badge — all currently inert because the code now correctly refuses to fabricate), real year built, and real map pins (lat/lng are null today, so every pin is a deterministic offset from the postal-code FSA centroid). Agents: do NOT guess at this; the code already picks all of it up automatically the moment the feed supplies it.

## Run Log

(append one line per run: date — what changed — commit)

- 2026-08-04 — [improvement agent, Hamza-reported] Vercel alerted "Edge Requests spike 62x" (111/5min baseline -> 6.8k). Established from runtime logs that the spike was NOT function traffic — only ~500 invocations across the entire hour — so it was static/cached edge requests, i.e. a crawler or scraper sweeping the 5,277-URL sitemap; the edge-level breakdown and any rate-limiting live in the Vercel dashboard, which no code change can reach. The investigation did surface a real, fixable problem though: the site called ITSELF ~14,700 times/day on crons, ~46% of its entire ~32k/day baseline. /api/gta-screener ran 48x/day at ~233 paged sub-requests per run (~11,200/day) for per-city aggregates that barely change; /api/warm/photos ran 48x/day (~49/run) against a few dozen real sessions a day. Cut to 8/day and 12/day — internal requests 14,736 -> 3,652/day, a 35% cut in total baseline. Frequency only, no logic or figures touched. Stated plainly to Hamza that this lowers the bill but does not stop the crawler — (this commit)
- 2026-08-03 — [seo agent, Hamza-directed] Diagnosed /rental-property-insurance-mississauga's "position 57.5" before changing anything: it is largely an artifact of the page collecting ~90 generic national insurance queries at position 45-85 (unwinnable against national insurers), while its local terms sit at 11-39 and it ALREADY ranks position 1 for two question-shaped queries. Acted on that evidence rather than the average: added 4 FAQ entries (5 → 9, schema + visible copy) phrased as searchers type them, each targeting a measured cluster the page earned impressions for but never answered — "does home insurance cover a rental property" (largest cluster, ~15 variants), the tax write-off phrasing, condo landlord insurance (wholly uncovered and the most locally relevant gap), and what to ask a broker (already pos 1). No invented figures; broker/accountant hedges kept. Corrected my own error mid-task: I claimed the page lacked FAQ schema — it emits it via FAQJsonLd and my grep missed it. Verified in built HTML: one real FAQPage block (the second is the escaped RSC payload), 9 Questions, single h1, all new copy visible, 375px clean. Did NOT claim this lifts the generic national terms — that is domain authority, i.e. backlinks — (this commit)
- 2026-08-03 — [seo agent, Hamza dropped a new GSC export] Traffic is compounding (clicks 12→22→135/mo, impressions 1.7k→7.8k) but CTR is the bottleneck. Measured live title/description lengths on every high-impression page against Google's ~60/~155 render limits — ALL breached, worst being the 1,915-impression condo-vs-townhouse post whose 76-char title was cut at "…Which Is Better for Ca", hiding cash flow and 2026 entirely, at 0.47% CTR from position 8.4. Root cause was the auto-blog schema itself asking for 50-70 char titles and 180 char excerpts; now a hard MAX 60 / MAX 155 with front-loading and no-figures rules, so future posts are born compliant. Published posts live in Supabase and can't be edited from the repo, so added lib/blog/seo-overrides.js (slug → title/description) consumed by generateMetadata, with 29 tests enforcing the limits, banning figures, and asserting every slug matches a real measured page. Left the homepage alone — 58/157 is within noise. Logged /rental-property-insurance-mississauga still at position 57.5 despite the July canonical consolidation. Override is unit-verified only: no Supabase in the sandbox, so SERP confirmation waits on the next crawl — (this commit)
- 2026-08-03 — [improvement agent, Hamza-directed] Split the multi-unit chip by financing tier: Duplex (5% down owner-occupied) vs Triplex+ (10%, or commercial at 5+ units) — the distinction that changes what a buyer needs at closing, matching the reasoning that split condo townhouses. Fourplex/Multiplex ride with Triplex on purpose: zero active fourplexes and two multiplexes in Mississauga, so separate chips would sit empty. Kept Duplex/Multi as a chip-less URL alias selecting all four because the already-sent multi-unit letter links to it and describes all 11 listings — a shipped email is a permanent URL contract; the param parser now validates against PROPERTY_TYPE_MATCH rather than the chip row. Tests assert disjointness over the chip row, that the alias is not a chip, and that every chip has a rule — 42 checks; 212 across 7 suites; build green; 8 chips clean at 375px with all three URL forms resolving — (this commit)
- 2026-08-03 — [improvement agent, Hamza-directed] Split condo townhouses from freehold townhouses and hardcoded ONE property-type scheme. Root cause of this bug class was four verbatim copies of mapType (lib + three API routes); there is now one, over a closed PROPERTY_TYPE set, and filters match by equality against PROPERTY_TYPE_MATCH instead of substrings — buckets are now disjoint AND exhaustive, asserted as a test property. New "Condo Town" chip; MULTI_UNIT shared with the multi-unit email so site/email/feed cannot drift. Caught a trap before shipping: estimateRent's fallback tested /condo/ before /town/, so the rename would have silently moved condo townhouses from the 0.0035 town ratio to the 0.0038 apartment ratio and changed rent/cash flow/cap rate/score — town is now tested first. Proved it: re-mapped all 99 live listings and diffed 8 financial fields old vs new, 0 moved, while the split landed exactly 20 condo town + 1 freehold. 208 checks across 7 suites; build green; 375px clean — (this commit)
- 2026-08-03 — [improvement agent, Hamza-reported] "Why only 140 sent when I have 484?" — found the mass-send path losing most of every audience silently. All four broadcast routes fired TEN concurrent Resend calls per batch with zero pacing (far over the rate limit), ended `return res.ok` so every failure reason was discarded, and had no time budget — with the announcement route, the one campaign that actually reached inboxes, capped at maxDuration 60s, which cannot fit ~480 sends. New lib/emails/send-bulk.js: paced, honours Retry-After and halves the rate on 429, retries only 429s, records every failure reason, and stops at 80% of maxDuration returning the exact untouched remainder so nothing is double-sent. Announcement 60s → 300s; starting rate 8/s, env-overridable, self-tuning via backoff because Resend's docs 403 automated fetches and the real ceiling varies by plan. Sent page now names failures instead of reading as success. 28 tests on an injected clock; build green. NOT verified against Resend (no key/network in sandbox) — and the panel's "sent" counts webhook events, not dispatches, so Resend's dashboard is the tiebreaker on what the 140 really was — (this commit)
- 2026-08-03 — [improvement agent, Hamza-directed] Swept every filter for the class of bug behind the multi-unit complaint — 12 strategy chips, 6 property types, 12 sorts, run through the REAL processListings/applyFilters over 99 live production listings. Two more found and fixed: "Detached" returned all Semi-Detached rows too (substring bleed — 11 of 49 results, 22%, in the wrong bucket; now exact on mapped type, 38 returned which reconciles to the raw counts), and "LEGAL SUITE" matched only the literal phrase "legal basement" while the site's own detector recognises seven phrasings, so listings the card badges legal were invisible to the filter — now reads the same basementTier the card reads, and its tooltip stopped promising "or has potential" (a different tier, 22 of 99 rows). Verified honest and left alone: Town/Condo overlap (condo townhouses are genuinely both), cf=0 (matches production's cfPlusCount 6/2,422), all sorts preserve row count with no NaN. 16 tests; build green — (this commit)
- 2026-08-03 — [improvement agent, Hamza-reported] Fixed "the email is lying": /listings' Duplex/Multi filter showed "No properties match" while the multi-unit email said 11 — the EMAIL was right (production /api/listings?multiUnit=1 returns exactly 11). Three defects: the empty state consulted `isLoading` (false once SSR's 30 rows land) instead of `analysisComplete`, so it declared the market empty while ~24 pages were still streaming — and multi-unit is the worst-hit filter because the feed is ordered ModificationTimestamp desc and all 11 are 12–84 days stale, so they load last; the chip's hand-written `duplex||multi||triplex` test silently excluded Fourplex; and the email's "search duplex or triplex" CTA sent readers to an unfiltered page (and couldn't find the 2 multiplexes at all). Grid and table now show "Still scanning the market… N of M analyzed" until the set settles, the chip reuses MULTI_UNIT_TYPES from the email's own module so the two cannot drift, and the email links straight to /listings?type=Duplex%2FMulti. 10 new tests over the real production rows; build green; streaming race NOT replayable in sandbox (no feed) — (this commit)
- 2026-08-03 — [improvement agent, Hamza-directed] Restored the retention engine. Newsletter: Monday cron silently didn't fire Jul 20 + Jul 27 (zero invocations in logs; last issue Jul 13) — added a daily 15:30 UTC retry cron with a broadcast_sends week-key dedupe so a missed Monday self-heals in a day and a working one makes the retry a no-op. Alerts: the route served only saved_searches — yesterday it reached ONE person (Hamza's test account) while 483 leads got nothing; every lead is now a default-audience recipient under strict anti-spam rules (score>=8 only, max 6 listings, 3-day per-recipient cooldown, 120/run, fail-CLOSED without the dedupe table, honest copy that never claims a saved search). Also established from Gmail in:anywhere + logs: NO campaign has reached the list since Jul 23 — 8 drafts, 0 completed mass-sends; Hamza believed he'd sent. 23 unit tests; build green; production cron runs today are the end-to-end verification — (this commit)
- 2026-08-03 — [seo agent] — **Swept every sitemap URL for followed parameterized internal links and closed the class with a shared rule.** The 2026-07-30 audit scored internal links at 25%; its nofollow half was applied by hand and had drifted. Audited all 83 sitemap URLs in BUILT HTML (not source) and found 7 followed links to filtered views that the checker never saw: `/cash-flow-positive-properties-ontario` → `/listings?cf=1&sort=cashflow` ×3, `/market-pulse` → `/listings?sort=dom` ×2 (client component AND server layout), `/hurontario-lrt-real-estate` → `/listings?lrt=1`, `/pre-construction/projects` → `/pre-construction?project=…` ×3 — while the 24 `/neighbourhoods` hood links were correctly nofollowed, confirming the original fix held where applied. New `lib/seo/internal-link.js` states the rule once and `StickyMobileCTA` DERIVES rel from its href instead of trusting callers (two of its callers were offenders), so this cannot drift back the same way. 18 tests, incl. that real pages, `#fragments`, `tel:`/`mailto:` and protocol-relative `//host/?x` stay FOLLOWED — my first implementation nofollowed protocol-relative external URLs and the test caught it. VERIFIED on a running production build: 80 parameterized internal anchors sitewide, 0 still followed; four affected pages 200 and no overflow at 375px with rel live in the DOM; no regression elsewhere in the July 30 set (X-Powered-By absent, apple-touch-icon present, 83/83 pages with twitter card + og:image + canonical + exactly one h1). Logged two link-quality classes as NOT fixed because both need a design call: "investment guide"/"view listings" anchor text reused across 24 destinations each, and 10+ whole-card anchors running 100–184 characters. Build green with no env vars — (this commit)
- 2026-08-03 — [improvement agent] Phone screening caught up with the day's mandatory-phone change. `/api/lead` only screened junk numbers when `source === 'registration'`, so the two surfaces that now FORCE a number — the signup gate (`Sign Up` / `View Limit`) and Google Sign-In (`google-signin`) — were the two nothing checked, and forcing the field is precisely what produces `5555555555`. Also fixed a never-working WhatsApp link in every lead notification: `/\\D/g` in a template literal matches a literal `\D`, not non-digits, so the href was `https://wa.me/(647) 361-1234` — proved by execution, broken for every formatted number the site collects. Screening now runs on every source and DROPS a bad number instead of 400-ing, because the quiz, pre-construction VIP and sell-valuation forms all send an optional phone and rejecting would have discarded a complete lead over a bonus field; the three mandatory surfaces reject fakes client-side first so nobody loses the field silently. `lib/phone.js` now owns all four helpers (40 tests, incl. that a 555 LINE number like 647-361-5551 is real and must not be blocked). Exercised against the real route on a production build — 8 cases, every one as intended; build green with no env vars — (this commit)
- 2026-08-01 — [improvement agent] **Security: closed the rest of the OData-injection class found incidentally in the `/gta` run.** Audited all 11 files that talk to the AMPRE feed. Four more public routes interpolated request input straight into a `$filter`: `/api/listings?city=`, `/api/rental-comps?city=` (both the leased-comps and active-rentals queries) and `/api/photos?id=` / `/api/photos-all?id=`. A single quote closes our string literal, so the CALLER decided what the query matched and under which conditions — on `/api/listings` that meant dropping `StandardStatus eq 'Active'`, the $300k floor and the Commercial/Business exclusions, i.e. reading rows through the site's VOW token that it never intends to publish; on the rent routes it meant steering the comp set that the estimated-rent median (and therefore cap rate and cash flow) is computed from. The two photo routes were the worst of the set: their `$filter` and their `/Property('<id>')` path segment were never percent-encoded either, so an `&` in `?id=` appended whole query parameters to the upstream URL. One shared `lib/listings/odata.js` now owns both cases — `odataStr` (double the quote; for values inside an `encodeURIComponent`'d filter) and `odataKey` (double the quote, then percent-encode; for a URL path segment or a hand-built query string, and `encodeURIComponent` leaves `'` alone so the doubling survives). The five routes that already escaped correctly by hand (sold-comps, price-history, estimated-value, listing-single, photos-batch) were migrated onto the same helper, and `/api/listings-gta`'s local copy deleted, so there is exactly one escaper. Verified by hand on worked examples: legit input is byte-identical (`W12345678` → `W12345678`, `City eq 'Richmond Hill'` unchanged — so no behaviour or number moves), while `X' or ListingKey ne '` becomes the inert literal `'X'' or ListingKey ne '''` and `X&$top=500` encodes to `X%26%24top%3D500`. **Deliberately NOT "fixed":** every `parseInt`/`parseFloat` param (page, limit, beds, baths, minPrice, maxPrice, price, sqft, lat, lng) — a number has no quote to escape; the `typeMap` allow-lists in sold-comps/rental-comps/estimated-value; and the regex-gated `?fsa=`. Audit found no injection outside these routes: `lib/listings/ampre-fields.js` only forwards a filter its callers built, and no other file reaches the feed. Build green with no env vars. **Not verified:** this sandbox cannot reach the AMPRE feed, so the escaped queries were not executed against production — the reasoning is that escaping is a no-op on every legitimate value, which the worked examples above confirm character-for-character. — 576dd18
- 2026-08-01 — [improvement agent] Hamza: "majority of the areas show 0 listings". Measured all 28 `/gta/[city]` pages against production before changing anything: **8 returned `total: 0`** (Etobicoke, North York, Scarborough, East York, York, Stoney Creek, Dundas, Ancaster) against working controls (Toronto 9,695 · Brampton 2,047 · Hamilton 2,169 · Oakville 1,118 · King 276 · Clarington 437 — full table in section 2). Cause: the page passes its `CITY_COPY` key straight to `City eq '<name>'`, and the board never uses those eight names. `CityRegion` doesn't carry the Toronto district names either (`eq 'Etobicoke'`/`'North York'`/`'Scarborough'` = 0 rows feed-wide, against positive controls `'Mimico'` 9,146 and `'Islington-City Centre West'` 8,198), so the five Toronto districts are isolated by Canada Post FSA — the one field present on 100% of Toronto rows — and the three Hamilton communities by `startswith(CityRegion, …)`, which measured strictly better there than postal codes (L9H holds 174 non-Dundas rows). Now returning: Etobicoke 1,431 · North York 2,613 · Scarborough 1,697 · East York 321 · York 519 · Stoney Creek 231 · Ancaster 137 · Dundas 58. Rows keep their parent city, so no rent tier, tax rate, cash flow, cap rate or Deal Score moves — verified byte-identical on 352/352 listings against the pre-change `processListings`. One `CITY_SUBAREAS` in `lib/constants.js` drives the feed filter, the saved-search alert pool and the screener aggregate from the same predicate. Also escaped the `?city=` param into the OData filter (it was interpolated raw — a crafted value rewrote the query; that hole is what made the measurements above possible). 62 fixture assertions, 20 other cities + hub feed byte-identical, build green — (this commit)
- 2026-07-31 — [improvement agent] WRONG-NUMBER fix (Georgetown / Halton Hills), reported by Hamza. `/api/listings-gta` relabelled the board's `Halton Hills` as `Georgetown` in the response mapper, and the relabel ran BEFORE estimateRent — `CITY_RENT_TIER` has `'Halton Hills': 0.95` and had no `Georgetown` key, so every listing in that town took the `|| 0.90` unknown-city default and its base rent was understated by 5.3%, dragging cash flow, cap rate, cash-on-cash and Deal Score down with it. BEFORE/AFTER, hand-verified through the real route and the real processListings on a 3-bed detached at $999,000: rent $3,000 → $3,100/mo, cash flow −$3,514 → −$3,419, cap 1.30% → 1.42%, CoC −19.23% → −18.71%, score 1.8 → 1.8; property tax unchanged at $8,492/yr (getTaxRate already keyed both names at 0.85%). The same line also made /gta/halton-hills display all its listings as "Georgetown" and left /gta/georgetown querying a city name the board may not use (a possibly-empty page). The board's real label is STILL UNKNOWN — the sandbox cannot reach query.ampre.ca — so the fix is correct either way: relabel removed (rows keep the board's city), the request asks for BOTH names for either param, both names in GTA_CITIES, and `'Georgetown': 0.95` added to both rent-tier tables; one CITY_ALIAS_GROUPS in lib/constants.js drives it, and saved-search alert matching (poolForSearch) now follows the same aliases so a Georgetown-scoped alert can't silently go dead. Screener exclusion of the pair lifted — both pages now issue the identical request, so the aggregate over the merged group is provably the set each page loads. 36 fixture assertions incl. a 20-city before/after in which every other city is byte-identical; build green — b6a778d

- 2026-07-31 — [improvement agent] GTA per-city Deal Screener aggregates (Hamza approved the cost). The /gta hub and the 27 /gta/[city] pages showed skeletons for their whole background load because /api/market-stats — the source of /listings' first-paint summary — is Mississauga-scoped. Rather than 27 feed walks: new /api/gta-screener walks the GTA feed ONCE, groups the rows by city and applies the SAME computeScreenerMetrics per group plus once over everything, so one walk yields every covered city's aggregate and a GTA-wide one; /gta and /gta/[city] pass it as initialSummary exactly as /listings does (unfiltered only). Made affordable by a new nomedia=1 on /api/listings-gta (drops the Media $expand — the dominant per-request cost — with a byte-identical $select, so no published figure moves) and by fetching the walk's pages with a 2h revalidate under a 45s budget, so an out-of-time run leaves its prefix cached and the next cron run gets further; own endpoint + cron (13,43), never a page render, since /api/listings-gta already TimeoutErrors during /gta/[city] ISR. Publishes NOTHING unless the walk covered every page with zero drops at ≥95% fill (the feed is ordered by ModificationTimestamp, so a truncated walk is a biased sample of every city at once); Georgetown/Halton Hills and the 10 non-feed CITY_COPY cities are excluded rather than guessed. 25 fixture assertions through the real modules and real components (grouping ≡ per-city aggregation, thin/dropped/capped walks all withhold, city page with a summary renders the true figures vs skeletons without, filtered views still withhold, real page functions hand over the right aggregate); 375px clean. NOT MEASURED — the sandbox cannot reach AMPRE, so real walk duration and upstream cost are unknown; the design fails safe to today's skeletons — a39870a

- 2026-07-31 — [improvement agent] Deal Screener stopped publishing false headline numbers mid-load, and the background load got materially faster. EVIDENCE (two real mobile screenshots, one page load, seconds apart): at "30 of 2,493 analyzed" the tiles read CF+ DEALS 0 / BEST CAP 4.7% / BEST CASH FLOW -$278/mo / PRICE DROPS 3 / AVG SCORE 3.2; at "524 of 2,493 analyzed" the same tiles read 1 / 5.6% / +$41/mo / 107 / 3.5. New lib/listings/screener-metrics.js (one shared aggregate + a resolver that withholds every set-dependent figure until the set is settled — skeleton, never a number); explicit analysisComplete signal replacing the length-vs-total guess that filters broke; /api/market-stats now also computes the whole-market screener aggregate it already had the rows for, and /listings passes it into the dashboard so the correct market-wide figures are in the FIRST PAINT at no extra request. Loader rewritten from 5-wide waves to a 6-wide worker pool with per-page processing and one retry for dropped pages: same final list (verified), processListings row-passes 7,500 -> 2,500 on /listings and 104,900 -> 9,900 on the GTA hub, ~29% faster in a latency simulation, and a dropped page is no longer lost for the session. Complete-set markup byte-identical to before — e65bb13

- 2026-07-23 — [improvement agent] WRONG-NUMBER fix (GTA tax): with the GTA rollout now prominent, traced the GTA feed → processListings → getTaxRate(hood) path. Neither feed passes the real MLS tax, so cash flow uses price×getTaxRate(city). Cities in TAX_RATES were correct, but the AMALGAMATED DISTRICTS weren't in the table and fell to DEFAULT (0.95%): Hamilton's communities (Stoney Creek/Dundas/Ancaster) were UNDER-taxed 0.95% vs 1.26% — overstating cash flow by ~$207/mo on an $800k home (the dangerous direction); Toronto's former boroughs (Etobicoke/North York/Scarborough/East York/York) and Georgetown (Halton Hills) could miss their parent rate too. Added all as aliases pointing to their parent municipality's rate (a legal fact post-amalgamation, not an estimate). Hand-verified getTaxRate for all 13 cases incl. unchanged Mississauga/Toronto/Brampton + unknown→default; build green. NEEDS HAMZA (minor): King Township isn't in TAX_RATES (separate municipality) so it uses the 0.95% default — provide its rate to refine. Bigger picture: neither the Mississauga nor GTA feed selects TaxAnnualAmount, so ALL listings use estimated (rate×price) tax rather than the real MLS tax — worth wiring the real field through for exactness (larger change).
- 2026-07-23 — [improvement agent] WRONG-NUMBER fix (GTA rent): processListings never read the feed-provided rent (no l.rent/l.estimatedRent reference), so the GTA feed's CITY-TIERED rent estimate (Toronto ×1.15 … Oshawa ×0.80) was discarded and rent was recomputed with a generic bedroom+price formula — over-renting cheaper cities and under-renting pricier ones, skewing every GTA listing's cash flow / cap rate / score. Made the non-Mississauga single-family (no-suite) rent path prefer l.estimatedRent when present. Mississauga is untouched: its hoods are in HOOD_RENTS so it takes the earlier branch and never reaches this code. Hand-verified the branch arithmetic: Toronto 2bd/$700k → $2,550 (city-tiered) vs old generic $2,300; Oshawa 2bd/$500k → $1,800 vs old OVERSTATED $2,100; no feed rent → generic fallback unchanged. Build green. NOTE: suite + multi-unit GTA paths still use their own estimates (they add basement/per-unit income) — a further refinement, flagged.
- 2026-07-23 — [improvement agent] CONSISTENCY fix (GTA rent, card vs detail): the earlier GTA-rent commit made processListings prefer the feed's city-tiered estimatedRent — but /api/listing-single (the listing-DETAIL fetch) returns estimatedRent:0, so a Toronto listing showed city-tiered rent on the /gta CARD yet the generic estimate on its DETAIL page → different cash flow for the same listing (a trust-killer I'd introduced). Centralised the city tier: added CITY_RENT_TIER + getCityRentTier() to constants (mirrors the GTA feed) and applied it inside processListings' fallback (both suite + no-suite), which is the ONE function both the card and detail flow through. Now the detail page (feed rent absent) computes the SAME tiered rent the card showed. Hand-verified card==detail for Toronto ($2,550), Oshawa ($1,800), Vaughan ($2,850); Mississauga tier = 1.0 (unchanged, and it uses HOOD_RENTS anyway); getCityRentTier spot-checked (Toronto 1.15 / Oshawa 0.80 / Hamilton 0.85 / Cooksville 1.0 / unknown-GTA 0.90). Build green (89 pages).

- 2026-07-23 — [seo agent] robots.txt group-override bug fixed: per the robots spec a crawler obeys ONLY its most specific matching group, so the file's Googlebot and Bingbot groups (each just "Allow: / + Disallow: /api/,/admin/") were silently CANCELLING the * group's disallows (/_next/, /admin.html, /profile/, /saved/, /recent/, /login, /signup) for exactly the two bots that matter. Rewrote to a single * group with infrastructure disallows only (/api/, /admin/, /admin.html, /_next/); the five user/auth routes are excluded via their `robots: noindex` metadata instead of a crawl block (Google can only see noindex on pages it may crawl — the repo's own stated approach, and all five verified emitting "noindex, nofollow" in SSR: login/signup via the (auth) layout, profile/saved/recent via their layouts). robots.txt serves 200 with one consistent group for every bot; build green (89 pages). Mississauga: 567 sales, avg $1,014,120 (was $963,747), median $880,000, 1,632 new listings, 2,589 active, SNLR 35.1%, 4.9 months inventory, 97% SP/LP, 29 LDOM, 47 PDOM. GTA: 6,770 sales (+9.4%), avg $1,058,658 (-3.9%), 17,282 new (-12.9%), 27,329 active (-13.5%), 29 LDOM, 42 PDOM. Peel: 1,167 sales, avg $966,024. Added `tRREBFreshness()` which computes months-behind from tRREBAsOf and exposes `tRREBMonthsBehind`, `tRREBIsStale` (>= 2 months, since a one-month lag is normal) and a `tRREBRefreshNote` reading "Need new market data…". The admin dashboard now shows an amber banner with a link to TRREB Market Watch the moment it's overdue. Unit-verified: June data in July = 1 behind/not stale; the old Feb data would have flagged 5 months behind. - 2026-07-25 — [correctness] RESOLVED the flagged "4.89% vs 6.09% rate inconsistency" — and the earlier framing of it was WRONG, so correcting the record: the two numbers measure different things. TRREB reprints the Bank of Canada CONVENTIONAL MORTGAGE series, which are POSTED rates; the engine's 4.89% is a discounted CONTRACT rate. With prime at 4.5%, nobody signs a 6.09% five-year fixed, so 4.89% is defensible and the deals are NOT "all overstated" as previously claimed. Do not raise the engine rate to the posted rate.
  The REAL bug underneath: `stressTest` was hardcoded 8.09 (posted 6.09 + 2). The federal test is the greater of CONTRACT rate + 2% or 5.25%, so it keys off the contract rate — correct value is max(4.89+2, 5.25) = 6.89%. It's now derived from DEFAULT_ASSUMPTIONS.annualInterestRate instead of hardcoded, so it can never drift from the engine again. That wrong 8.09% was being fed verbatim into every AI listing analysis and every generated blog post, telling investors they had to qualify ~1.2 points higher than reality — i.e. it made deals look UNREACHABLE, the opposite of the earlier claim. (The /mortgage-calculator was always right: it computes max(rate+2, 5.25) from the user's own rate.)
  Also fixed the presentation, which was the actual user-facing inconsistency: /market-pulse listed posted fixed rates bare next to a variable CONTRACT rate, so a 5-year fixed appeared to cost 1.6 points more than variable, under a header claiming "approximate rates from major Canadian lenders" (they're BoC benchmarks, not lender quotes). Now: each posted rate carries a "posted" tag, the intro names the real source, and the footnote states plainly that posted is not the rate you'll be offered and discloses the ~4.89% contract assumption behind the cash-flow figures. The analysis prompt and blog data block now separate POSTED from CONTRACT rates and forbid quoting a posted rate as the investor's borrowing cost. No calculation changed — build green (89 pages), stressTest verified 6.89 live — (this commit)
- 2026-07-25 — [correctness — Hamza: "pull it from Google"] The "5.0 on Google · 28 reviews" claim was HARDCODED in 11 places (homepage trust chips + agent profile + reviews header, header trust bar, /about, /sell x2, /auth layout, blog post CTA + author box, signup-gate modal, email-capture, announcement email). Nothing verified it and nothing updated it — the same silent-drift failure as the TRREB data, except this one is a public claim about our own reputation, which is worse. Replaced all of them with a single live source: lib/google-rating.js calls the Places API (New) place-details endpoint with a rating,userRatingCount field mask (cheapest SKU), 6h revalidate; /api/google-rating exposes it to client components so the key never reaches the browser. Server components (homepage, /about, /sell, /auth, blog post) call the lib directly; client components (header, signup gate) use the route. DELIBERATE DESIGN: there is NO hardcoded fallback rating anywhere. Missing env vars, an API error, a malformed response, or a profile with zero reviews all yield null, and every consumer HIDES the claim rather than showing an unverified number. Verified: build passes with no env vars, and fetchGoogleRating() returns null when unconfigured or partially configured; label formatting handles singular ("1 review") and rounds to one decimal.
  NEEDS HAMZA — this is NOT deployed yet, on purpose: shipping before the credentials exist would make the Google rating vanish from the hero and cost real social proof. Add to Vercel env: GOOGLE_PLACES_API_KEY (Google Cloud → enable "Places API (New)" → create key → restrict to Places API) and GOOGLE_PLACE_ID (the Business Profile place ID). Both documented in .env.example. The moment they're set, the rating goes live and self-updates every 6 hours — (this commit)
- 2026-07-25 — [correctness — Hamza: "any blog any email that gets sent is updated with every stat and based on data"] Audited every generated-content path and removed the last places market numbers were frozen in code. (1) lib/analysis-prompt.js — which drives EVERY per-listing AI analysis — had the whole market context hardcoded. It now BUILDS ITSELF from live /api/market-stats at request time: avg/median price, SNLR, inventory, sale-to-list, DOM, market type, the Feb→Jun direction-of-travel line from mississaugaMonthly, posted-vs-contract rates, stress test, sold benchmarks by type with GTA-labelled YoY, and asking rents. No figure is hardcoded; if the fetch fails the market block is REPLACED with an explicit "unavailable — do not quote market-wide numbers" instruction rather than reciting stale figures. (2) getPromptHash() now hashes the rendered prompt, so a data refresh automatically invalidates every cached analysis — verified: June and July payloads produce different hashes, identical payloads produce identical ones. Combined with the earlier fix (it used to hash only the first 100 chars of a static string) this closes the path where cached analyses could quote months-old prices forever. (3) The weekly-newsletter ?preview=1 route was rendering from a hardcoded sample stat block — a preview built on frozen numbers can't verify the one thing that matters, so it now renders from the real live stats. - 2026-07-25 — [improvement agent] BROKEN NUMBERS, worst-bug class: none of the homepage's "Live MLS data" stats were actually live. market-stats' fetchLiveListingStats() read RAW MLS field names (ListPrice, DaysOnMarket, PropertyType/PropertySubType) but /api/listings returns MAPPED listings (price, dom, type, subType) — so every filter matched zero rows and each stat silently fell through to its fallback. Production proved it: avgDOM served the hardcoded 28, avgPrice served the TRREB monthly figure, and every avgPrices.*.count came back 0 — all of it labelled "Live MLS data · PropTx" on the homepage trust chip. Now reads mapped names with the raw names kept as a secondary fallback, so it works whichever shape it's handed. SECOND bug found while testing the fix: the per-type buckets used independent substring filters, and "Semi-Detached" contains "Detached" while "Condo Townhouse" contains "Townhouse" — semis were being counted into the detached average and condo-towns into freehold towns, inflating both. Replaced with a single-assignment classifier that checks most-specific first (semi → condo → town → detached); verified each listing now lands in exactly one bucket. Hand-verified against a synthetic 5-listing set: avgPrice 837,500 and avgDOM 25 computed correctly from mapped rows, raw-shape rows still work, no double counting. Build green — (this commit)

- 2026-07-25 — [seo agent] Made sitemap `lastmod` honest. The sitemap regenerates every 6 hours and stamped `lastModified: now` on ALL ~80 static URLs (28 static pages + 24 neighbourhood guides), telling Google that every guide, legal page and landing page changed twice a day. Google treats a consistently unreliable lastmod as grounds to ignore lastmod for the entire site — which would have discarded the accurate per-listing and per-post dates too, the ones that actually drive efficient recrawl. Now: `now` only on the 7 genuinely feed-driven URLs (/, /listings, /gta, /recent-sales, /news, /blog, /pre-construction/projects) where it's true; neighbourhood guides carry HOOD_OUTLOOK_AS_OF (their real as-of date); static editorial/legal pages carry NO lastmod (explicitly fine per Google — omitting beats lying); blog posts use updated_at||created_at with no fake fallback. ALSO found the listing pages never had a real date at all: ModificationTimestamp is selected from the AMPRE feed but was dropped during mapping in /api/listings, so the sitemap always fell back to `now` for every listing — now exposed as `modificationTimestamp` and consumed, giving ~2,500 listing URLs genuine per-listing lastmod. Caught a latent trap while testing the month parser: `new Date('not-a-month 1 UTC')` does NOT return Invalid Date in V8, it coerces to the year 2001 — so a typo in HOOD_OUTLOOK_AS_OF would have silently published a 25-year-old lastmod on all 24 guides; replaced with explicit month-name + 4-digit-year validation, verified against 12 edge cases. Build green — (this commit)

- [x] **1. Brokerage string + build check** — the sanitizer already removes the old brokerage name at render AND permanently via the auto-blog cron (shipped Jul 25, verified 0 live occurrences). What was MISSING is what the advisor actually asked for: a gate. New `scripts/compliance-check.mjs` runs as the first step of `npm run build` and FAILS the build on the old brokerage name (any spacing/casing, and on "Signature Realty" alone) or a U+FFFD mojibake character, anywhere in app/, components/, lib/, public/ or content files. Negative-tested: planting the string in a component exits 1, removing it exits 0. Internal docs that describe the incident (this backlog, CLAUDE.md) and the sanitizer itself are allowlisted, since they must contain the string to do their job and are never published.
- [x] **2. IDX vs VOW field table for the broker of record** — RESOLVED 2026-07-26. Investigating this surfaced a live compliance gap, not just a document to write: THREE fully public pages (`/recent-sales`, `/neighbourhoods`, `/market-pulse`) called `/api/sold-comps` with zero authentication and rendered individual sold records — full street address, exact sold price, DOM, close date — to anonymous visitors, while the IDENTICAL data was already correctly gated behind email capture on the listing-detail "Sold Comps" tab. `/recent-sales`'s own on-page FAQ copy even states the VOW rule being violated ("released only through a REALTOR® or a Virtual Office Website... may only be used by people with a bona fide interest, not for general commercial purposes"). Hamza confirmed directly: his VOW agreement requires visitor registration before this data is shown. FIXED: extracted the listing-detail page's existing AuthGate into a shared `components/ui/auth-gate.js` and applied it to all three pages — the `/api/sold-comps` fetch is now skipped entirely while gated (not just hidden with CSS), and unlocks inline via the same email-capture pattern used everywhere else, turning the compliance fix into another lead-capture surface rather than data just vanishing. `/api/market-stats` and `/api/neighbourhood-stats` (aggregate, non-individual-record data) were left ungated — VOW restrictions apply to individual sold records, not published aggregate figures. VERIFIED via headless-Chromium CDP with network-request logging (no AMPRE token in this sandbox, so this deliberately tests the code path, not real data): as an anonymous visitor, 0 requests to `/api/sold-comps` fire on all three pages and 0 `<table>` elements render — only the email-gate form; after setting `user_registered=true` in localStorage and reloading, exactly 1 request fires with the correct query params on each page. Build green, 5 files changed (1 new). NOT DONE (can't be, from here): the actual IDX-eligible vs VOW-only field list per TRREB's specific DDF/VOW agreement — that's real, board-specific legal text this environment has no access to; if Hamza has the signed agreement, a follow-up run can cross-check the current field set (address/price/beds/baths/photos public, sold price/date/brokerage-remarks gated) against it directly.

  - MEASURED 2026-07-26 (seo), replacing last run's guess: the live GTA feed carries **24,551** active listings (`/api/listings-gta` `total`, fetched against production). The sitemap was submitting 1,000 of them — ~96% of GTA listing detail pages were never offered for discovery. Live sitemap audited the same run: 3,733 URLs, of which 3,533 are listing pages (2,547 Mississauga + ~1,000 GTA). PARTIAL FIX SHIPPED: both feeds now share one `LISTING_PAGE_BUDGET` (15 pages x 200), so GTA goes 1,000 -> 3,000 and is no longer arbitrarily given a third of the Mississauga budget; the feed is ordered by ModificationTimestamp desc so the slice submitted is the most recently updated. STILL OPEN and NOT a matter of raising the number: full coverage needs ~123 upstream calls in one function invocation, which would time out and leave Google with NO sitemap. That requires a **sitemap index** with child sitemaps (static / hoods / Mississauga listings / GTA listings / blog), which also gives each type its own recrawl cadence. Deliberately not attempted in an autonomous hourly pass: Next's generateSitemaps changes the URL structure to /sitemap/{id}.xml, needs a Search Console resubmit, and cannot be verified here against the real feed — a botched attempt takes out discovery for every page on the site. NOTE also worth weighing in that dedicated run: 24,551 near-duplicate MLS pages on a site currently earning ~8.9K impressions/3mo is a crawl-budget question, not just a coverage one; prioritising is probably better than submitting everything.
  - FIXED 2026-07-26 (seo): the live sitemap contained 26 DUPLICATE /listings/{id} entries, all from the Mississauga branch — its pages are fetched in parallel from a feed sorted by ModificationTimestamp desc, so a listing whose timestamp changes mid-fetch shifts position and lands on two pages. Inherent to paginating a live-sorted feed, so deduped at the point of output instead. Verified with a stub that re-emits a page-1 row on page 2: 0 duplicates out.
  - VERIFIED CLEAN 2026-07-26 (seo): the 28 /gta?city= URLs are submitted with query params, so their canonical was worth checking — /gta?city=Toronto self-canonicalises, carries a unique title and h1, correct og:url and robots index,follow. No sitemap/canonical conflict. Recording as checked rather than assumed.
  - (superseded) REOPENED PARTIALLY 2026-07-26 (seo): coverage is complete by *page type* but capped by *volume* on one type. The GTA-listing branch fetches at most 5 pages x 200 = **1,000** listing URLs (Mississauga gets 15 x 200 = 3,000). The GTA feed spans 17 cities plus every Toronto sub-area, so the live count is very likely well above 1,000 — which would mean a large share of the now-server-rendered GTA listing detail pages are never submitted for discovery, i.e. the SSR work paid for pages Google is not told about. UNVERIFIED on purpose: no AMPRE token in the agent sandbox, so the real total is unknown and I am not guessing it. TO CHECK: fetch `/api/listings-gta?limit=1&page=1` on production and read `total`. If it materially exceeds 1,000 the fix is NOT simply raising the cap — this single route already makes up to 20 upstream fetches per regeneration and would start risking a function timeout, which fails the sitemap entirely. The right shape is a **sitemap index** with child sitemaps (static / hoods / Mississauga listings / GTA listings / blog), which also gets each type its own lastmod and recrawl cadence. Dedicated run.

- 2026-07-26 — [improvement agent — Hamza: "run the rent model check against production"] Ran it. The check itself surfaced a bug that made per-neighbourhood calibration impossible: /api/rental-comps ACCEPTED lat/lng but never used them — fetchLeaseComps filters on `City eq 'Mississauga'` and BedroomsTotal ±1 only — so every "comp" came from anywhere in the city while the response was labelled "similar leases NEARBY". Investors read that as neighbourhood-level evidence on listing pages; it was city-wide. Fixed: new `fsa` param applies `startswith(PostalCode, 'L4T')`-style scoping, PostalCode is selected and returned per comp, and the labels now say "in this area" only when actually scoped, otherwise "across Mississauga". verify-rent-model.mjs now scopes each row by the hood's FSA (via FSA_TO_HOOD) instead of sending lat/lng the API ignored. PRODUCTION EVIDENCE gathered with the deployed (pre-fix) endpoint, on the Malton claim specifically: city-wide DETACHED 3-5 bed leased median = $3,375 over 20 comps in 12 months, and that sample includes exactly one Malton listing — 3428 Monica Drive, L4T, 3-bed detached, leased at $2,800 (DOM 50). Our model says Malton 3-bed detached $3,300 and 4-bed detached $3,900. So the model sits ~16% ABOVE the city-wide detached median for the city's CHEAPEST neighbourhood (Malton avg price $618K vs city ~$1.01M), and ~18% above the one real Malton comp. That is three independent signals — the advisor's $2,800-3,500 read, the city-wide median, and the L4T comp — all pointing the same way: the Malton rents are too high. STILL NOT AUTO-CHANGED: HOOD_RENTS drives cash flow, cap rate, CoC and deal score on every listing, and a full recalibration needs the per-hood run, which requires this FSA fix to be deployed first. NEXT: once this ships, `node scripts/verify-rent-model.mjs` returns a real per-hood/per-bed table and I recalibrate in one evidenced pass — (this commit)

He was right, and it was TWO fallbacks stacking. (1) /api/market-stats computed the top-level `avgPrice` — an average of ACTIVE LIST prices — but fell back to `tRREBSold.all.avgPrice` when the feed returned nothing, i.e. published a SOLD average under a list-price key. (2) The homepage stats bar then computed `avgSoldPrice = data.avgPrices?.all?.soldAvg || data.avgPrice`, falling back to that same list-price field for the SOLD tile. With the feed down both paths landed on the identical TRREB June figure ($1,014,120 = $1.01M), so the bar printed one number under two contradictory labels next to a 97% sale-to-list ratio — three figures that cannot all be true. Both fallbacks removed: a list-price average now comes only from live listings and a sold average only from sold data; either missing means the tile is OMITTED, which is the omit-never-fake rule the rest of the bar already followed. DELIBERATELY LEFT: `avgPrices.all.avg` keeps its TRREB fallback, because the only consumer is the market-pulse chart whose caption explicitly states "Live list prices where available, otherwise TRREB sold averages" — disclosed, not disguised. Unit-verified all three states (feed down / both real / no sold data): no case can now print the same figure under both labels. - 2026-07-26 — [ops — Hamza: "do all 3", item 3 of 3] **Email delivery is now verifiable from inside the product.** "Are my emails sending?" has been an open question for days at a time, because the only evidence lived in Vercel's cron log and the Resend dashboard — neither of which the app can see and neither of which anyone was checking. New `/api/admin/email-health` answers it from history the app ALREADY writes: `alert_sent_listings` rows are recorded only after Resend accepts a send (a rejected send stays eligible and records nothing), so a recent row is proof of a real delivery rather than an inference. No new table and no migration — deliberately, because a migration would have handed the dependency straight back to Hamza. Reports deliveries in the last 24h/7d, last delivery timestamp, alert subscriber count, last weekly newsletter, and whether RESEND_API_KEY/CRON_SECRET are set (booleans and the public from-address only — the key is never returned). Status is deliberately conservative about what the data can PROVE: `critical` when subscribers exist but nothing was ever delivered, but only `warn` at 48h+ with an explicit note that a run where no subscriber had a new match correctly sends nothing and records nothing — the one thing this evidence cannot distinguish, so it says so instead of crying wolf. Surfaced as a colour-coded dashboard panel beside the existing stale-market-data and Google-rating banners. Build green — (this commit)
- 2026-07-26 — [security] **Three privileged endpoints authorised ANYONE when their secret was missing from the env.** Found while working the "verify auth protects every admin route" item — the admin/* routes were all fine; the cron ones were not. (1) `/api/alerts/send` and `/api/seo/ping` used `if (cronSecret && authHeader !== ...)`, so with CRON_SECRET unset the condition short-circuits and NO check runs. alerts/send is invoked by Vercel Cron with GET, so that left a mass email to every subscriber open to anyone with the URL, repeatable at will — spam, sender-reputation and Gmail/Yahoo bulk-sender damage, and billable on every send. (2) `/api/auto-blog` and `/api/newsletter/weekly` compared the header against `Bearer ${process.env.CRON_SECRET}`, which renders as the literal string "Bearer undefined" when unset — so sending exactly that header authenticated you. (3) `approvalToken()` returned the literal `'dev'` with no CRON_SECRET, so `?approve=1&t=dev` could approve and mail the ENTIRE subscriber list. Fixed by centralising the guards in `lib/api-auth.js`, where a missing secret is a misconfiguration to REFUSE (503), never a door to open. The token now returns null instead of a guessable constant — and note the subtlety: an absent `?t` is ALSO null, so `searchParams.get('t') !== approvalToken(wk)` would have passed; both compare sites check for a real token explicitly. VERIFIED by probing every privileged route in both configurations — no secrets: alerts/send 503, seo/ping 503, auto-blog 401, weekly 401, all admin 401, with "Bearer undefined" and "x-admin-key: undefined" rejected everywhere and ?t=dev rejected; secrets set: unauthenticated 401, wrong credential 401, correct credential passes the guard (alerts/send reaches its no-Supabase 503, email-health 200), ?t=dev and absent ?t both rejected. Whether CRON_SECRET is actually set in production is now visible to Hamza — yesterday's email-health panel reports `cronSecretSet`. Build green — (this commit)
- 2026-07-26 — [data — Hamza sent his live MailerLite export: "add their phone numbers in too"] **The subscriber importer would have imported ZERO from that file, and had never imported a single phone number.** Two defects, both found by running the parser against the real 477-row export rather than assuming. (1) The export is TAB-separated; `parseCsvLine` only split on commas, so every row collapsed into one field, the email column was never located, and the route answered "No contacts found in payload" — a real 477-contact list would have imported nothing, with a message that reads like the file was empty. The delimiter is now detected from the header row (tab/comma/semicolon). (2) `phone: ''` was HARDCODED on insert, so every phone number in every export ever uploaded through this route was silently discarded. Phones now come from any phone-ish column — "Phone", "other phone #", "work phone #", mobile, cell — preferring the primary and falling through to the extras, because exports routinely leave the main blank and fill a secondary (in this file: 206 primary, 89 other, 33 work → 258 people with at least one). Each is normalised to (XXX) XXX-XXXX and validated with the same fake-number rules as /api/lead. Existing contacts are still never overwritten and unsubscribes are never resurrected, but a blank phone is a GAP not a decision, so a re-upload now BACKFILLS missing phones and names onto contacts already in the database — which is the only thing that makes re-importing useful once the ESP has collected numbers. Admin result line now reports phones imported vs backfilled. VERIFIED against the real file: delimiter detected as tab, 477/477 rows parsed with valid emails, 464 names, 258 phones all normalised to one `(###) ###-####` shape, 477 unique emails — the 258 matches an independent count of the source. Build green. NOTE: this environment has no Supabase credentials, so the import itself has NOT been run — Hamza uploads the same file at /admin and the counts above are what he should see — (this commit)
- 2026-07-26 — [data — Hamza: "fix it i need phone numbers on admin page"] Hardened the subscriber import so it survives his actual 477-row list. The admin leads page ALREADY displays phone (list row, detail panel, tel: link, WhatsApp link, CSV export) — the gap was never the UI, it was that no phone number had ever reached the database. Two defects in the phone support added earlier today, both fatal at real list size: (1) the existence lookup passed all 477 addresses to ONE `.in()` filter — PostgREST puts `in` in the query string, so that is a ~12KB URL, past the server limit; the lookup would have failed, the entire list would have looked new, and the insert would then have collided with rows already present. Chunked at 100. (2) The backfill did one UPDATE per row sequentially — 258 round trips would have run past the function timeout and left the import half-applied; now batched 25 at a time, only for rows that actually need a change (a re-upload of a complete list costs ZERO writes), with `maxDuration = 60`. VERIFIED by running the REAL POST handler against the REAL export with the database stubbed (stub models the PostgREST URL limit, so the un-chunked version genuinely fails against it): empty db → 477 imported / 258 phones / 5 chunked selects, max in-size 100 / 3 inserts; re-upload of the same file → 0 imported, 0 writes, database byte-identical; phones blanked (HAMZA'S ACTUAL SITUATION — contacts already imported by the old code that discarded phones) → 258 backfilled, 0 duplicates. Build green. Still cannot run the import from here (no Supabase credentials) — Hamza uploads at /admin — (this commit)

- 2026-07-26 — [seo agent] Unblocked Googlebot from the site's CSS, JS and fonts. `public/robots.txt` carried a bare `Disallow: /_next/`, and every stylesheet, script chunk and font Next.js emits is served from `/_next/static/` — so the crawler was blocked from every render-critical asset on every page. Google renders a page in order to index it and states plainly that disallowing CSS/JS "directly harms how well our algorithms render and index your content"; the practical effect is that Googlebot was rendering and assessing this site UNSTYLED. That was cheap when the money pages were empty client shells, but this week's SSR work put real content into ~2,600 pages, and this line was quietly discarding much of that win — the rendered-page signal, mobile-friendliness assessment, and any layout-derived judgement all came from a page with no CSS. Fixed by adding `Allow: /_next/static/` (plus `/_next/image` so optimized images stay eligible for Google Images as the open next/image migration lands); per Google's spec the most specific matching rule wins, so the longer Allow beats the shorter Disallow while build internals stay blocked. VERIFIED with a matcher implementing Google's actual precedence rules (longest match wins, Allow wins ties), run against the robots.txt SERVED BY A PRODUCTION BUILD and asserted on real asset URLs pulled from that same build's HTML: /_next/static/css/5df62b512aa49525.css and the main-app chunk are now ALLOW, while /_next/server/*, /_next/BUILD_ID, /admin/leads, /admin.html and /api/* remain DISALLOW — 15/15 assertions pass, and the same suite failed 3/3 on the asset URLs before the change, which is what confirmed the bug rather than assuming it. SECOND FIX, same theme (crawl directives that were lying to Google): the sitemap's GTA-listing branch stamped `lastModified: now` on up to 1,000 URLs every 6 hours. The file's own comment on the static pages explains exactly why that is harmful — Google responds to consistently unreliable lastmod by IGNORING lastmod for the whole site, which would throw away the accurate per-listing and per-post dates that actually drive recrawl — but the GTA branch, added later, reintroduced the bug it warns about. Root cause was upstream: /api/listings-gta $selects ModificationTimestamp in all three of its fallback selects but never returned it, so the sitemap had no real date to use. Added the passthrough (4 additive lines, no logic reads it) and the sitemap now uses it conditionally, exactly like the Mississauga branch. Verified against stubbed feeds on a dev render: a GTA listing with a timestamp emits `<lastmod>2026-07-24T10:11:12Z</lastmod>`, one without emits NO lastmod (previously both got a fabricated `now`), the Mississauga control is unchanged, and static editorial pages still carry none. Stubs reverted; `git diff` confirms the only API change is the 4-line passthrough and zero changes to /api/listings. Build green. NOT FIXED, logged for a dedicated run: the sitemap caps GTA listings at 5 pages x 200 = 1,000 URLs (Mississauga gets 15 x 200). Across 17 GTA cities plus all Toronto sub-areas the live feed is very likely well above that, meaning a large share of the now-server-rendered GTA listing pages are never submitted for discovery — but I have no AMPRE token here, so I am recording it as unverified rather than guessing a number. The real fix is probably a sitemap index with child sitemaps rather than raising the cap on one monolithic route that already makes up to 20 upstream fetches — (this commit)

- 2026-07-26 — [seo agent] Resolved last run's open question with a real measurement instead of a guess, then fixed what it exposed. Production says the GTA feed carries **24,551** active listings; the sitemap was submitting 1,000, so ~96% of GTA listing detail pages were never offered for discovery — the SSR work paid for pages Google was never told about. Audited the LIVE sitemap the same run: 3,733 URLs (3,533 listing pages = 2,547 Mississauga + ~1,000 GTA), which also confirms the advisor's "2,547" figure was Mississauga only. TWO FIXES, both verified against stubs that reproduce the real feed's behaviour. (1) DUPLICATES: the live sitemap shipped 26 duplicated /listings/{id} entries, every one from the Mississauga branch — its 15 pages are fetched in PARALLEL from a feed ordered by ModificationTimestamp desc, so a listing whose timestamp changes mid-fetch shifts position and lands on two pages at once. That is inherent to paginating a live-sorted feed and will recur, so it is deduped at the point of output rather than by trying to freeze pagination; first occurrence wins, which keeps the higher-priority Mississauga entry if a URL ever appears in both feeds. Verified with a stub that deliberately re-emits a page-1 row on page 2: it appears exactly ONCE in the output, 0 duplicates total. (2) COVERAGE: the GTA branch was capped at 5 pages while Mississauga got 15 — an arbitrary third, not a decision. Both now share one `LISTING_PAGE_BUDGET`, taking GTA from 1,000 to 3,000 URLs; verified both feeds clamp at page 15 against stubs advertising 20 and 123 pages respectively. Deliberately did NOT chase full coverage: 24,551 rows is ~123 upstream calls in one function invocation, which would time out and leave Google with no sitemap at all — that needs a sitemap index, logged with the reasoning (including the crawl-budget argument that submitting 24.5K near-duplicate MLS pages may not even be desirable for a site earning ~8.9K impressions/3mo). ALSO CONFIRMED LIVE this run: the robots.txt fix from the previous run is deployed and Googlebot can now fetch the site's CSS/JS; the modificationTimestamp passthrough is live in the API; and the 28 /gta?city= pages self-canonicalise correctly with unique titles/h1s and index,follow — checked rather than assumed, since submitting query-param URLs whose canonical points elsewhere would have wasted the per-city content work. ONE THING I COULD NOT FULLY EXPLAIN and am recording honestly rather than glossing: 986 GTA entries in the live sitemap carry no lastmod even though the API now returns modificationTimestamp. Most likely the sitemap's own `next: { revalidate: 3600 }` fetch cache still held pre-deploy payloads at audit time, which self-heals within the hour — and either way "no lastmod" is strictly better than the fabricated `now` it used to emit. Worth re-checking on the next run. Stubs reverted; git confirms 0 changes to any API route. Build green — (this commit)

- 2026-07-26 — [rent model] RECALIBRATION DELIBERATELY NOT DONE, and the reason is the finding. Ran the check against the now-live FSA endpoint and discovered the medians it returns CANNOT calibrate this model: /api/rental-comps mixed basement, upper and lower half-house leases in with whole-property leases, while HOOD_RENTS feeds estimateRent(), which is a WHOLE-property figure (basement income is added separately by getBasementIncome). Proof from production: Cooksville (L5A) detached reported a $3,200 median while the whole-house leases inside that same response were $4,200 / $4,400 / $5,750 / $6,500, dragged down by a "Lower Unit" at $1,900, a "BSMT" at $1,700 and an "F2" at $2,200. Had I done the blanket recalibration as planned, I would have cut most hoods 20-30% BELOW the real whole-house market — the exact catastrophic wrong number the mission warns about, in the opposite direction from the one I was asked to fix. FIXED THE MEASUREMENT INSTEAD: the endpoint now classifies every comp as whole/partial from the address (MLS encodes the unit there, not in a field), returns `unitType` per comp, computes the median over whole-property leases when at least 3 exist, falls back to all with `wholeUnitOnly:false` so no caller has to guess, and labels itself "whole-property leases". The classifier is tested against 19 cases — every real address returned by production this run plus deliberate traps that must NOT match ("123 Main Street", "5100 Lower Base Line", "88 Upper Middle Road", "700 Upper Sherman Avenue", "1 Mainway Drive"); it needed a real fix mid-test, since a trailing "Upper" sits before ", Mississauga, ON ..." so anchoring to the end of the full address never matched — it now tests the street segment. 19/19 correct. WHAT THE DATA SAYS SO FAR: Malton (L4T) detached whole-house leases are $2,700-$3,000 (median $2,800, and $2,800 whether or not partial units are excluded, so the gap is NOT an artifact of the contamination) against a model of $3,300 for a 3-bed and $3,900 for a 4-bed — 18% to 39% high, consistent with the advisor's $2,800-3,500 read, the city-wide detached median, and the single L4T comp gathered previously. Cooksville by contrast models 4-bed detached at $4,100 against whole-house comps of $4,400 — 7% BELOW market, i.e. conservative. So the model is NOT systematically high; Malton specifically is. NEXT RUN (needs this fix deployed first, since the clean medians only exist once it ships): re-run all 24 hoods x beds 2/3/4 against the corrected endpoint, then recalibrate Malton — and only the hoods the clean data actually indicts — with counts attached. Build green — (this commit)

Also surfaced `cronSecretSet` in the note, which was already computed by the API two commits ago but never rendered: it is the single likeliest explanation here, since the fail-closed auth guard shipped earlier today refuses Vercel Cron's request outright when CRON_SECRET is unset, silently, with no prior surface telling Hamza the daily schedule needs that env var. CAUGHT MY OWN BUG WHILE VERIFYING: the result box classified severity by `res.ok` alone, but `/api/alerts/send` returns HTTP 200 even when every send was rejected by Resend (rejections are in the body, not the status) — so a fully-rejected run painted itself green as "Ran successfully." Fixed to classify from the payload's `failed` count: red only on a real HTTP failure, amber when Resend rejected sends with the reason shown, green only when sends actually went out. Crawled all 32 indexable routes against a production build. THE REAL FINDING was heading-level SKIPS on 20 of 32 routes, and almost all of it came from ONE place: the footer's four column headings ("Platform", "Investor Guides", "Service Areas", "Compliance") were h4 with no h3 or h2 anywhere in the footer, so EVERY page on the site ended in a skipped level — h2->h4 on 18 routes and h1->h4 on the rest. A broken outline is what screen-reader users navigate by and what crawlers read as document structure. Fixed at the source: those four are h2 now, which is the correct level (top-level sections of the contentinfo landmark, siblings of the page's own h2s) and keeps the outline flat whatever the page above ends on. Two page-level stragglers remained after that. InlineCTA's headline was an h3 and on /blog it was the FIRST heading after the h1 — now h2, since it is a genuine page section. The booking calendar's month label was an h3, making /book-call read "h1 -> July 2026"; that one is NOT a document section but a date-picker control sitting between the prev/next buttons, so promoting it to h2 would have fixed the skip by putting a month name in the page outline, which is worse — it is now a div with aria-live="polite", which also announces the month when a visitor pages between months, something the static h3 never did. RESULT, re-measured across all 32 routes: zero skipped levels, zero duplicate h1s, zero missing titles. NO VISUAL CHANGE, verified rather than assumed — Tailwind's preflight resets heading margins and sizes, so the footer headings still compute to 14px with margin-top 0 / margin-bottom 12px / width 343 at 375px, and scrollWidth == clientWidth == 375 on /, /blog and /book-call.
