- 2026-07-26 — [improvement agent] Fixed a real, previously undiscovered defect in the daily alert email: the per-search "Unsubscribe" link (`/api/alerts/unsubscribe?id=`) was a bare GET that deactivated the saved search IMMEDIATELY on fetch, with no token check at all. Corporate email security gateways (Microsoft Defender for Office 365 Safe Links, Proofpoint URL Defense, Mimecast, Barracuda) routinely prefetch every link in an incoming email to scan for phishing/malware — a well-documented antipattern, the email-unsubscribe descendant of the classic "delete via GET link" bug. That means any subscriber whose employer runs one of these (common on real-estate/corporate domains) could have their deal alerts silently killed by their own mail security software before they ever opened the message — with zero action on their part, and no signal anywhere in the product that it happened; it would just look like the alerts quietly stopped. This is a direct hit against the "great weekly emails & deal alerts" mission and, worse, a silent LEAD-RETENTION LEAK in the opposite direction of every capture-friction fix shipped this week. THE FIX, matching a pattern already half-present in the same file: GET now only ever READS (looks up the search / validates the token) and renders a confirm page; the actual mutation moved to POST, behind a real click on that page's button. This mirrors why the RFC 8058 List-Unsubscribe-Post header exists at all — mail clients only send THAT POST on an explicit user action, never via prefetch — but the header only ever covered the whole-account email+token unsubscribe (verified via grep: both send routes' List-Unsubscribe header always uses `unsubscribeUrl(email)`, never the bare `?id=` link), leaving the per-search link with no such protection. Applied the same GET-confirms/POST-mutates split to the email+token path too, since its visible footer link is the exact same class of scannable href. ALSO FIXED a secondary honesty bug found while rewriting this: an unsub link for an id that no longer exists (or was already unsubscribed) used to silently return the same "you've been unsubscribed" success page regardless — now it says plainly "link no longer valid" or "you're already unsubscribed", matching the omit/state-honestly convention used everywhere else on this site. VERIFIED against the REAL, unmodified route.js (a Node ESM loader resolving the project's `@/` alias and stubbing `next/server`/`@supabase/supabase-js`, same technique the alert-send safety harness used earlier this session) across 9 cases: GET on an active id never mutates and shows the confirm page (0 DB calls); POST on that id mutates exactly once and shows the real success page; GET on an already-inactive id and on a nonexistent id both report their true state with 0 mutating calls; the email+token path shows the same GET-confirms/POST-mutates behaviour and POST correctly deactivates both `leads` and every `saved_searches` row for that email; and — with a real `CRON_SECRET` set, matching production — a forged token is rejected with 400 on both GET and POST, with zero mutating calls. Exactly 3 mutating DB calls across the whole 9-case run, matching hand-calculated expectations precisely. Confirmed the rendered confirm page is structurally sound (balanced tags, viewport meta present) and cannot overflow at 375px: the only width rule is `max-width: 420px; width: 100%` with `box-sizing: border-box`, plus body padding I added that the original page lacked. Could not get a live browser screenshot this run — the sandbox's background dev/prod server kept getting killed mid-session (environment flakiness, not a code issue) — so this is recorded as statically verified rather than visually confirmed; worth an eyeball on production. Test harness files and its two stub shims were written outside `app/` and fully deleted before finishing; git confirms exactly one file changed (`app/api/alerts/unsubscribe/route.js`). Build green — (this commit)
- 2026-07-26 — [seo agent] Declared og:image:width/height/alt on every page whose openGraph block still lacked them. The ROOT layout has always had the complete form (`{url, width: 1200, height: 630, alt}`), but because Next REPLACES rather than merges an openGraph object across nesting levels, every page/layout that declares its own openGraph to fix its title/url (the correct, established pattern on this site) silently DROPPED width/height/alt in the process — leaving `images: ['/opengraph-image']` as a bare string on every one of them. Verified the true dimensions are safe to state before writing any of them: app/opengraph-image.js exports `size = { width: 1200, height: 630 }` and always renders that exact PNG, so 1200x630 is a fact, not a guess. Missing width/height means a social crawler (Facebook, LinkedIn, Slack) can't reserve the correct preview aspect without fetching the image first; missing alt is a completeness gap for screen readers reading link-preview cards. FOUND IN TWO PLACES my first pass missed entirely, because I only grepped `--include=page.js`: 11 `layout.js` files also declare their own openGraph, and for 9 of them (mortgage-calculator, blog index, market-pulse, pre-construction, pre-construction/projects, quiz, compare, alerts, neighbourhoods index) their sibling `page.js` is a `'use client'` component with ZERO metadata of its own — meaning the layout is the ONLY, currently-LIVE source in production, not a redundant copy. Confirmed one of these (`/faq`) was rendering exactly the predicted bug: curled the live build and got a bare `<meta property="og:image">` with no width/height/alt companion tags at all. Fixed all 11 layout.js files plus 17 page.js files (28 total call sites across 29 files). ONE DELIBERATE EXCEPTION, to avoid fabricating a number: `blog/[slug]/page.js` uses `blogCoverUrl(post, true)`, which returns `post.cover_image_url` — an arbitrary imported photo with genuinely UNKNOWN dimensions — whenever a post has one, falling back to the generated 1200x630 cover only when it doesn't. Width/height/alt are now conditional on that same check, so a post with a real cover gets an undecorated (but correct) og:image and only the generated-cover fallback gets the verified dimensions. Left `twitter.images` alone everywhere — the Twitter Card spec has no companion width/height meta tag, so there was nothing to add there. VERIFIED the actual build output, not just the source: `next build` already renders every static/SSG route to `.next/server/app/*.html`, so no live server was needed (useful this run — the sandbox's dev/prod server kept dying mid-session for reasons unrelated to this change) — checked 10 routes' rendered HTML directly, confirmed `/faq`'s previously-bare tag now carries width 1200 / height 630 / the real title as alt, confirmed `/sell` correctly shows its PAGE-level alt (proving page.js still wins over the also-fixed but superseded layout.js copy there), and confirmed a statically-generated neighbourhood page (`/neighbourhoods/clarkson`) carries its own per-hood alt text. - 2026-07-27 — [improvement agent] **Hamza re-crawled production and reported three things still broken: DOM stamped on /listings + /gta, the ~198 cap, and contradictory count claims. He was right on all three, and the root cause was ONE bug feeding the others.** Ran a 4-way parallel investigation with adversarial verification, plus direct probes of the live production API (this sandbox has no AMPRE token, but `mcp__Vercel__web_fetch_vercel_url` reaches production).
  - **ROOT CAUSE — a poisoned `$select`.** AMPRE rejects the WHOLE query with a 4xx if the `$select` names one field it does not serve. The toxic group is `{OnMarketDate, ListingContractDate, OriginalEntryTimestamp}`, isolated by differential testing rather than guesswork: `/api/price-history`'s primary `$select` is byte-identical to `/api/sold-comps`' (which returns 200 with real CloseDate/Latitude/Longitude) PLUS exactly those three fields — and price-history's primary query fails while sold-comps' succeeds. Both listing routes requested all three, so their primary query could NEVER succeed. Worse, tier 2 of the fallback reused the SAME `$select` and only dropped `$expand`, so it could never rescue a `$select` rejection either. The cascade was degenerate: every request landed on the minimal tier, which silently drops Media, LivingArea, BuildingAreaTotal and AssociationFee. That is why production served `photos:[]`, `sqft:0` and `condoFee:0` on obvious condo apartments — the site has apparently never once served a real square footage or a real condo fee from this feed.
  - **DOM was fabricated, and inverted.** With the real date fields stripped, DOM fell through to `ModificationTimestamp` — the feed's SYNC stamp. Because results are ordered by that same field, every listing on a page got one identical value tracking when we last synced: page 1 (synced that morning) all read `dom 0`; page 300 (synced Jul 15) all read `dom 11`; page 400 (synced Jun 29) all read `dom 27`. Arithmetic confirmed to the day. The failure direction is what made it dangerous: a listing sitting 200 days that had a price change this morning rendered the **"New"** badge, while one nobody had touched in 45 days rendered **"Motivated Seller"**. Fixed via new `lib/listings/market-timing.js` — DOM now comes only from a listing's own market-timing fields or the feed's `DaysOnMarket`, never the sync stamp; `0` means UNKNOWN, which every consumer already treats as no-data (New needs >=1, Motivated >=45, BRRR >=60, the deal score adds no bonus at 0), so nothing fabricates and nothing breaks. `daysSinceUpdate` exposes what `ModificationTimestamp` CAN honestly say, under its true name, and the card now shows "Updated 11d ago" instead of a bare `0` that read as "listed today". Hand-verified against the exact production rows: old code produced `[27,27,27,27]` for four listings synced within 20 minutes of each other, new code produces `[0,0,0,0]`, and a real `DaysOnMarket` or list date is picked up immediately if the feed ever starts serving one. Also stopped `market-stats.avgDOM` and `hood-stats.avgDOM` averaging unknowns into a confident figure (0 was passing their null checks), and removed a hardcoded `|| 28` fallback that published an invented number under a "Live MLS Data" label.
  - **The ~198 cap was already fixed — but invisibly.** Verified in a real headless browser against a production-shaped fixture (2,606 across 14 pages): the full set loads in ~2s and the footer reads "of 2606 properties", no 375px overflow. The catch is that the SERVER renders only the first page, so the HTML permanently says 198 and every non-JS reader — Googlebot, and any audit tool that doesn't execute and wait on the background fetch — sees a capped site. That is almost certainly why it kept getting reported as unfixed. The Deal Screener now states the real market size in the server-rendered markup ("198 of 2,547 analyzed" while streaming, settling to "2,547 analyzed"), so a partial load is never presented as the whole market and the true figure is in the HTML.
  - **The count contradiction was two renderings of ONE number.** `/api/listings` `total` is AMPRE's raw `@odata.count` taken BEFORE the commercial/lease filter (2,606); `/api/market-stats` `activeCount` pages the whole feed and counts surviving rows (2,547) — a ~59-listing gap of rows the site never shows. The hero rounded its copy to "2,500+" while the stats bar printed "2,547" exactly, which read as two contradictory facts. Now: one canonical source (`market-stats.activeCount`) for the hero, the bio, /about and /sell; rendered EXACTLY with no "+" rounding, since rounding is what made two identical counts look like a disagreement. `/api/listings` also now returns `browsableTotal` (raw count scaled by the observed exclusion rate) and both index pages use it. NOTE: /about and /sell were already showing the live 2,500+ before this run — Hamza's "1,800" reading predates the PR #49 deploy.
  - **Also added:** `fieldTier` in both listing API responses, naming which `$select` tier the feed accepted, so a field regression is diagnosable from production in a single request instead of being inferred from symptoms like `sqft:0` — which is exactly how the silent fall-through went unnoticed for so long.
  - **NEEDS HAMZA (highest-value item on the list):** the feed supplies no `DaysOnMarket`/`YearBuilt`/`Latitude`/`Longitude` on ACTIVE listings but does on Closed ones — the IDX-vs-VOW suppression signature. See the new "Needs Hamza" entry: adding `AMPRE_VOW_TOKEN` to Vercel may restore all of it with zero code changes.
  - **NOT a bug, but a decision Hamza needs to make:** he flagged that the site's own analytics report an average deal score of ~3.6/10, i.e. very little Mississauga inventory cash-flows at today's rates. That is an honest read of real data, not a defect. His own framing is the right one — either lean into it ("only N of 2,547 Mississauga listings cash flow — here they are", which is genuinely compelling and true) or keep pulling investors into a page that mostly says no. Deliberately NOT changed unilaterally: it is positioning, not code.

Real defects were in the photo backfill: /api/photos-batch ran 25 concurrent AMPRE queries per call x 4 parallel client calls = 100-query bursts against an upstream that throttles bursts, died at a Hobby-era maxDuration of 10s, and was POST-only so its s-maxage header was dead code (CDN never caches POST). - 2026-07-31 — [hotfix — post-release regression] Two defects from the 3680066 headers()→SITE_URL refactor, found by verifying the PR #77 release on production instead of trusting the build gate. (1) **`/listings` served ZERO server-rendered cards and no ItemList schema for ~50 minutes.** Turning real ISR on (the audit was right that `headers()` had been silently disabling `revalidate = 600`) made the money page worse in practice: the Vercel build cannot reach the site's own `/api/listings` while building, so the prerender baked empty, and the page then sat at CDN `cache=HIT` with **no serverless invocation at all** — runtime logs over 2h show zero page-function runs for `/listings`, so ISR never regenerated and the empty HTML was permanent. Pre-release (dynamic) the same page served cards + a valid ItemList — confirmed in the 23:49 UTC production audit. Reverted to `export const dynamic = 'force-dynamic'` with a comment explaining why, keeping the SITE_URL constant (correct, and removes the local `next start` trap). An empty highest-value commercial page is far worse than one server render per request, which is what this page did for months. (2) **Stale `${proto}://${host}` reference** survived the refactor on the market-stats fetch — `proto`/`host` no longer exist in that scope. Node-verified the real behaviour rather than assuming: the ReferenceError is caught by the INNER `catch {}` around that fetch, so it did NOT empty the page (my first hypothesis, disproved by test) — it silently defeated the unified-listing-count invariant, dropping `displayTotal` back to `feedTotal` so `/listings` could again disagree with the homepage and `/about` on how many listings exist. Fixed to `${origin}`. Also replaced the outer bare `catch {}` with one that `console.error`s — that silence is why an empty money page produced nothing in the logs, and the env-less build cannot catch this class of bug at all (the feed fetch fails first and returns early, so the faulty line never executes at build time). **Verification limits stated honestly: this sandbox cannot reach the live API (curl returns 000), so "real cards render" could NOT be verified locally — build green 121/121 and `/listings` emits no static HTML (so it can no longer serve a stale empty page), with final confirmation deferred to post-deploy on production.**
- 2026-07-31 — [ATTACKED, verify on production] **`/api/listings-gta` exceeds a 25s timeout during GTA city-page regeneration.** Runtime logs show `fetch-feed: /api/listings-gta page 1 failed (TimeoutError)` on essentially every `/gta/[city]` ISR regeneration (aurora, caledon, burlington, toronto, brampton observed), so those 28 pages currently serve their hero/FAQ/schema but no server-rendered listings — the client fetch fills them for real visitors. This is still strictly better than before the release (they were hard 404s), so it was deliberately NOT hotfixed at 01:40 UTC: making 28 pages fully dynamic against an API that takes >25s would risk a timeout on every request, which is worse. `/api/listings` by contrast completes fine (homepage regenerations collect ~2,200 rows), so the problem is specific to the much larger GTA query. Next run: profile `/api/listings-gta`, and consider serving city pages from a cached slice of the Mississauga-wide feed rather than a per-city upstream query.
- 2026-07-31 — [correction to the hotfix entry above — MEASUREMENT ERROR, recorded so nobody trusts the wrong version] The 9814eec hotfix commit and PR #78 both claim ISR "never regenerated" and the empty `/listings` HTML "was permanent, 50 minutes after deploy still 0 cards". **That claim is false and was my error.** The regex used to count cards (`href="(/listings/[A-Z0-9]+)"`) does not match the RSC Flight payload, where hrefs are escaped (`\"href\":\"/listings/W…\"`), so it reported 0 on pages that genuinely had listings. Re-measured the same saved captures with `/listings/([A-Z]\d{6,})`: **00:51 post-deploy = 0 listings (genuinely empty, the build baked empty — that part was real); 01:29 = 20 listings + ItemList (ISR HAD healed it); 01:48 post-hotfix = 30 listings + ItemList.** Lesson: a zero from a hand-written regex against an RSC payload is a claim about the regex, not the page — verify a negative with a second, broader pattern before building a diagnosis on it, especially before shipping a hotfix. What survives scrutiny: the build genuinely bakes `/listings` empty (no API reachable at build time), so every deploy leaves the money page contentless for crawlers for tens of minutes; the ISR-healed render was also thinner than the dynamic one (2 vs 33 cap-rate figures for 20 vs 30 listings). force-dynamic remains the right call for this page on those grounds, but the rationale in the code comment has been corrected to say so honestly rather than asserting ISR never ran. Production verified healthy at 01:48: 30 listing IDs, ItemList present, no runtime errors, no timeouts from the dynamic switch.
- 2026-07-31 — [improvement agent] — Two changes aimed at the open `/api/listings-gta` 25s-timeout item, both on the request path rather than on the page's caching mode (deliberately did NOT make the 28 city pages dynamic — a >25s API behind every request is worse than one slow regeneration per 5 minutes). (1) **The GTA SSR fetch was asking for 100 rows and rendering 30.** `fetchGtaListings` used `fetchFeedPages`' default page size and then passed the result straight to `slimForSSR`, which slices to 30 — so 70 of every 100 rows, *and the Media `$expand` rows that come with them*, were fetched and discarded on the one call whose logs show `fetch-feed: /api/listings-gta page 1 failed (TimeoutError)`. Media is the dominant cost of a feed request (~5 CDN size-variants per photo; the route's own comment measures a 200-row page at ~36k media rows and AMPRE rejecting it) and it scales with `$top`, so 100→30 rows takes the GTA page-1 payload from ~18k media rows to ~5.4k. `fetchFeedPages` gained a `limit` option (default stays 100, the feed's clamp and the known-clean cache-key size) and a shared `SSR_CARD_ROWS = 30` that `slimForSSR` now defaults to, so the fetched count and the rendered count cannot drift. Nothing rendered changes — 30 cards either way — and `total`/`browsableTotal` come from the upstream `$count`, which is independent of `$top`. Only the GTA path changed; `/listings`, the homepage, market-stats, neighbourhood-stats, both sitemaps still request 100. (2) **WRONG-NUMBER bug found while profiling, unrelated to the timeout but on the same code path:** `probeSupportedFields` treated a dropped connection exactly like a 4xx — both meant "field unsupported" — and cached the verdict for an hour. So a single transient blip while probing `AssociationFee` stripped condo fees from every listing that instance served for the next hour, and a condo with no fee has its cash flow, cap rate and Deal Score all overstated by the fee (the site's most common property type). Confirmed against the pre-change module with a stubbed fetch: one `ECONNRESET` on that one probe → `AssociationFee` dropped. Now answered-vs-unanswered are distinguished: a 4xx still drops the field (verified: a genuinely revoked field is still dropped), an error keeps the last known-good set and expires in 60s instead of an hour (verified: field kept through a blip, and a total upstream outage no longer collapses the query to core-only). Also added a 5s `AbortSignal.timeout` to each probe request — there was none, so one hung `$top=1` connection blocked the whole route via `Promise.all` until the platform killed it, which is *precisely* the shape of "caller sees TimeoutError, API logs nothing". Verification limits stated plainly: this sandbox cannot reach AMPRE, so the latency win is reasoned from the route's own measured media-row figures, NOT measured — whether city pages now server-render listings must be checked in production runtime logs (look for the absence of `fetch-feed: /api/listings-gta page 1 failed`) and by grepping a city page for `/listings/([A-Z]\d{6,})`, never the unescaped-href pattern. The probe fix was verified locally with real stubs. Build green (121 pages), 3 files — 709c65f

- 2026-07-31 — [VOW TOKEN RESOLVED — partial win, and the old theory was half wrong] Hamza set fresh PropTx tokens (`AMPRE_TOKEN` = IDX, `AMPRE_VOW_TOKEN` = VOW) and redeployed. **Feed restored and days-on-market is now REAL.** Measured on live `/api/listings` at 05:29 UTC: HTTP 200, `fieldTier: probed+media`, `dom`/`daysOnMarket` populated with genuinely distinct values (11 and 1) where every active listing previously returned null — so the DOM sort, the Motivated-Seller and BRRR filters and the "New" badge are all live on real data for the first time. `condoFee` returns a real $504 on the condo and 0 on the detached (correct), `neighbourhood` resolves to Applewood/Hurontario rather than the city fallback (CityRegion is flowing), and `sqft` parses from LivingAreaRange (1300/950, approximate by nature). **BUT `lat`, `lng` and `yearBuilt` are still absent** — the keys do not appear in the JSON at all, which in JS means the mapped value was `undefined`, i.e. AMPRE omitted them. They ARE named in SELECT_CORE and the query returned 200, so this is not a rejected `$select`: the VOW feed simply does not serve them. **This disproves the long-standing "it's all IDX-vs-VOW suppression" theory for those three fields** — VOW fixed DaysOnMarket but NOT coordinates or year built, so map pins remain deterministic FSA-centroid offsets and there is no year-built data. Anyone revisiting map accuracy should start from "the feed does not provide coordinates" rather than "we need a better token". Also learned the hard way: revealing/regenerating a token in the PropTx portal REVOKES the one in production immediately — the site's listings were down ~40 minutes (04:50–05:29) because of that, with the twist that `AMPRE_VOW_TOKEN` had existed since Jul 26 holding a stale value, so the code's VOW-first preference meant it kept presenting the dead token even after a fresh IDX token was available. Next token rotation: put the replacement in Vercel and redeploy BEFORE revoking the old one.
- 2026-07-31 — [improvement agent] — **Audited every code path that reads days-on-market now that real DOM is finally flowing, and found four live wrong-number bugs of the same shape: `0` means UNKNOWN, and four consumers read it as the number zero.** They were all harmless while EVERY active listing was 0 (the sort was a no-op, the filter matched everything uniformly); they became wrong the instant real values started sitting next to the unknowns. Measured before/after on a fixture built from the real live sample (dom 11, dom 1, an unknown, and an unknown-with-a-62-day first-seen floor). (1) **WORST — Hamza's Take fabricated freshness in a named human's voice.** `lib/hamza-take.js` opened with a bare `dom < 14` test, so every listing of unknown age produced *"Fresh listing — this 3-bed detached in Cooksville just hit the market **0 days ago**. Move fast if you're interested."* — rendered under Hamza's headshot in the "Hamza's Take" tab on the listing detail page. Verified against the pre-change module: the fixture listing we can PROVE has been on market 62+ days got exactly that sentence, i.e. the commentary asserted the inverse of the truth and told the reader to hurry. Now gated on `dom >= 1` (unknown falls through to the neutral opener), narrowed to 1–7 days so it matches the site's own "New" badge instead of calling a 13-day-old listing "just hit the market", and the "1 **days** ago" grammar bug is fixed. (2) **"DOM (Newest First)" ranked unknown-age listings as the freshest inventory on the page** — ascending on a 0-means-unknown value put every no-data listing at the top of the exact sort an investor uses to find what just came to market (verified: old order `C(unknown),B,A,D,E`; new `B,A,D,E,C`). (3) **The DOM range filter used raw `dom`, so unknowns passed any range starting at 0**: the 62-day fixture listing was returned by a "0–30 days" search while its own card printed "62+ DOM", and was *excluded* from "60–365" despite a provable floor. Bounds now take different evidence, which is the actual logic: an upper bound ("under 30 days") requires the exact feed DOM; a min-only range is satisfied by the provable floor. (4) **`/api/seo/ping` submitted the first 100 rows of the feed to IndexNow as "new" on every run** (`dom <= 3` accepted 0), repeatedly re-pinging unchanged URLs — precisely what IndexNow asks publishers not to do. Also aligned two claims that disagreed with each other: the Motivated badge on the card and detail page tested raw `dom >= 45` while the MOTIVATED chip tests the floor, so a listing could be IN the motivated result set, print "60+" in its own DOM cell, and show no badge; and the BRRR chip's tooltip said "below assessed value with renovation potential" while the filter has only ever tested 60+ days and a 5%+ cut — it now states the real rule. The general principle is now written into the code: a lower bound (`domFloor`) can support an "at LEAST N days" claim and never an "at most N" one, so New/upper-bound tests keep using exact DOM. **NOT changed, deliberately, and worth a live check by whoever is next in the feed:** `computeDaysOnMarket` still discards a raw `DaysOnMarket` of exactly 0, so a listing entered TODAY may be classed unknown and miss the New badge and the alert freshness gate. Fixing it means treating a numeric 0 as day-one — but `market-timing.js`'s own recorded measurement says a listing that had already taken a price cut read 0, so a real 0 may still mean "withheld" rather than "listed today". That needs one observation of raw `DaysOnMarket` on a known-brand-new active listing to settle; guessing would fabricate a New badge on old inventory, which is the exact sin that module exists to prevent. Verification limits stated plainly: this sandbox cannot reach AMPRE, so nothing here was re-measured against the live feed — the fixture uses the real values recorded in the entry above, and the four before/after comparisons were run against the actual pre-change modules. The API was healthy, just slow on a cold cache (many `cache=MISS` entries); the homepage's own 5s budget is what gave up. Earlier the same day the same page logged `3/25 extra pages failed ... 2198 rows collected`, i.e. page 1 landing is the whole difference between a full deals section and an empty one. **The asymmetry the old code missed: page 1 decides whether we get ANY data (its loss returns `{listings: [], first: null}` and the section renders empty), while extra pages only affect COMPLETENESS (a 22-of-25 fill still ranks four deals fine) — yet `timeoutMs` was applied identically to both.** Fix: `fetchFeedPages` takes a new `firstPageTimeoutMs` option, used for page 1 only; the batched extra pages keep the tight `timeoutMs` so the walk stays bounded. It **defaults to `timeoutMs`, so behaviour is byte-identical for every caller that does not opt in** — deliberate, because `/listings` is force-dynamic (a longer page-1 wait would sit on every visitor's TTFB) and the sitemaps carry a documented build-timeout scar. Only the homepage opts in: `firstPageTimeoutMs: 15000` with `timeoutMs: 5000` unchanged. New worst case, still inside the 60s build-prerender budget that #68 taught us not to blow: 15s (page 1) + 5 batches x 5s (25 extra pages at BATCH=6) + 5s (top-4 photo fetches, parallel) = **45s**, with `fetchLiveStats` (8s) running in parallel rather than after. Verified: `npm run build` green with no env vars, plus five stubbed-fetch unit checks proving page 1 receives 15000ms and all 25 extras receive 5000ms; that omitting the option leaves every page on `timeoutMs`; that a page-1 timeout still degrades to `{listings: [], first: null}` instead of throwing; a real-abort timing test where a hang longer than the short budget now survives on page 1 and still drops the extras; and a regression witness showing the OLD single-budget call returns nothing against that same hang. **NOT verifiable from the sandbox: the actual production latency.** This environment cannot reach the live AMPRE feed or the production domain, so whether 15s is enough for a genuinely cold `/api/listings` page 1 can only be confirmed from production logs — watch for `fetch-feed: /api/listings page 1 failed (TimeoutError) after 15000ms` (the log line now prints the budget it spent). If that line appears, the answer is not simply a bigger number: the build budget is the ceiling, so the next move would be shrinking the walk (the homepage only needs `first.total` plus enough rows to rank four deals) rather than waiting longer. — 32ba4f8

The page hitting the LARGER feed is the one that works**, so feed size is not the variable — rows-per-request is. `/gta` asks for `limit: SSR_CARD_ROWS` (30); `/listings` asked for 100 and the homepage walked ~25 pages of 100. Runtime logs name it exactly: `fetch-feed: /api/listings page 1 failed (TimeoutError) after 8000ms` (that is `/listings`) and `after 15000ms` (the homepage), 29 occurrences, routes `/`, `/listings`, `/sitemap.xml`, `/api/market-stats`, `/api/neighbourhood-stats`. The Media `$expand` is the dominant cost of a feed request (~5 CDN size-variants per photo, ~18k media rows on a 100-row page) and it scales linearly with `$top`. **Not a regression from today's release** — the error group first appeared 2026-07-31, and the 32ba4f8 budget raise treated the symptom (wait longer) rather than the payload. Fix, in two shapes: (1) `/listings` SSR now asks for `SSR_CARD_ROWS` — the configuration already proven live by `/gta` — chosen over dropping media so the cards keep their server-rendered LCP image; (2) every consumer that AGGREGATES the feed and never renders a photo now passes `nomedia=1`: the homepage top-deals walk (`HomeDealCards` reads only the per-id `photoMap`, verified in the component), `app/sitemap.js` (both branches — reads `ListingKey` + `modificationTimestamp` only), `/api/market-stats` and `/api/neighbourhood-stats` (grepped all four: zero photo/image references). `/api/listings` gained the `nomedia` switch `/api/listings-gta` already had — `fetchWithFieldTiers(..., { media })`, same `$select`, so rows are byte-identical apart from the photo arrays; it defaults to media ON, so no existing caller changes. `app/image-sitemap.xml` is the one aggregate that genuinely needs photos and was deliberately left alone. **One trap closed on the way: `initialPages` is consumed by `ListingsContainer` to background-load the rest of the feed AT limit=100, and it was `data.pages` — correct only because the SSR fetch also asked for 100.** At 30 rows that becomes ceil(total/30) ≈ 84, and the container would have walked 84 pages of 100, 59 of them past the end of the feed. Recomputed at the container's own page size using the API's own formula (`Math.ceil(total / limit)`), from the RAW `@odata.count` rather than `browsableTotal` — deriving it from the post-filter count is what once computed 13 where the truth was 14 and made page 14's listings permanently unreachable. - 2026-08-01 — [campaign] — **Scrapped the Offer Gap; built "This Week's Picks" instead.** Hamza: *"stop fuckin making same old emails we sent the same one 2x before."* He was right and the proof is structural — the two campaigns already sent and the one I'd just built share one skeleton: navy gradient masthead + gold all-caps kicker → big-number hook → bordered stats table → navy "Book a free call" box → headshot signoff. I'd changed the dataset and told myself that made it a new email; to anyone who got the radar it lands looking identical. **Deleted the Offer Gap entirely** (route, template, data module, tests, admin panel) rather than parking it — it was never sent, the format was rejected, and leaving a one-click send button for a rejected email is a hazard, not an asset. **Also found the likely literal cause of "sent the same one 2x":** `broadcast_sends`' own migration comment says the routes "degrade gracefully if this table is absent (it just skips the guard), so running this migration is what actually turns the guard on" — if it was never applied in Supabase, the idempotency guard AND the self-disarm are both no-ops, so the daily motivated-sellers cron mails an `[APPROVE]` draft every day forever and approving two of them sends the identical email twice. Removed that cron from `vercel.json` (its own code comment already said to, once sent). **Needs Hamza:** confirm `broadcast_sends` exists in Supabase; if not, run `supabase/migrations/create_broadcast_sends.sql` — until then no campaign has duplicate protection. New format is a genuinely different artifact, not a reskin: no masthead, no kicker, photo-led cards, three *specific* addresses with the price to open at, and the primary CTA is **replying**, not clicking. The offer is anchored to what that property type actually sold for last month (TRREB sale-to-list), adjusted only by evidence the listing itself carries — sat longer than the type's typical sale, and/or already cut — with both adjustments capped at 4 points below the market ratio and an absolute 88%-of-ask floor, because past that you don't get a discount, you get ignored. Each card prints its own reasoning inline, assembled only from the leverage that actually applied (`usedDom`/`usedCut` come from the arithmetic, so the copy cannot claim a reason the maths didn't use). Freehold vs condo townhouse matters (101% vs 98% last month) and `mapType` collapses them, so the condo fee disambiguates, falling back to the *freehold* anchor — the higher one, which understates buyer leverage, because erring that way costs a few thousand in room while erring the other way tells someone to lowball a property that sells over ask. Offers on types selling at/over ask are capped at 100% and then dropped from the picks entirely. Duplex/triplex/multiplex have no TRREB category and are excluded rather than anchored to a ratio that doesn't describe them. Copy states plainly these are opening positions, not appraisals, and the CASL footer says so again. `node lib/emails/offer-picks-data.test.mjs` — **39 checks, all passing**. Same safety harness (draft→HMAC approval→send, `broadcast_sends` guard, one-click unsubscribe) plus a stricter refusal: **no sample-data fallback on preview**, because inventing a plausible property for an email that tells people what to bid is not acceptable; the dev-only `?sample=1` fixture stamps every address "SAMPLE (not a real listing)". Draft banner lists all three addresses and offer numbers for checking before send. Verified at 375px, 0px overflow; build green (121/121). Not on a cron by design. — (this commit)
- 2026-08-01 — [bug/broadcast] — **Approve button 404'd: draft links were hardcoded to production.** Hamza triggered the Picks draft on the branch preview, loved the email, clicked "Review & Send" and got the site's 404 page. Cause was mine and it was in every broadcast route: the approval URL was built from a constant that resolved to `https://www.mississaugainvestor.ca` for anything where `NODE_ENV !== 'development'` — and on a Vercel **preview** deployment NODE_ENV *is* "production". So a draft raised on a preview linked to the live domain, where the route doesn't exist yet. The campaign was sitting healthy on the preview the draft came from. Fixed by deriving the origin from the request actually being served (`lib/emails/self-origin.js`) and threading it through all three routes — the approve link *and* each route's own internal `/api/listings` + `/api/market-stats` fetches, so a preview reads the preview's data rather than production's. **The host is validated, not trusted:** `Host`/`X-Forwarded-Host` are attacker-controllable and this value lands in an emailed link that authorises a send, so only `*.mississaugainvestor.ca` and `*.vercel.app` are honoured and everything else falls back to the canonical origin. Comma-chained forwarded headers take the first entry, and a chain *starting* with a bad host does not pass. `node lib/emails/self-origin.test.mjs` — **19 checks, all passing**, including 8 poisoning attempts (`mississaugainvestor.ca.evil.com`, `evil.com/mississaugainvestor.ca`, `notvercel.app.evil.com`, bare IPs) that must all fall back rather than resolve to the attacker. Applied to all three broadcast routes, not just the one that broke — announcement had the production URL literally inline, motivated-sellers had the same constant. Build green (121/121); offer-picks 39/39 still passing. — (this commit)
- 2026-08-01 — [deliverability/bug] — **The "just reply" CTA didn't reach Hamza, and every broadcast was HTML-only.** Checked the actual Resend payload before answering a deliverability question, and found two things. **(1) No `reply_to` on ANY campaign.** The Picks email's primary CTA is *"just reply to this email — it comes straight to me"*; without `reply_to` that was false — replies landed on the `notifications@mississaugainvestor.ca` from-address, not Hamza's inbox. For a campaign whose entire conversion mechanism is the reply, that's the difference between leads and silence. Added `reply_to` (env `REPLY_TO_EMAIL` → `LEAD_NOTIFICATION_EMAIL` → `hamza@nouman.ca`) to all three broadcast routes. **(2) No plain-text part.** Every campaign sent HTML-only, which is a long-standing spam signal — filters expect `multipart/alternative`. Wrote a proper `buildOfferPicksText()` rather than tag-stripping (a stripped table-layout email reads like garbage): full property blocks with address, ask, DOM, the opening offer and its reasoning, the methodology note, the CASL block and the unsubscribe URL. Wired into both the draft and the fan-out, and exposed at `?preview=1&text=1` so the half of the message spam filters actually read can be reviewed before sending. Build green (121/121). **Still needs Hamza (DNS-level, dominant factor and not fixable in code):** confirm SPF, DKIM and DMARC are verified for the sending domain in Resend — those three plus the now-present List-Unsubscribe headers are what keep a broadcast out of spam. — (this commit)
- 2026-08-01 — [safety/automation] — **Fail-closed broadcast guard + full send pipeline set up; Gmail evidence confirmed the table is missing.** Hamza: "set the whole thing up perfectly for me so I don't do anything." First, evidence via his connected Gmail: the radar `[APPROVE]` draft arrived **daily Jul 27–Aug 1 at 17:15 after that campaign had already sent**, and the announcement (sent Jul 23) **re-drafted twice today** — both routes probe `broadcast_sends` before drafting, so the table provably doesn't exist and NO campaign has ever had duplicate protection. Those re-drafts carried live approve buttons; clicking one is almost certainly how the same email reached the whole 483-contact list twice. Also confirmed: nothing from notifications@ is landing in his Gmail spam (17 threads, zero SPAM labels), and Resend can only send from the domain at all because SPF/DKIM are verified — so deliverability basics are in place. **The fix inverts the failure mode:** new `lib/emails/broadcast-guard.js` (dependency-free, 15 tests) makes every mass-send FAIL-CLOSED — the send proceeds ONLY when the `broadcast_sends` lock row was actually written; missing table, RLS error, schema-cache miss and client throw all BLOCK with a refusal page containing the exact 6-line migration SQL to paste into Supabase. Wired into all three campaigns. The old fail-open block ("proceed without the idempotency guard") is dead. Offer-picks additionally probes at draft time and, when the guard is missing, renders a red warning **with the SQL inline in the draft email itself**, so Hamza fixes it before clicking rather than discovering at the refusal. Added a daily cron (13:30 UTC = 9:30am ET) that mails him a fresh picks draft until the campaign sends, after which the lock row makes it a silent no-op — the fail-closed guard is what makes a cron safe where it wasn't before (updated the route comment that previously forbade this). `REPLY_TO_EMAIL` documented in .env.example. Build 121/121; tests 15+39+19 all green. Merging PR #89 to production so the draft already in his inbox (22:05Z, real picks, 483 contacts) gets a working approve button. — (this commit)
- 2026-08-01 — [feature/leads] — **Campaign analytics: Resend webhooks → Supabase → an admin follow-up list.** Hamza asked how to see received/opened "like BombBomb". Checked first: Resend's own dashboard already shows delivered/bounced/complained, but **open and click tracking are domain-level toggles that were never switched on, and they are not retroactive** — so that had to be flagged as time-sensitive (flip them before the send or that campaign has no open data, ever). The site already had per-person *click* attribution via `tagRecipient`'s `mi` token → page_views → leads; what was missing was delivery, bounces and opens. Built the webhook layer: `lib/emails/resend-webhook.js` (dependency-free, **37 tests**) doing Svix signature verification and event rollup; `app/api/webhooks/resend/route.js`; `email_events` table with `event_id` (the svix-id) UNIQUE because **Svix retries any non-2xx and a retried "opened" would inflate the numbers Hamza makes follow-up calls from**; `app/api/admin/email-analytics/route.js`; and a Campaign Performance panel in the admin. **Security, since the endpoint is necessarily public:** the signature is the only thing between the internet and forged analytics, so verification runs on the RAW body before any parse or write and is fail-closed — no secret configured rejects rather than trusts. Covered: forged/absent signature, tampered body, tampered svix-id, tampered timestamp, signature from a different secret, non-v1 scheme, replay outside the ±5-min tolerance, and a parsed-object-instead-of-raw-string mistake that would silently break verification. Multiple `v1,` entries are accepted (secret rotation). **Honesty in the metrics, deliberately:** rates are computed over DELIVERED not sent (quoting an open rate over "sent" counts bounces as people who could have opened); unique people lead with total events beside them so one repeat-opener can't look like engagement; and opens are labelled inflated in the UI *and* in the API payload, because Apple Mail Privacy Protection pre-fetches images and registers opens nobody performed — clicks lead instead. Campaign attribution comes from a `campaign` **tag** added to all three broadcast sends, not the subject line, since subjects are generated from live listing data and change per edition (verified all three campaign keys are Resend-legal: ASCII/dash only). The panel's real payload is the follow-up list — clickers first, with name/phone joined from `leads` and tap-to-call. Verified: build 121/121; unsigned and forged POSTs both 401; analytics API 401s without the admin key; /admin renders 200. **Needs Hamza:** run `create_email_events.sql`, add the webhook in Resend, set `RESEND_WEBHOOK_SECRET` — the panel shows those exact steps inline until they're done. — (this commit)
- 2026-08-02 — [campaign] — **Fourth campaign, third distinct style: the Multi-Unit Letter** (`multi-unit-2026-08`). Hamza asked for "a different style of email maybe about duplex and triplexes". Two constraints shaped it: **multi-unit has no TRREB category** (which is exactly why offer-picks excludes those types — no sale-to-list anchor exists, so no offer numbers), and **the site's rent model is calibrated on houses/condos**, so quoting it for a 3-unit building would be fabrication — no rent figures either. The letter says both out loud; the honesty is the pitch, and it explains why this email has no green boxes. What it DOES claim is all computed from live MLS: how many duplex/triplex/fourplex/multiplexes are on the market (scarcity IS the story — a handful out of ~2,500 active), price range, median DOM (0-unknowns excluded), and how many have already cut. Plus three CMHC financing facts stated as regulatory rules with the lender-varies hedge (5% owner-occ 1–2 units first tier, 10% owner-occ 3–4 units, 20% investment; rental offset qualitative only). **Style is a typed letter** — white background, no masthead, no images, no cards, no buttons; one underlined link; CTA is replying with the word "multi". Looks written in Gmail on a Tuesday night, which for a solo agent is the format that gets replies — and it's maximally distinct from the navy-template campaigns AND the photo-card picks. Feed side: added a narrow `multiUnit=1` param to `/api/listings` — a **hardcoded** PropertySubType eq-list (no caller input reaches the OData string, nothing to escape, per the injection lessons) + `nomedia=1` since a letter needs no photos. The email layer re-verifies mapped types on the rows it gets back, and validation REFUSES on 0–1 rows — which is also the tripwire if TREB ever renames the subtypes (better a refused send than a semi labelled as a duplex). Other refusals: >60 rows (over-matching filter), out-of-band prices, DOM known for under half the rows, missing/implausible totalActive. `node lib/emails/multi-unit-data.test.mjs` — **22 checks passing** (all five suites: 22+15+39+19+37 = 132 green). Full harness: draft→HMAC approve→fail-closed lock, reply_to, plain-text part, campaign tag for the analytics webhook, guard-missing warning in the draft banner, NOT on a cron, admin panel added. Verified: sample preview renders, text part clean, 375px screenshot 0px overflow, build 121/121. — (this commit)
- 2026-08-02 — [bugfix] — **Multi-unit draft refused on production: my own totalActive bug, plus two visibility gaps.** Hamza clicked the new panel and got "Snapshot unavailable or failed validation". Root cause read straight from the code: `fetchSnapshot` took `browsableTotal` from the **multiUnit-filtered** query, whose total IS the multi-unit count — so `totalActive ≈ count` and the validation rule "city-wide total must exceed the multi-unit count" refused **every** draft, deterministically. The guard caught my bug; the panel then hid the reason (generic "check your admin key/Resend" advice for what was actually a validation refusal). Fixed three things: (1) the city-wide total now comes from an **unfiltered** probe, always; (2) if the server-side subtype eq-list yields <2 rows, the route now **walks the whole city feed** (nomedia, the homepage's own pattern, capped 30 pages) and trusts the mapped `type` — so a low count becomes a market fact only after the walk, never an artifact of unverified TREB subtype spelling; (3) refusal reasons are now `console.warn`ed (visible in Vercel logs) AND the BroadcastPanel error box shows the `detail` string instead of generic config advice — that second gap cost a debugging round-trip through Hamza's screenshots and shouldn't recur for any campaign. Build 121/121, 22/22 tests. — (this commit)
- 2026-08-02 — [tooling/admin] — **Guided analytics setup panel; automated the one step that has an API.** Hamza: "set up the analytics steps for me." Checked what was actually reachable first: no Supabase credentials in this environment (and DDL can't run through the anon/service client the app holds anyway), no Resend dashboard, and the Vercel MCP exposes no environment-variable tool — so three of four steps genuinely need his hands and saying otherwise would be a lie. Built the next best thing: `/api/admin/analytics-setup` + a **🔧 Finish analytics setup** panel that (1) **live-checks the real status** of all four steps — `email_events` exists?, `RESEND_WEBHOOK_SECRET` set?, domain tracking flags?, `broadcast_sends` exists? — rather than asking him to remember what he did; (2) puts the SQL and the webhook URL **one tap from the clipboard**, which matters because he does all of this on a phone; and (3) **fully automates the tracking toggles** by PATCHing the Resend domain with the API key production already holds, then **re-reads the domain to verify** instead of trusting the write. Two honesty details: an unknown tracking flag (null) is never reported as "on" — a false green would send him to fix something already correct, or worse, let him send believing tracking was live; and the real Resend error body is echoed on failure, so if the API's field names ever differ from what I coded, the actual message is what makes it fixable rather than a generic "failed". The tracking flip is a deliberate button press, not something the status check does on load, because it changes settings on his Resend account. Panel hides itself once all four are green. Verified: build 121/121, GET and POST both 401 without the admin key, /admin renders 200. — (this commit)
- 2026-08-02 — [improvement agent] — **Surfaced the live deal-alert duplicate-send defect in the admin instead of leaving it in Vercel logs.** Assessed the backlog against the high bar: the CITY_COPY meta descriptions belong to the daily SEO agent; `/api/gta-screener` convergence needs production observation this sandbox cannot do; the AMPRE_VOW_TOKEN and backlinks are Hamza-only; the /mortgage-calculator maintenance floor is explicitly flagged as needing a product call, not an agent's. The one item that was both a real live defect and actionable from here was `alert_sent_listings` missing in Supabase (open since 2026-07-26). Re-verified it in code rather than trusting the note: `app/api/alerts/send/route.js` reads the table for a 7-day lookback and, on error, logs and continues with an EMPTY already-sent set — so every match is treated as never-sent. Combined with the DOM<=3 "new listing" window (quantified in the migration's own header) the same property can headline a subscriber's alert **up to four days running**, which is precisely the spam mission #3 forbids. I cannot run the migration (no Supabase credentials, and DDL doesn't go through the client key the app holds), so this run attacks the reason it has sat unfixed for a week: it was only ever visible in Vercel runtime logs. It is now the **first** row of the admin email-setup panel, styled red with a **LIVE ISSUE** badge to separate it from the merely-pending analytics steps, with one-tap Copy SQL, and **undismissable while missing** — a pending feature can be hidden, an actively-spamming alert pipeline cannot. The served SQL is a deliberately re-runnable variant: the repo migration ends with a bare `CREATE POLICY` and Postgres has no `CREATE POLICY IF NOT EXISTS`, so pasting the original twice errors — exactly the class of mobile SQL-editor failure that cost two rounds earlier today. Verified the served columns (`email`, `listing_id`, `sent_at`) match what the alerts route actually queries. Build green 121/121; setup API still 401s without the admin key on both GET and POST; /admin renders 200. Not verified here: the panel's live status against a real Supabase — this sandbox has no credentials, so the row-state rendering is unproven until Hamza loads /admin. — (this commit)
- 2026-08-02 — [seo agent] — **Swept the h1-terms-absent-from-body class sitewide; fixed the one indexable page still failing it (/faq).** Verified first that the 2026-07-30 fixes held: no unmarked parameterized internal links anywhere in app/ or components/, `poweredByHeader:false` intact. Then extended the audit to pages Seobility never crawled, with these results recorded so tomorrow's run doesn't repeat them — **all clean:** (a) **openGraph/twitter parity** — exactly one file (`sell/layout.js`) declares openGraph without a `twitter` key, and its own comment notes it is superseded by `sell/page.js`, which has one; no page emits a stale homepage twitter card. (b) **Footer anchors** (sitewide, so highest leverage) — 24 links, zero duplicate anchor texts across destinations, zero hrefs with conflicting texts, none over 80 chars. (c) **Broken internal links** — extracted all 39 distinct internal targets from source and resolved each against the real route tree (43 static + 4 dynamic patterns): **zero** unresolvable. (d) **Orphan pages** — only /admin/* and /profile are unlinked, which is correct; no public page is stranded. (e) **noindex on user/auth pages** — I hypothesised /login and /signup were indexable (login has no layout.js; signup's layout sets only `title`), then **checked the built HTML instead of trusting the hypothesis and was wrong**: the shared `(auth)/layout.js` carries `robots:{index:false,follow:false}` and Next merges metadata field-by-field, so all five of /login /signup /profile /saved /recent emit `noindex, nofollow`. Nothing to fix — logged so nobody 'fixes' a working page later. **The one real finding:** `/faq`'s h1 is "Frequently Asked Questions" and neither *frequently* nor *asked* appeared anywhere in its body copy — the same relevance gap the July audit flagged elsewhere, on a page it never saw. Its subtitle was a generic "Everything you need to know about investing in Mississauga real estate". Rewrote it to carry both terms naturally **and** to name what the page actually covers (deal scores & analysis, working with Hamza, investment strategy — its real category headings), ending with a direct 'send it to me' ask. Better on-page relevance and a lead prompt on a page that had none. Verified in the built HTML, not the source: body now contains both terms, h1 still single, FAQPage JSON-LD and canonical intact; the sitewide h1 sweep went 9 issues → 8, and all 8 remaining are /admin/* (robots-disallowed) or /login /signup /saved (noindex) — i.e. **zero indexable pages now fail this check**. Rendered /faq from `npm run start` at 375px: 0px horizontal overflow, 1 h1. **Honest note on a red herring:** a mid-run `npm run build` failed with `PageNotFoundError: Cannot find module for page: /_error`. That was NOT a branch defect — I had left four `next dev` servers running from the audit, writing into `.next` during the build. Killed them, `rm -rf .next`, clean rebuild passed 121/121. The branch was healthy throughout; no build fix was needed or claimed. — (this commit)
- 2026-08-02 — [bugfix] — **Tracking status read the wrong Resend endpoint, so a successful change reported "unknown".** Hamza completed the setup (screenshot: 4/5 green — deal-alert repeat guard **active**, so the 2026-07-26 duplicate-alert defect is now closed; analytics table live with 11 events stored; webhook secret set; duplicate-send guard active). The one red row: "Resend accepted the change but reports opens=null, clicks=null." That was my bug, not Resend's — `resendDomain()` read the tracking flags off `GET /domains`, which is a **summary** response (id/name/status) and does not carry them, so the PATCH genuinely succeeded while the verify step saw null. The fail-closed design behaved correctly (it refused to claim ON without confirmation) but pointed at the wrong culprit. Now fetches the per-domain **detail** endpoint `GET /domains/{id}` and prefers those flags, falling back to the list values if the detail call fails, and still reporting "unknown" rather than guessing if both come back empty. The unknown-state copy is now actionable too — it says the setting may already be ON and to confirm in Resend → Domains, instead of dead-ending. Build green 121/121. Not verifiable from this sandbox: the live Resend response shape — the sandbox has no RESEND_API_KEY and cannot reach api.resend.com, so whether the detail endpoint returns the flags is confirmed only when Hamza taps Re-check. — (this commit)
